EXACT SPEC\n\n### 2.1 Storage layout\n\nUnder `~/.sigil/spine/` (config.py gains `SEGMENTS_DIR`, `MANIFEST_PATH`, `LOCKFILE_PATH`, `ARCHIVE_DIR`; config.py itself does no I/O):\n\n```\n~/.sigil/spine/\n  spine.jsonl        # ABSENT after migration. store.path still NAMES it — pure, stable\n                     #   lock/identity token, NEVER opened for data. The\n                     #   inode-stable flock target (invariant 14 / D3).\n  head.json          # UNCHANGED single signed head (checkpoint.py). Not touched by rotation.\n  keys/              # UNCHANGED owner keypair.\n  segments/\n    seg-00000000.jsonl.gz   # sealed, immutable, gzipped (cold)\n    seg-00000005.jsonl      # sealed, immutable, uncompressed (warm window: newest ~2)\n    seg-00000006.jsonl      # ACTIVE (highest id, always uncompressed) — append target\n  trash/             # superseded plaintext awaiting reader-grace reaper (D2)\n  archive/           # OPTIONAL cold-move target (default-OFF; §2.7)\n```\n\n**Migration is O(1), no 93 MB copy:** `os.rename(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` (atomic, same filesystem) then write a single-segment manifest naming `seg-00000000` as the active segment (id 0, first_seq 0, first_prev_hash=`_GENESIS_PREV`). Back-compat: if `manifest.json` is absent, the store treats a bare `spine.jsonl` as the implicit single active segment (an un-migrated install still reads).\n\n### 2.2 Manifest schema (unsigned — by design)\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"schema_version\": 1,\n  \"generation\": <int>,          // monotonic epoch; ++ on EVERY manifest swap. The unified\n                                //   index / kill-switch / session change-token (A3/A4).\n  \"scope\": \"<SCOPE>\",\n  \"segments\": [\n    { \"id\": 0,\n      \"file\": \"segments/seg-00000000.jsonl.gz\",\n      \"codec\": \"gzip\",          // \"none\" | \"gzip\"\n      \"sealed\": true,\n      \"first_seq\": 0,\n      \"last_seq\": 40000,        // null while active\n      \"count\": 40001,           // null while active — CONVENIENCE ONLY (see below)\n      \"first_prev_hash\": \"0000…\",   // MUST == prior segment's boundary_hash; seg 0 == _GENESIS_PREV\n      \"boundary_hash\": \"ab12…\",     // == entry_hash(last_seq); null while active\n      \"bytes\": 41231232,\n      \"sha256\": \"…\" },          // at-rest file integrity; NOT a chain digest\n    { \"id\": 6, \"file\": \"segments/seg-00000006.jsonl\", \"codec\": \"none\",\n      \"sealed\": false, \"first_seq\": 58001, \"last_seq\": null, \"count\": null,\n      \"first_prev_hash\": \"cd34…\", \"boundary_hash\": null, \"bytes\": 812340, \"sha256\": null }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n**Signed? No — and this is sound because the manifest is non-load-bearing:**\n\n- **Order comes from the chain, not the manifest.** `verify()` re-derives every seam linkage from actual segment *bytes*. A forged manifest that drops/reorders an **interior** segment produces a `prev_hash`/seq-gap that `verify_chain` rejects (chain.py:40,44); a dropped **tail** segment shortens `entries()` so `classify_head`'s `n < signed_n` fires (checkpoint.py:93); a dropped **front** segment fails the genesis check (chain.py:40). A doctored manifest fails **closed**.\n- **The convenience fields (`count`, `last_seq`, `boundary_hash`) are advisory.** Standing rule: `count()`, `entry_count`, and every tamper decision **re-derive from records** and MUST NOT read the manifest's `count`/`last_seq` fields. The seam seed (§2.5) reads the prior sealed segment's *actual* last record and cross-checks the manifest boundary rather than trusting it. This keeps the manifest's correctness/trust surface at exactly zero and pre-closes the deferred signed-manifest tier (where a `manifest_sig` field — reserved `null` now — becomes trust-bearing because it would attest pruned ranges).\n\n### 2.3 Path-stable lockfile protocol (invariant 14 / D3)\n\n`spine.lock` is opened `O_RDWR|O_CREAT 0600` **once** and never replaced/unlinked. Its inode is stable across every `os.replace` of segments and the manifest.\n\nOrdering for **every write operation** (`append`, standalone `rotate`):\n1. `with spine_lock(self.path):` — the **in-process re-entrant RLock, keyed on the stable `store.path`** (store.py:37-47, key **unchanged**). This is the line envelope.py:130 already shares; keeping the key stable is what preserves the check-then-append nonce-gate atomicity.\n2. `flock(open(LOCKFILE_PATH), LOCK_EX)` — cross-process exclusion on the stable inode. **Acquired exactly once per top-level op.** Rotation triggered *inside* append runs under the already-held flock (no nested/second flock — flock is per-open-description and a second same-process fd would deadlock).\n3. **Only then** read the manifest → resolve the active segment → re-validate its `(dev,ino)` vs the manifest under the lock → do work.\n4. Release on `with`-exit.\n\n`spine_lock(path)` resolves `str(Path(path).resolve())`; `resolve(strict=False)` is stable even though `spine.jsonl` is absent, so append's `spine_lock(self.path)` and `envelope.spine_lock(store.path)` return the **same** RLock. **This is a hard, tested invariant, not an assumption** (Slice-0 test: `append` and `envelope.consume` share one RLock object).\n\n### 2.4 Crash-safe cutover (write-then-rotate)\n\nAll under the flock from §2.3. **Locked section is O(1)+O(tail); the O(file) gzip is lock-free (D1).**\n\n**A. Normal append (unchanged shape):**\n1. Resolve active segment `P` and generation `G` from the manifest.\n2. `_last_valid_boundary(P)` → truncate any torn tail (**BLOCK-1, active only**, store.py:168-171), get tip. O(1) in-active index update.\n\n**B. Rotation trigger check (same locked section, after the ack):** if `P.size ≥ 16 MiB` or `count ≥ 12 000` → **SEAL-SWAP** (the just-written record is already durable in `P`, becomes `P`'s sealed tip):\n   - `R1` GC any orphan `seg-(k+1)` not in manifest `G` (peer-crash leftover), inline.\n   - `R2` `_last_valid_boundary(P)` (already clean — we just fsync'd) → `boundary_hash H`, `seq_hi`. This is the **A2 seal-time torn-tail truncate** — the only place a sealed file is ever cleaned, since the append-path truncate never revisits a sealed segment (§2.6).\n   - `R3` create `segments/seg-(k+1).jsonl` empty via `O_CREAT|O_EXCL`; `fsync(fd)`; **`fsync(segments/ dir)`**.\n   - `R4` build manifest `G+1`: `seg-k` → `sealed:true` with `last_seq/count/boundary_hash=H`; add `seg-(k+1)` active (`first_seq=seq_hi+1`, `first_prev_hash=H`). `_atomic_write_text(manifest)` = temp→fsync→`os.replace`→**dir-fsync**. **← ATOMIC COMMIT INSTANT.** `generation=G+1`.\n   - `R5` (the empty active already exists and is dir-fsync'd from R3 — active *always* exists, no \"absent==empty\" special case).\n\n**C. Lock-free post-processing (immutable `seg-k`, no lock — D1):**\n   - `P1` gzip `seg-k.jsonl → seg-k.jsonl.gz.tmp`; fsync; `os.replace → .gz`; dir-fsync.\n   - `P2` brief-locked manifest swap (`generation++`): flip `seg-k` `codec:gzip`/`file`.\n   - `P3` move superseded `seg-k.jsonl` to `trash/`; reaper deletes trash entries older than a grace **> any read** (D2).\n\n**Head coupling: NONE.** Retain-all keeps the same records, tip seq, and `head_hash`, so no head swap is part of the cutover — **the head/manifest dual-commit crash window (brief scenario 3) cannot arise.** The head stays a separate, untouched commit.\n\n**Crash consistency (reconciler runs at open AND on every flock acquisition):**\n- Before `R4`: manifest `G` intact; `seg-k` holds the triggering record (durable from step A3); empty `seg-(k+1)` orphan → GC. **Zero loss.** Next append re-fires the trigger and seals cleanly.\n- After `R4`: `seg-k` sealed, `seg-(k+1)` active. Consistent.\n- gzip crash: `.gz.tmp` orphan (GC); manifest still names `.jsonl` until `P2` commits → readers use `.jsonl`, never double-count.\n- **Reconciler is lock-triggered, not startup-only:** the first writer after any crash reconciles before doing work (GC un-manifested segments after confirming the referenced set is chain-valid at every seam). Set derivation is **manifest-only, never a directory glob** (a glob double-counts during the trash window).\n\n### 2.5 Seam-tip derivation & torn-tail (A2, invariants 7/12/13/19)\n\n- `seg-k`'s `boundary_hash=H`, `seq_hi` are written into the manifest **and** are the actual entry_hash/seq of `seg-k`'s last record (guaranteed clean by the R2 seal-time truncate + the fact that the triggering record was fsync'd clean).\n- `seg-(k+1)`'s `first_prev_hash=H`. Its first append (§2.4-A step 2) seeds `seq=seq_hi+1`, `prev_hash=H` from the **prior sealed segment's real tail** (cross-checked vs manifest), **never** `build_chain([...])` (which would restart at seq 0 / genesis — the invariant-13/19 fork trap at store.py:174).\n- gzip is byte-preserving → post-decompress hashes are identical → `verify()` unaffected.\n- `verify_chain`'s strict contiguity (chain.py:44) and prev-linkage (chain.py:40) hold unbroken across every seam because the concatenated stream is one genesis-rooted chain.\n\n### 2.6 Segment-aware, epoch-invalidated index (invariant 9, A3, A4 — unified)\n\n- `_offsets: dict[int, tuple[int,int]]` = `seq → (segment_id, byte_offset)`. A same-size rotation changes generation → a stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction is impossible.\n\n### 2.7 Rotation trigger policy\n\nChecked **after each successful acked append** (cheap `fstat`, already in hand — write-then-rotate): seal when active `size ≥ 16 MiB` **or** `count ≥ 12 000`, whichever first. CONSUMER PLAN — all 34 stay transparent\n\nEvery read primitive presents **ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain** across all segments (invariant 12), and retain-all means **no seq is ever unresolvable**.\n\n- **The 23 full-scanners** (graph/rebuild, revise archivist supersession + promotion-key ledgers, killswitch `_scan_engaged` latch, promotion grants, budget, actor_scope creation-cap, mesh/registry `authorized_devices`+`capability_map`, envelope `device_nonce_highwater`, approvals `pending`, recall, audit, dashboard, bastion `_resolutions`, consolidate/queries.py B1 threads/commitments/contradictions, …): **zero edits.** `iter_records()` spans all segments, so every monotonic high-water / cap / latch / ledger / citation reconstructs over the **complete** record set. This is exactly why retain-all needs **no state-folding**.\n- **The 9 old-seq resolvers** (actor_gate `get(seq)`+forward-iter, actor, operator, perception/egress, bridge/server `/api/record`, ui/server `/api/record`, mcp `episodic_range`, consolidate/gate.py grounding `get(seq)`, transcript.py parent_id): **zero edits.** Every seq that ever existed still resolves; the honest \"compacted below N\" contract (Q5) **never needs to fire**.\n\n**Exactly 3 consumers change — all mechanical, all already reached *past* the API into `store.path`:**\n1. `killswitch.py:56-57` — `store.path.stat().st_size` verdict-cache key → `store.change_token()` (A4). *Required* because `store.path` no longer names a data file.\n2. `gesture/session.py:121` — kill-switch growth detector `store.path.stat().st_size` → `store.change_token()` (A4).\n3. `cli.py:22` — `--reset` `SPINE_PATH.unlink` → `SpineStore.reset()` (else `--reset` leaves stale segments/manifest/lock).\n\n**One audit, NO edit:** `envelope.py:130` `spine_lock(store.path)` — **must stay unedited** and is *verified* to share append's RLock (Slice-0 test asserts one RLock object across a rotation). This is the reconciliation of the RLock-key contradiction the judges flagged: keep the RLock keyed on the stable `store.path`; move only the flock.\n\n**No edit, fixed internally:** `bridge/daemon.py` `tail(2048)` dedup — B3 is fixed inside `tail()`.\n\n---\n\n## 4. TAMPER-MODEL\n\n**The recommendation does NOT touch `verify_chain`, `classify_head`, `verify_head`, `sign_head`, or the head schema.** They are reused byte-identical over a segment-spanning stream. `SignedChainHead`/`ChainEntry` (`extra=\"forbid\"`, `schema_version=1`) gain no fields, so the **A1** breaking-migration hazard (SpineTailer's silent swallow at tail.py:118 vs `verify_checkpoint`'s crash-with-no-try/except at checkpoint.py:108) **never arises**.\n\n**Why C1, C2, state-folding, and head-migration DO NOT arise (retain-all):**\n\n- **C1 (durable external anti-rollback floor) — N/A.** A durable floor is only *needed* when hard-prune turns `classify_head`'s `n < signed_n` alarm (checkpoint.py:93) into a no-op for a removed range. Retain-all removes no record: `entries()` still yields all N, `entry_count` stays the true absolute physical count, and `n < signed_n` remains a **live** guard exactly as today. A replayed old (short-spine, low-count-head) pair is still caught because the current chain is longer/higher-count. The floor stays exactly where it is now — implicitly in the invariant (physical length ≥ signed `entry_count`) plus the persisted head's `last_seq/head_hash` and SpineTailer's in-memory `_high_water` (tail.py:60,90-93). **Rotation weakens the anti-rollback posture by zero bits.** A cold/fresh verifier judges a rotated store *identically* to today's store.\n- **C2 (Merkle commitment over a pruned prefix) — N/A.** `verify()` re-hashes **every** record from actual segment bytes (gzip is byte-preserving). There is no committed-but-not-re-hashed prefix, so no Merkle commitment is needed.\n- **State-folding (invariant 15) — N/A.** Every monotonic state-bearer (per-device nonce high-water, newest warden_checkpoint, account.create cap, device authorize/revoke ledger, kill-switch latch, promotion grants, queued approvals, archivist supersession + promotion-key ledger, open threads/commitments/contradictions + citations) reconstructs over the full record set because scans span segments. **No snapshot record, no state schema, no fold logic.**\n- **Head migration (A1) — N/A.** Rotation is content-preserving: same records, same tip seq, same `head_hash`. The existing owner-signed `head.json` stays valid over the reassembled chain unchanged. No `schema_version` bump.\n\n**The one residual, unchanged from today:** a full `(spine + head)` rollback to a prior owner-signed pair is undetectable by a cold verifier — but that is a *pre-existing* capability of any filesystem-write attacker against today's single-file spine (the owner key is FS-resident, checkpoint.py:9). Rotation does not widen it.\n\n### Deferred hard-prune tier — the operator threat-model decision\n\nIf (and only if) the operator later needs a **hard total-disk cap** (record deletion, not just ~5–8× compression), the following is the full spec — **built only on explicit operator sign-off**, owner-key-gated, fleet-upgrade-gated. Every residual failure mode flags in the **safe** direction (false-tamper, never false-clean):\n\n- **C1 durable floor:** `~/.sigil/floor.json` (0600, **outside** `spine/` so `--reset` can't delete it), monotonic `{last_seq, base_seq, base_count, head_hash}`, updated upward only after a successful full anchor. `classify_head`/`verify_checkpoint` reject any head with `last_seq < floor.last_seq` or `base_count < floor.base_count`, and enforce a `prev_head_hash` meta-chain. **Honest limit:** closed for warm/same-host/returning-paired verifiers; **unclosable from data alone** for a cold verifier bootstrapping off an untrusted mirror (fundamental to any prunable log) — mitigated by seeding the initial floor over the authenticated WireGuard device-pairing channel, never a mirror.\n- **C2 Merkle commitment:** the `kind=\"snapshot\"` record commits `merkle_root` over every pruned leaf (`leaf=entry_hash`) — an **invariant**, because once `classify_head` slices only the live window the pruned prefix is no longer re-hashed.\n- **Head migration:** `SignedChainHead` → `schema_version=2` with six defaulted fields (`base_seq=0, base_prev_hash=_GENESIS_PREV, base_count=0, merkle_root=\"\", snapshot_seq=-1, prev_head_hash=\"\"`), `entry_count = base_count + live` (stays **absolute**). **Blocking gate (from Design-2's judges):** make the signed-head payload **version-conditional** — `_head_payload` must use `model_dump(exclude_unset=True)` (or drop the six fields when `schema_version==1`) so existing v1-signed heads verify byte-identically under the v2 model; otherwise Slice-0 false-alarms *every* deployment to TAMPERING until the next `sigil sign`. Harden `verify_checkpoint` (try/except) and `SpineTailer._resolve_head` (distinct \"schema too new — upgrade\" state) before the first prune.\n- **State-folding:** all ~9 bearers fold their pruned-`[0..K)` contribution into the snapshot payload; consumers switch to `state = SnapshotState.load(store); fold(state, iter_records())`. Seed an **empty** snapshot first (Design-2 Slice 3) to exercise every seed path *before* any deletion.\n- **Referential integrity (invariant 16):** refuse to prune if any retained `parent_id`/`supersedes_id` points into the pruned range unresolved by the archivist ledger; never mutate a retained record's binding; floor `K ≤ oldest-open-workflow-seq`.\n- **Q5 honest resolve:** `resolve(seq) → SpineRecord | PrunedRef{pruned, base_seq, snapshot_seq}` → a \"compacted below N (see snapshot X)\" / 410-Gone, never a bare 404.\n\n**This tier is flagged as an operator decision (§6). It is NOT built by this recommendation.**\n\n---\n\n## 5. SLICE PLAN\n\nEach slice is independently reviewable, shippable, default-safe, and leaves the full test suite green. Rotation stays OFF until Slice 3.\n\n- **Slice 0 — Manifest + path-stable lockfile + split-brain-safe migration.** Add `SEGMENTS_DIR/MANIFEST_PATH/LOCKFILE_PATH` consts. **Two-step migration:** (a) deploy the flock-repointing (flock `spine.lock` before resolving the active segment) to **all** spine-writers and restart them; (b) only then, under `spine.lock`, `os.rename(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` + write the single-segment manifest; add the permanent guard \"refuse to auto-create bare `spine.jsonl` once `manifest.json` exists.\" Keep the RLock keyed on the stable `store.path`. **Adversarial focus:** cross-process append-vs-rotate + append-vs-migration race (asserts no acked-record loss and no forked genesis); **assert `append` and `envelope.consume` share one RLock object** across a rotation. Byte-identical reads on the real 93 MB spine.\n- **Slice 1 — Segment-spanning read API + segment-aware epoch index.** `iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/verify/next_seq` span an ordered multi-segment set; `(segment,offset)` index; generation/dev-ino invalidation; `change_token()`; seam seed from the prior sealed segment's real tail (cross-checked vs manifest); `iter_records` **RAISES** on an unreadable referenced segment. **Adversarial focus:** hand-crafted 2- and 3-segment manifests proving `verify()`/`count()`/`get()` span seams with no gap/dup; assert a manifest-referenced-missing segment RAISES (never yields a short chain → no state-scanner fail-open). Byte-identical outputs on the real spine (single segment in practice).\n- **Slice 2 — A4 unification + reset.** Migrate `killswitch.py:57` and `gesture/session.py:121` to `store.change_token()`; add `SpineStore.reset()` and switch `cli --reset`. **Adversarial focus:** a same-size rotation must change the token at all three sites (no stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction). Self-contained security fix; no rotation yet.\n- **Slice 3 — Crash-safe rotation cutover (the safety-critical slice).** Write-then-rotate; bounded locked seal-swap (seal-time A2 truncate, create+dir-fsync fresh empty active, atomic manifest swap, seam carry); lock-triggered reconciler + open-time orphan GC; trigger policy. **Adversarial focus (REQUIRED merge gate): a kill-9-at-each-cutover-barrier crash-fuzz harness** asserting a `verify()`-clean, non-double-counted, **non-lossy** chain — targeting specifically (i) the empty-active seam seed (must seed from the boundary, never `build_chain`-from-genesis → no seq-0 fork) and (ii) the reconciler (only records provably duplicated in the committed sealed segment may be dropped; **never truncate a possibly-shared inode** — we rename, we don't hardlink). Plus a simulated kill-switch **panic append concurrent with a rotation** asserting the panic is never blocked (D1). Growth is now segmented; disk not yet reclaimed.\n- **Slice 4 — gzip-on-seal (lock-free) + reader-grace unlink.** Compress sealed segments off the lock; brief-locked codec flip; `trash/` + reaper; manifest-reread-on-ENOENT retry on the read path. **Adversarial focus:** a long-running `verify()`/`iter_records(-1)` must not ENOENT mid-scan while a concurrent rotation unlinks a superseded plaintext (D2 fd-snapshot + reread retry); get()/iter transparently decompress. **This is where disk is reclaimed (~5–8×).**\n- **Slice 5 (optional) — persisted `seg-*.idx` sidecars** for O(1) cold-start `get()` into sealed segments; (further optional, default-OFF) cold-archive MOVE with the missing-segment-RAISE + state-bearer floor guardrail.\n\n---\n\n## 6. OPERATOR DECISION\n\n**One decision, framed neutrally:**\n\n> **Retain-all rotation (what ships) bounds per-file size (16 MiB segments) and read/get latency, and reclaims disk ~5–8× via gzip-on-seal — but total history still grows (no record is ever deleted). It weakens tamper-evidence and append-only by zero bits: `entry_count` stays absolute, the chain stays genesis-rooted, the signed head is untouched, and a cold verifier judges the store identically to today.**\n>\n> **If you later need a *hard cap on total disk* (record deletion), that is the deferred hard-prune tier. It reclaims unbounded disk but is a genuine tamper-evidence tradeoff: it widens the rollback blast radius for cold/untrusted-mirror verifiers (a fundamental, unavoidable limit of any prunable log), requires a durable external anti-rollback floor + a Merkle commitment over deleted records + folding ~9 monotonic security-state bearers into a signed snapshot, and turns the first prune into a fleet-upgrade flag-day (breaking v2 head schema). Every residual fails safe (false-tamper, never false-clean), and it is owner-key-gated and opt-in.**\n>\n> **Do you want the hard-prune tier built? If not, retain-all rotation is the autonomous ceiling and ships now.**\n\nRecommendation: **ship retain-all (Slices 0–4); do not build hard-prune unless disk pressure genuinely forces record deletion and you explicitly accept the cold-verifier residual.**",

The current 93 MB spine.jsonl becomes segment-0 with ZERO byte movement: on first open with no manifest, synthesize a manifest naming the existing spine.jsonl as segment id 0, state=active, first_seq=0, last_seq=T, tip=(T,H,...) read from its own tail via _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83-121). No rename, no rewrite. On first rotation seg-0 (spine.jsonl) stays in place, is marked sealed in the manifest, and the new active becomes segments/seg-00000001.jsonl (created empty); the 93 MB file is never moved, only later gzipped in place to spine.jsonl.gz (lock-free) and its manifest path/codec flipped. Each segment records an explicit path so segment 0 keeps its legacy name while new segments use seg-NNN. The active segment is always uncompressed and always the highest id.",
        "manifest_and_lockfile": "MANIFEST (manifest.json, written via checkpoint._atomic_write_text pattern: temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync, checkpoint.py:33-60). Fields: schema_version:int; generation:int (monotonic; THE index/epoch token for invariant 9/A3/A4); created_ts (informational); scope; tip:{seq,prev_hash,cert_digest,entry_hash} (the global chain tip as of last cutover, for invariant-13 next_seq when the active segment is freshly empty); active_id:int; segments:[{id, path, state:active|sealed, codec:none|gzip, location:local|archive, first_seq, last_seq, first_prev_hash, last_entry_hash, records, bytes}]. Segments are strictly ordered and contiguous: segments[i].first_seq == segments[i-1].last_seq+1, and segments[i].first_prev_hash == segments[i-1].last_entry_hash (the carried seam). NOT signed: an unsigned manifest cannot cause an undetected tamper because any set manipulation is already caught by the existing verify path -- dropping the front segment fails verify_chain's genesis check (chain.py:40), dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks prev_hash linkage + seq contiguity at the seam (chain.py:41,44), and dropping the tail segment fails the signed head's entry_count/last_seq/head_hash (checkpoint.py:93, chain.py:73). The manifest's boundary hashes are advisory (index/fast-path only); full verify() recomputes every hash from the actual segment bytes, so a doctored manifest field at worst fail-closes. Signing the manifest is an explicit deferred option, not needed for tamper-evidence. LOCKFILE (spine.lock, invariant 14/D3): a zero-byte file created once (O_CREAT), NEVER os.replace'd/renamed/unlinked. Both append and rotation: (1) acquire the in-process RLock keyed on the STABLE lock path (spine_lock, store.py:37-47, keyed on spine.lock not the rotating active segment); (2) flock(spine.lock fd, LOCK_EX) -- cross-process exclusion bound to a stable inode, so it is sound across os.replace (fixes the flock-binds-to-inode-not-path hazard, D3); (3) while holding both, read manifest -> resolve active -> do tip-read/truncate/append or the cutover; (4) release. SpineStore.path stays a STABLE identity/lock token (= the canonical spine.jsonl path) so envelope.py:129 spine_lock(store.path) needs no edit even though the physical active target rotates.",
        "cutover_algorithm": "Trigger: at the top of append(), under the lock, if the active segment size/count exceeds threshold. BOUNDED LOCKED CRITICAL SECTION (all O(1)-metadata / O(tail), never an O(file) copy, so a kill-switch PANIC append is not blocked -- D1): R1 acquire spine_lock RLock + flock(spine.lock). R2 seal-time torn-tail truncate on the ACTIVE segment: clean_end,tip=_last_valid_boundary(active) (store.py:83-121); if size>clean_end: truncate; fsync fd (A2 -- the only chance to clean a sealed segment, since the append-path truncate never revisits it). R3 read tip=(T,H,prev,cert) from the clean boundary. R4 create fresh empty next segment segments/seg-{active_id+1:08d}.jsonl via O_CREAT|O_EXCL; fsync fd; fsync segments/ dir. (The current manifest-referenced active segment is NEVER renamed or removed -- this is what eliminates the false-truncation crash window.) R5 build manifest gen+1: old active flipped state=sealed with records/bytes/first_prev_hash/last_entry_hash=H filled in, new segment state=active first_seq=T+1 first_prev_hash=H, active_id=+1, tip=(T,H,...). Write manifest.json.tmp; fsync. R6 os.replace(manifest.json.tmp, manifest.json) <-- THE atomic cutover instant; fsync spine dir. R7 release locks. LOCK-FREE POST-PROCESSING (O(file), no lock, on an IMMUTABLE sealed segment -- D1): P1 gzip sealed segment -> .gz.tmp, fsync, os.replace to .gz, dir-fsync. P2 brief-locked manifest update flipping that segment codec=gzip/path=.gz. P3 after a READER-GRACE (D2: readers snapshot ALL segment fds immediately after reading the manifest; unlink deferred behind a refcount/grace timer) unlink the plaintext. P4 optional MOVE .gz to archive/ (fsync both dirs), brief-locked manifest location=archive flip, reader-grace, done. CRASH-CONSISTENCY: crash before R6 = pre-rotation manifest gen G intact, active segment intact with [S..T], orphan empty next-segment ignored (not in manifest G) and GC'd on next open -> zero loss, no false truncation. Crash after R6 = post-rotation set consistent. HEAD coupling: no head swap is part of the cutover (entry_count stays absolute, so entries() still equals the signed count) -- there is NO head/manifest crash-consistency window to manage, unlike the prune case. RECOVERY at open: if an orphan seg-{len(sealed):08d} exists that is not in the manifest, delete it (roll-back) -- deterministic and idempotent.",
        "index_changes": "Segment-aware + epoch-invalidated (invariant 9, A3, A4). (1) _offsets becomes dict[int, tuple[int,int]] mapping seq -> (segment_id, byte_offset_within_segment). Sealed segments are immutable -> indexed once (for gz/archived segments, get() decompresses the one owning segment (bounded to the seal threshold, a few MB) and linear-scans rather than byte-offset-seeking -- avoids gz random-access complexity; deep-history get() is rare). The active segment keeps the current O(1) byte-offset index (store.py:130-138,187-192,353-382). (2) IDENTITY/EPOCH invalidation replaces size-only (fixes the store.py:335,345-347 size-only heuristic): the store caches manifest.generation + active (st_dev,st_ino,size). _check_epoch() runs before any indexed read: if manifest.generation changed OR active (dev,ino) changed -> REBIND (re-resolve segments, invalidate active offsets, reset self._last for A3, reset active scan pos); elif active size grew -> EXTEND (append-only within active, offsets never move); elif active size shrank WITHOUT a generation change -> genuine truncation attack -> REBUILD + surface (stays detectable exactly as today, because rotation ALWAYS bumps generation and so never masquerades as an in-place shrink). (3) A4 UNIFICATION: a single SpineStore.epoch() -> (generation, active_dev, active_ino, active_size). killswitch.py:57 (verdict-cache key) and gesture/session.py:121 (kill-switch growth detector) replace their raw store.path.stat().st_size with store.epoch(); the store's own _ensure_index uses the same token. One shared definition across all three size-as-change-token sites, so a same-size rotation changes generation -> epoch differs -> a stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction is impossible.",
        "rotation_trigger": "Checked at the top of append() under the lock (cheap fstat + manifest.records): seal the active segment when active size >= SPINE_SEGMENT_MAX_BYTES (default 32 MiB) OR active record-count >= SPINE_SEGMENT_MAX_RECORDS (default 50,000), whichever first. Plus a MANUAL trigger (`sigil spine rotate` / store.rotate()) and an opportunistic hook at checkpoint() time (rotate-then-sign, so a freshly sealed segment is immediately covered by the new signed head). Default policy: size-based 32 MiB -> the existing 93 MB spine yields ~3 sealed segments after the first run. Thresholds are configurable in config.py; set to infinity to disable rotation entirely (byte-identical passthrough to today).",
        "consumer_transparency": "All 34 keep working because the SpineStore read API presents ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain spanning all segments (invariant 12). The 23 full-scanners (graph/rebuild.py:55, revise.py:16, audit.py:19, dashboard.py:28, mcp ingest_status count()/verify() server.py:119-127, killswitch _scan_engaged:75, promotion.py:48, budget.py:30, approvals pending:62, actor_scope.py:49, envelope device_nonce_highwater:92-104, mesh/registry authorized_devices:71 + capability_map:46, recall.py:35, bastion _resolutions:253, consolidate/queries.py, perception, gesture/session pending+nonce scans) now have iter_records() span segments automatically -> they see full history -> correct, NO edits; this is exactly why retain-all needs NO state-folding (their monotonic high-waters, caps, latches, ledgers, and citations are all reconstructed over the complete record set). The 9 old-seq resolvers (actor_gate get(seq)+iter L22/27, actor L193/203, operator L241/391, perception/egress L34/39, bridge/server /api/record L322, ui/server /api/record L161, mcp episodic_range L88, consolidate/gate.py:93) resolve ANY historical seq because retain-all keeps every seq present (archived -> decompress/fetch); no 404 for any record that ever existed, honoring the cite-its-seq contract. EDITS REQUIRED (they reach PAST the read API): killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 -> store.epoch() (A4); cli.py:22 SPINE_PATH.unlink on --reset -> SpineStore.reset() clearing all segments+manifest+lockfile+head. bridge/daemon.py tail(2048) needs no edit (tail() is fixed inside the store, B3). transcript.py:97/102 parent_id references stay resolvable (retain-all). Everything else: zero edits.",
        "c1_handling": "C1 does not arise. The durable non-replayable anti-rollback floor is only NEEDED when hard-pruning turns classify_head's n<signed_n truncation alarm (checkpoint.py:93) into a no-op for a removed range. Retain-all removes no record from the logical chain: entries() still yields all N records, entry_count stays the true absolute physical count, and n<signed_n remains a LIVE guard exactly as today. Replaying an old (short-spine, low-count-head) pair is still caught precisely because the current spine remains longer/higher-count than that old head -- byte-identical to today's behavior. So the anti-rollback floor stays exactly where it is now: IMPLICITLY in the invariant (physical chain length >= signed entry_count) that retain-all preserves, plus the persisted signed head's last_seq/head_hash and SpineTailer's monotonic high-water (tail.py:60,89-93). No per-verifier persisted floor and no authenticated head-delivery channel are introduced or needed, because no new rollback surface is opened. A fresh/cold verifier judges a compacted store identically to how it judges the store today.",
        "un_prunable_state_handling": "Untouched. Because retain-all keeps every record and every consumer's full scan spans all segments via iter_records(), NONE of the ~9 monotonic state-bearer classes need folding into any snapshot: per-device nonce high-water (envelope.py:92-104), newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py:283), account.create creation-cap counts (actor_scope.py:49, actor.py:139/247), latest device authorize/revoke per key (mesh/registry.py:66-76), kill-switch latch (killswitch _scan_engaged:75), standing promotion grants (promotion.py:48), unresolved queued approvals (approvals.py:62), archivist supersession chain + promotion-key ledger (revise.py:16-25), and open threads/commitments/contradictions + their source_seq/verified_seq citations (consolidate/queries.py, B1). The ONLY obligation is that their scans reach every segment -- guaranteed since they all route through the segment-spanning store.iter_records(). No snapshot record, no state schema, no fold logic. This is the central safety win of the conservative path.",
        "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "At seal (rotation step R2, before the segment is marked sealed and while it is still the active target under the lock), run _last_valid_boundary on it (store.py:83-121) and truncate(clean_end) any torn tail, then fsync. This guarantees a sealed segment ends exactly on a valid newline-terminated record; since no future append ever revisits a sealed file, and iter_records/_scan_from SKIP a torn line (store.py:209-216,372-378) turning it into a fatal verify_chain seq gap (chain.py:44), the seal-time truncate is the only chance to keep it clean -- this is precisely the A2 fix (the append-path BLOCK-1 truncate at store.py:168-171 only ever protects the active segment). The clean boundary yields the tip entry (seq T, entry_hash H, prev_hash, cert_digest), written into manifest.tip AND into the sealed segment's last_seq/last_entry_hash AND into the next segment's first_seq=T+1/first_prev_hash=H. The fresh active segment starts empty; its first append derives seq=T+1, prev_hash=H from manifest.tip (invariant 13), so verify_chain's strict seq contiguity + prev_hash linkage never break at the seam (chain.py:41,44). gzip is byte-preserving, so hashes recomputed after decompress are identical -> verify() unaffected by compression.",
        "slice_plan": [
          "Slice 0 -- Manifest + stable lockfile + zero-touch migration (rotation OFF/passthrough): introduce manifest.json + spine.lock, route the store's single active segment through the manifest indirection, move the RLock+flock to spine.lock (keyed on the stable path). On first open with no manifest, synthesize one naming the existing spine.jsonl as sole active segment (zero data touch). Golden test: verify()/entries()/count()/get()/iter_records()/tail()/next_seq byte-identical over the real 93 MB spine. Pure plumbing; behaves exactly as today.",
          "Slice 1 -- Segment-spanning read API + segment-aware epoch index: iter_records/get/tail/count/entries span an ordered multi-segment set; index keyed (segment_id,offset); invalidate on manifest generation / active dev-ino change (invariant 9, A3); add store.epoch(). Exercised by tests that hand-craft a 2-segment manifest; byte-identical outputs on the real spine.",
          "Slice 2 -- A4 unification: migrate killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 to store.epoch(); add SpineStore.reset() and switch cli --reset (cli.py:22) to it. No rotation yet; the size-only sites are gone. Independently reviewable security fix.",
          "Slice 3 -- Crash-safe rotation cutover, UNCOMPRESSED: bounded locked critical section (seal-time torn-tail A2, create next segment, atomic manifest swap, carry tip across seam), the open-time recovery/GC of an interrupted rotation, and the trigger policy. Crash tests (kill -9 at each fsync/replace barrier). Growth is now segmented; disk not yet reclaimed.",
          "Slice 4 -- gzip-on-seal (lock-free) + manifest path/codec flip + reader-grace unlink (D2): compress sealed segments off the lock, flip codec via a brief-locked manifest update, defer plaintext unlink behind a reader refcount/grace. This is where disk is actually reclaimed; get()/iter_records transparently decompress.",
          "Slice 5 -- Cold-archive MOVE + honest deep-read: optional location=archive move of old sealed .gz to a separate mount, decompress/fetch-on-read, `sigil spine status|archive`, retention/report. Deep-history reads pull from archive."
        ],
        "risks": [
          "invariant 13 (durable tip -> next_seq): the freshly-empty active segment must resolve its tip from manifest.tip, not from build_chain-at-genesis; a bug here would reset next_seq to 0 and reuse a live seq -> chain fork. Mitigation: tip = (active tail read if non-empty else manifest.tip); heavy test around the first append after a rotation.",
          "invariant 14 / D3 (path-stable lock migration): moving the lock from the target file to spine.lock is the single highest-risk durability change and every ~15 append site inherits it. Mitigation: Slice 0 does ONLY this, with a cross-process append-vs-rotate race test and a re-validate-active-under-lock check.",
          "invariant 9 / A3 / A4 (epoch index): rotation MUST bump generation so it never reads as an in-place shrink (which must stay a detectable truncation attack). Residual: a bug that failed to bump generation would let a rotation mask a truncation. Mitigation: the generation bump and the manifest swap are the same atomic write; the shrink-without-generation-change -> REBUILD+flag path is retained.",
          "A2 (seal-time torn tail): if seal forgets the truncate, a sealed segment ending torn is a permanent false verify() gap that no future append can fix. Mitigation: the seal-time _last_valid_boundary truncate is R2, the first step of the critical section, with a crash-during-rotation test.",
          "D2 (reader vs unlink): a very long-running lock-free reader can pin a plaintext segment so its disk is not reclaimed until it finishes. Bounded and acceptable (correctness preserved via fd snapshot + grace); worst case is delayed reclaim, never a torn read.",
          "verify() I/O cost grows once segments are archived/compressed (it must fetch+decompress every segment). Acceptable: verify is off the hot path (sigil verify / ingest_status), not per-append; note it in the runbook."
        ],
        "invariants_at_risk": [
          "invariant 13 (durable-tip-determines-next_seq): most stressed -- the empty-active-after-rotation case must read the tip from manifest.tip or it forks the chain. Residual risk LOW with tip=max(active-tail, manifest.tip) and a dedicated first-append-post-rotation test.",
          "invariant 14 + D3 (cross-writer serialization / path-stable lock): the flock must move to spine.lock because os.replace of the manifest and rotating active files makes flock-on-the-target inode-unsound. Residual risk MEDIUM until Slice 0's race test passes; this is the highest-risk change.",
          "invariant 9 + A3 + A4 (index correctness under external mutation / epoch invalidation): size-only detection must become generation+dev/ino based, unified across store/killswitch/session. Residual risk LOW-MEDIUM: rotation always bumps generation so a real shrink stays a detectable truncation attack.",
          "A2 (sealed-segment torn-tail): seal-time truncate is the only cleanup path for a sealed file. Residual risk LOW with R2 ordering + crash test.",
          "invariant 12 (one contiguous genesis chain across seams): a boundary gap/dup or wrong segment order would false-TAMPER; mitigated because ordering rests on prev_hash linkage + seq contiguity (chain.py:41,44) recomputed from bytes, not on a directory glob. Residual risk LOW.",
          "D1 (append lock not held across heavy copy): satisfied by design (gzip is lock-free on an immutable segment, locked section is O(1)); residual risk LOW -- only the O(tail) seal-time fstat/truncate runs under the lock."
        ]
      },
      {
        "name": "SEGMENT+SNAPSHOT-ANCHOR spine (aggressive hard-prune behind a signed, Merkle-committed, externally-floored snapshot)",
        "one_line": "Rotate the single 93MB spine.jsonl into hash-linked segments, gzip-on-seal, then hard-prune whole sealed segments below an owner-signed snapshot whose base_prev_hash is the dropped boundary's entry_hash (a chain accumulator over all pruned records) plus a signed Merkle root; entry_count stays ABSOLUTE (base_count+live) so tail-truncation is still caught, a durable external floor.json + head meta-chain close anti-rollback for warm/paired verifiers, and the ~9 monotonic state-bearers seed from a folded-state snapshot record.",
        "retains_all_records": false,
        "reclaims_disk_how": "Two stacked wins. (1) gzip-on-seal (retain-all, no tamper-model change): each sealed segment is gzip-compressed in place; JSONL spine compresses ~5-10x, so 93MB retained -> ~12-18MB, reads stay transparent via a gzip-aware segment reader. This alone bounds per-file size + read latency and is the safe stopping point (slices 0-2). (2) Hard-prune (aggressive, operator-gated): whole sealed segments whose seq_hi < baseline K are UNLINKED after the cutover. The pruned records' bytes are removed from hot disk entirely; the archive of dropped segments is handled per operator flag --archive={cold,delete,keep} (default cold): `cold` moves snap-K-<gen>.jsonl.gz off the hot mount (keeps membership-proof material), `delete` hard-deletes it (max reclaim; you keep the signed merkle_root so a proof someone ELSE presents is still verifiable, but you can no longer reconstruct pruned records), `keep` leaves it hot (audit-heavy). Net after aggressive prune: hot disk ~= the retention window only (e.g. 20k records ~= 40MB raw / ~6MB gzipped) with zero pruned bytes on the hot path. Rotation without prune reclaims nothing but bounds file size; the compression is the retain-all disk win, the segment-unlink is the true space reclaim.",
        "touches_tamper_model": true,
        "head_schema_change": "YES — SignedChainHead -> schema_version=2, adding six fields with never-pruned defaults so a v1 head still validates under the v2 model: base_seq:int=0, base_prev_hash:str=_GENESIS_PREV, base_count:int=0, merkle_root:str=\"\", snapshot_seq:int=-1, prev_head_hash:str=\"\" (the meta-chain link = the immediately-prior accepted head_hash). model_config stays extra=\"forbid\" (defaults make old heads forward-valid; new heads are not backward-valid). REDEFINED semantics, all ABSOLUTE: last_seq = absolute tip seq (front-prune leaves it unchanged), entry_count = base_count + len(live entries) = absolute total records EVER (stays monotonic; this is what preserves invariant 4 as a real anti-truncation gate), head_hash = live tip entry_hash (= base_prev_hash if live is empty). sign_head is refactored to sign_head_fields(head_hash,last_seq,entry_count,base_seq,base_prev_hash,base_count,merkle_root,snapshot_seq,prev_head_hash,...) so the cutover never rescans O(file) to build a head. Migration (A1): (phase 1 / Slice 0) deploy schema-v2-capable code EVERYWHERE while still emitting effectively-v1 heads (all new fields at defaults) — the new model reads every existing v1 head unchanged, so nothing breaks and nothing prunes; harden the two divergent failure sites — checkpoint.verify_checkpoint gets a try/except around model_validate_json (today it crashes `sigil verify` + mcp ingest_status on an unparseable head, checkpoint.py:108) returning (False,'head unreadable — upgrade sigil'), and SpineTailer._resolve_head (tail.py:113-119, today silently swallows -> un-anchored) gates on head.schema_version<=MAX_SUPPORTED and surfaces a DISTINCT loud 'head schema too new — upgrade' state instead of silent un-anchored. A verifier rejects head.schema_version>MAX_SUPPORTED fail-closed rather than crashing. (phase 2) the FIRST prune emits a head with base_count>0; the compactor REFUSES to prune until every known device pubkey has checked in running a v2-capable version (recorded on-spine) OR the owner explicitly overrides — because old binaries cannot be retrofitted (they model_validate_json before they can read schema_version), the prune is a genuine flag day gated on fleet upgrade.",
        "spinestore_api_changes": "Constructor SpineStore(path=SPINE_PATH) unchanged; path.parent becomes SPINE_DIR; if no manifest.json exists the store treats the single file as an implicit one-segment manifest (base=genesis) so every existing deployment is byte-identical until rotation is enabled (default-OFF). Deltas: (1) iter_records(since_seq) concatenates manifest segments in seq order; since_seq<0 => byte0 of segment[0] through active EOF spanning ALL segments; since_seq>=0 => (segment,offset) index seek. Presents ONE contiguous base_prev-anchored chain; BYTE-IDENTICAL yield for a single-segment store. (2) get(seq) segment-aware index; UNCHANGED signature/return (SpineRecord|None) for back-compat, but seq<base_seq returns None. NEW resolve(seq)->SpineRecord|PrunedRef|None where PrunedRef={pruned:True, base_seq, snapshot_seq, archive_ref} — the honest Q5 answer; UI/bridge/mcp switch to resolve(). (3) tail(n) walks segments backward active->sealed until n collected (B3 fix; today seeks only self.path). (4) count() = physical RETAINED count spanning segments; NEW logical_count()=base_count+retained (=head.entry_count absolute total); mcp ingest_status reports 'retained (of logical total; base_count compacted)'. (5) entries() spans segments. (6) verify() calls verify_chain(entries, genesis_prev=manifest.base_prev_hash) and cross-checks manifest.base_prev_hash==head.base_prev_hash; honest msg 'N retained link cleanly from base seq K (M compacted, committed by merkle_root ...)'. (7) next_seq from ACTIVE segment tip (invariant 13), invalidated on generation change (A3). (8) append() resolves active segment from manifest UNDER spine.lock, re-validates dev/ino, torn-tail-truncate on the ACTIVE segment only, seeds seq/prev from the sealed boundary when the active segment is empty. NEW methods: change_token() (A4 unified epoch), rotate(), prune(policy) (owner-key-gated), SnapshotState.load(store) helper. What stays byte-identical: for any store with base_seq==0 and a single segment (every deployment until first rotation) iter/get/tail/count/entries/verify/next_seq/append are byte-for-byte today's behavior — the segment machinery is inert until rotation is switched on.",
        "storage_layout": "$SIGIL_HOME/spine/ (parent of today's spine.jsonl):\n  spine.jsonl                     # THE ORIGINAL 93MB FILE, bytes untouched; becomes segment id=0 with zero rewrite — migration only writes a manifest naming it {id:0,path:'spine.jsonl',seq_lo:0,seq_hi:<current tip>,first_prev_hash:'0'*64,last_entry_hash:<current tip entry_hash>,sealed:false}\n  segments/seg-<NNNNNNNN>.jsonl[.gz]  # all sealed+active segments minted after migration; sealed ones gzipped; id = zero-padded ordinal\n  manifest.json                   # THE authoritative pointer, atomic-swapped (temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync)\n  spine.lock                      # path-stable, inode-stable cross-process lockfile, created once, NEVER os.replaced (fixes flock-binds-to-inode, invariant 14 / D3)\n  cutover.json                    # transient crash-recovery journal (holds the fully-signed new head + full new manifest during a prune commit); absent in steady state\n  head.json                       # HEAD_PATH, now v2 SignedChainHead, atomic-swapped exactly as today (checkpoint._atomic_write_text)\n  keys/                           # unchanged owner keypair\n  archive/snap-<baseSeq>-<gen>.jsonl.gz  # gzipped dropped-segment records for cold membership proofs (moved off-mount / deleted / kept per --archive)\n$SIGIL_HOME/floor.json            # C1 durable external monotonic floor (0600), OUTSIDE spine/ so `--reset` of the spine does NOT delete it\nThe current single 93MB spine.jsonl becomes segment-0 WITHOUT a rewrite: migration is O(1) — write a one-entry manifest + create spine.lock; no copy of 93MB. It stays the active segment (base=genesis) until the first rotation seals it (then optionally gzips it to segments/seg-00000000.jsonl.gz).",
        "manifest_and_lockfile": "MANIFEST (unsigned, but every claim cross-checked against the SIGNED head — keeps invariant 18's single commit point): {schema_version:1, generation:int (epoch, bumped every cutover — the SHARED index/killswitch/session token, A4), scope, active_segment:path, segments:[{id, path, seq_lo, seq_hi, first_prev_hash, last_entry_hash (the sealed boundary carried across the seam), sealed:bool, gz:bool, sha256, record_count, bytes, dev, ino}], base_seq, base_prev_hash (= segments[0].first_prev_hash; the synthetic genesis), base_count, snapshot_seq (latest kind='snapshot' state record), head_hash_at_swap (the head_hash this generation is crash-consistent with)}. WHY UNSIGNED: segment ORDER/linkage self-verifies via prev_hash+seq contiguity in verify_chain (a missing/reordered interior segment breaks the seam link -> caught); a DROPPED TAIL segment is caught by the signed head's absolute entry_count/last_seq; a DROPPED FRONT segment below base is caught by base_prev_hash + head. So the manifest is a performance pointer and verify() re-derives base_prev_hash + boundary hashes from actual segment bytes and reconciles against the signed head; a lying manifest cannot survive verify(). It is bound to the head via generation/head_hash_at_swap for crash-consistency. LOCKFILE PROTOCOL (invariant 14): spine.lock is opened O_RDWR|O_CREAT 0600 ONCE and never replaced; both append and compaction do fcntl.flock(lock_fd,LOCK_EX) BEFORE resolving the active segment from the manifest, then release on completion. In-process spine_lock RLock is re-keyed on SPINE_DIR (not the segment path). Because the flock target is a stable inode that os.replace never swaps, cross-process exclusion is sound even across a manifest/segment cutover; appends additionally re-validate the opened active segment's (dev,ino) against the manifest under the lock and retry on mismatch. This replaces today's flock-on-spine.jsonl which binds to the inode and cannot exclude an appender after an os.replace.",
        "cutover_algorithm": "ROTATION (seal+new active, retain-all, cheap — happens often): bounded locked section only. flock spine.lock -> re-read active tip, run _last_valid_boundary and truncate any torn tail of the active segment NOW + fsync (A2 seal-time truncate, since the append-path truncate can never reach a sealed file) -> create empty new active segment (fsync file + dir) -> write new manifest marking old active sealed (seq_hi,last_entry_hash,record_count cheap; sha256+gzip done lock-free afterward and patched) and naming the new active -> _atomic_write_text(manifest) -> bump generation -> release. No head change (entry_count/last_seq unchanged). PRUNE (space reclaim — the aggressive path): PRECONDITION (lock-free): pick baseline K = seq_lo of a retained segment s.t. K<=tip-RETENTION_RECORDS AND K<=oldest-open-workflow-seq (unresolved approvals / un-consumed steps / pending egress, so old-seq resolvers never dangle) AND K within RETENTION_DAYS AND all device pubkeys v2-capable (A1) OR owner override; REFUSE if any RETAINED record's parent_id/supersedes_id references a pruned seq not resolved by folded_state (invariant 16). HEAVY, LOCK-FREE (D1 — immutable sealed records never move; a kill-switch PANIC append is NOT blocked): scan dropped segments [0..K-1] to build folded_state (all 9 classes) + merkle_root over pruned leaves (leaf=entry_hash) ; gzip dropped records -> archive/snap-K-<gen>.jsonl.gz, fsync, compute archive_sha256; precompute the snapshot record content + cert_digest. BOUNDED LOCKED CRITICAL SECTION (small, O(1)+O(tail) — no O(file)): flock spine.lock -> re-read+torn-truncate active tip -> APPEND the kind='snapshot' record (payload=base ptr+merkle_root+folded_state+archive_ref) : normal O(1) append, fsync -> its seq S becomes the new tip; compute head v2 fields O(1) (head_hash=snapshot entry_hash, last_seq=S, entry_count=prev_entry_count+1, base_seq=K, base_prev_hash=dropped boundary last_entry_hash, base_count=K, merkle_root, snapshot_seq=S, prev_head_hash=old head_hash), sign_head_fields (Ed25519 ~us) -> build new manifest (drop segs<K, set base_*, snapshot_seq, generation+1, head_hash_at_swap=new head_hash) -> WRITE cutover.json journal (temp->fsync->replace->dir-fsync) holding the FULL signed head + FULL new manifest -> os.replace(head.json) -> os.replace(manifest.json) -> dir-fsync -> delete cutover.json -> release lock. AFTER lock, behind reader grace (D2): unlink/cold-move dropped segments. RECOVERY on startup: if cutover.json exists, RE-APPLY idempotently (re-replace head + manifest from the journal, dir-fsync, delete journal) — deterministic because the journal holds the fully-signed head + full manifest; dropped segments not-yet-unlinked are simply absent from the manifest so readers (manifest-only, never glob) don't see them; orphan segments/archive GC'd next start. Crash before journal = no prune happened (old head+manifest intact, appended snapshot record reads as benign 'stale', archive is an orphan). Crash after journal = forward-completed. FSYNC/REPLACE ORDERING across {segments,manifest,head}: archive fsync'd and snapshot record fsync'd BEFORE the journal; journal fsync'd before either swap; head swapped before manifest (both captured in the journal so order within the pair is recovery-idempotent); dir-fsync before journal delete; unlink strictly last. D2 reader grace: readers open ALL segment fds immediately after reading the manifest (POSIX keeps an unlinked-but-open inode readable to completion), and the compactor defers unlink behind a bounded grace after the manifest swap.",
        "index_changes": "Replace size-only detection (store.py:335,345-347) with GENERATION + IDENTITY. _offsets becomes seq->(segment_path,byte_offset). Sealed segments are immutable -> indexed once, never re-scanned. The ACTIVE segment keeps size-grew->extend / (dev,ino changed or size<scan_pos)->rebuild, but rebuild is now driven by an EPOCH check not a bare size compare: the store caches manifest.generation and the active segment (dev,ino,size); on each targeted read it cheaply re-stats manifest — if generation changed it REBINDS (reloads segment set, invalidates offsets for changed segments, re-seeds _last/next_seq from the new active tip, fixing A3's stale cached next_seq on a long-lived cross-process-compacted store instance). This makes a same-size rotation (an equal-or-larger inode swap, the core hazard the size heuristic cannot see) a detected epoch change, not a silent stale-index extend. A4 UNIFICATION: expose SpineStore.change_token() = hash(generation, active_dev, active_ino, active_size); the THREE size-as-change-token sites all switch to it — store._ensure_index (store.py), killswitch.py:57 (`self.store.path.stat().st_size` cache key -> change_token()), gesture/session.py:121-124 (`store.path.stat().st_size` kill-switch growth detector -> change_token()). One shared epoch token replaces all three, closing the same-size-rotation-serves-stale-killswitch-verdict hole in the un-halting (guarded) direction. Invariant 9 met: identity/epoch-based, segment-aware ((segment,offset)), rotation modeled explicitly so a genuine truncation attack (unexpected shrink of a sealed segment / active shrink without a generation bump) stays a detectable rebuild-and-flag, never masked as a benign rotation.",
        "rotation_trigger": "Two independent triggers. ROTATION (seal active, retain-all, always safe): default when the active segment reaches SEGMENT_MAX_BYTES=8MB (~4-5k records) OR SEGMENT_MAX_RECORDS=5000, whichever first; also opportunistically on a `sigil sign` checkpoint boundary (natural seam). Default policy = rotation ENABLED once slice-2 code is deployed (bounds per-file size, read latency, index size; reclaims via gzip-on-seal). PRUNE (space reclaim, tamper-model tradeoff): OPT-IN, never autonomous. Triggered by `sigil spine prune [--keep-records R] [--keep-days D] [--archive={cold,delete,keep}]` or a policy daemon that MUST hold the owner key. Default retention = keep last max(RETENTION_RECORDS=20000, RETENTION_DAYS=90) and never above the oldest-open-workflow-seq, pruning only WHOLE sealed segments below that floor. Default = prune DISABLED. This matches the strategic doctrine: rotation is autonomous/safe; hard-prune (C1 + state-folding + breaking head migration) is an OPERATOR decision, gated behind the owner key + the A1 fleet-upgrade flag day.",
        "consumer_transparency": "The 23 full-scanners keep working via segment-spanning iter_records for the RETAINED range — automatically, no edit — for the coverage-only ones: audit.py, dashboard.py (head from next_seq, recent window by seq arithmetic — gap-safe), perception/recall.py, agents/bastion.py _resolutions, vectors/index.py (cursor in Qdrant, sparse seq set already), notifier.py, budget.py (today-only filter, most prune-tolerant), graph/rebuild.py (full replay — loses pruned history into the graph but doesn't undercount security state). The 9 old-seq resolvers work for retained seqs via segment-aware get()/iter(since_seq); recent-workflow resolvers (operator.py, actor.py, perception/egress.py, approvals.py, actor_gate.py) are protected by the retention floor (K never above oldest-open-workflow-seq) so their seqs always resolve; deep-history resolvers (mcp episodic_range, ui/bridge /api/record/<seq>, graph/query.py + consolidate/queries.py citations) switch from a silent 404/empty to resolve()'s honest 'compacted below seq N (see snapshot S)' (Q5, 410 Gone). CONSUMERS THAT NEED EDITS (the honest cost of the aggressive stance — exactly the ~9 monotonic state-bearers that fold FULL history, invariant 15): each changes from fold-over-iter_records() to state=SnapshotState.load(store); fold(state_seed, iter_records()) — bridge/envelope.py device_nonce_highwater (replay floor), cli.py cmd_warden_anchor_get (newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey), agents/actor_scope.py creation_allowed (account.create cap count), mesh/registry.py authorized_devices+capability_map (latest-wins device auth), governor/killswitch.py _scan_engaged (latch), governor/promotion.py is_promoted (standing grants), agents/approvals.py pending (unresolved — normally empty via floor, defense-in-depth), consolidate/revise.py (supersession closure + promotion-key idempotency ledger), consolidate/queries.py (open threads/commitments/contradictions + source_seq citations, B1). Composition is exact because the snapshot folds ONLY the pruned [0..K-1] contribution and all retained [K..now] records are still physically scanned live — max()/count()/latest-wins-by-seq compose seed⊕retained without double-count. A missed state-bearer edit is the highest-severity failure (silent under-count of nonce floor / creation cap / device authz); Slice 3 de-risks by exercising the seed path with an EMPTY snapshot (base still 0) before any record is ever deleted.",
        "c1_handling": "The durable non-replayable floor lives in $SIGIL_HOME/floor.json (0600), OUTSIDE spine/ so a spine `--reset` does not delete it, atomic-written like the head. Monotonic content {schema_version,last_seq,base_seq,base_count,head_hash,ts}, updated ONLY-upward after a successful full anchor verification. classify_head/verify_checkpoint reject any head/snapshot with last_seq<floor.last_seq OR base_count<floor.base_count OR entry_count<floor.entry_count, and enforce the meta-chain: head.prev_head_hash must equal a previously accepted head_hash (floor.head_hash) so the sequence of heads is an unbroken chain even as records are pruned. This is the persisted, cross-restart, cross-process version of tail.py:60's in-memory _high_water (which resets to -1 each process and cannot carry the guarantee across a compaction run in a separate process) and it replaces the DORMANT prev_highwater on the checkpoint path (checkpoint.py:96 -> chain.py:78). HONEST THREAT BOUNDARY: C1 is CLOSED for warm same-host verifiers, a restarted host, and a returning paired device (each holds a monotonic floor that rejects a replayed old (short-spine, low-count, validly-owner-signed) pair — the pair hard-prune makes otherwise indistinguishable from current, because prune turns classify_head's n<signed_n alarm into a no-op for the pruned range). C1 is NOT closable from data alone for a truly COLD verifier bootstrapping trust off an UNTRUSTED MIRROR with no prior floor — it can only trust 'validly owner-signed' and cannot distinguish current from replayed-old; this is a FUNDAMENTAL limit of any prunable log (you cannot prove the absence of pruned records to a party that never observed them). Mitigation, not a proof: a cold device seeds its initial floor over the AUTHENTICATED device-pairing channel (WireGuard + device key, SIGIL Phase 9) directly from the live host — never from a mirror — so first-sync is out-of-band; thereafter it is monotonic. Residual honest caveat: an attacker with filesystem write to the host defeats floor.json AND the owner private key together (both FS-resident, the accepted local-first simplification checkpoint.py:9 already makes for the key), so the floor does not need to exceed the key's protection — but hard-prune widens the blast radius (a rolled-back short pair now reads as legitimate to a fresh verifier where retain-all would have caught it via the absolute count). A TPM/secure-element monotonic counter is the named upgrade path for hosts that have one.",
        "un_prunable_state_handling": "All folded into one kind='snapshot' spine record (appended at the tip in the prune's locked section, so it binds+chains normally per invariant 10, is covered transitively by the signed head, and is retained), payload.folded_state folding ONLY the pruned range [0..K-1]; consumers compose seed⊕retained-live-scan. Schema: device_nonce_highwater:{device_pubkey:max_nonce} (envelope.py replay floor; consumer seeds hi from this then max() over retained); warden_checkpoints:{pubkey:{count,seq,head_hash}} (cli.py newest-per-pubkey anti-rollback high-water); creation_counts:{service_label_or_origin:int} (actor_scope.py account.create cap; seed+count retained); device_auth:{device_pubkey:{state,seq,ts}} (mesh/registry latest-wins; retained higher-seq overrides); killswitch:{latched:bool,seq} (killswitch.py latch as-of K; retained engage/release override by seq); promotion_grants:{grant_key:{state,seq}} (promotion.py standing grants latest-wins); open_approvals:[{target_seq,proposer,seq,payload_digest}] (approvals.py unresolved — normally empty because the retention floor keeps unresolved workflow records unpruned; carried as defense-in-depth); archivist:{superseded_seqs:[...],promotion_keys:{key:seq},live_entities:[{seq,digest}]} (revise.py supersession closure so a pruned superseding record can't resurrect a stale fact, + promotion-key idempotency ledger so a pruned promotion can't be re-promoted); open_loops:{threads:[...],commitments:[...],contradictions:[...],citations:{...}} (queries.py open set + source_seqs so the 'never forget a loop' promise survives, B1). Referential integrity (16): the precondition scan REFUSES to prune if any retained record's parent_id/supersedes_id points into the pruned range unresolved by archivist.superseded_seqs; retained records' parent/supersedes are NEVER mutated (would break their binding). SnapshotState.load(store) reads the latest snapshot record (via manifest.snapshot_seq) and returns these maps for the ~9 consumer edits.",
        "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "A2 is closed by running the SAME _last_valid_boundary truncate at SEAL time, inside rotation's locked section, against the segment being sealed, then fsync BEFORE marking it sealed=true in the manifest. This is required because the append-path truncate (store.py:168-171) only ever protects the ACTIVE append target (self.path); once a segment is sealed no future append touches it, so a crash on the record that triggered rotation would otherwise leave a torn line that iter_records/_scan_from skip (store.py:209-216,372-378) and verify_chain turns into a permanent false seq-gap at the seam (chain.py:44). Sealing therefore guarantees every sealed segment ends on a valid newline-terminated record. Seq/entry_hash carried across the seam: the sealed segment's (seq_hi, last_entry_hash) is written into the manifest; the freshly-created active segment is EMPTY, so append's first write on it must NOT call build_chain (which would restart at seq 0) — _active_tip() seeds seq/prev from the manifest's prior-segment boundary (seq_hi+1, prev_hash=last_entry_hash) when the active segment has no valid records. Thus append_entry([boundary], cert_digest) yields seq_hi+1 with prev_hash=last_entry_hash, so verify_chain's strict seq contiguity (chain.py:44) and prev_hash linkage (chain.py:40) hold unbroken across every segment boundary and the concatenated stream reads as one chain.",
        "slice_plan": [
          "Slice 0 — schema-v2-capable + inert (the A1 flag day): add the six snapshot fields to SignedChainHead with never-pruned defaults, bump MAX_SUPPORTED_SCHEMA, add genesis_prev param to verify_chain (default _GENESIS_PREV), refactor sign_head -> sign_head_fields, harden verify_checkpoint (try/except) and SpineTailer (loud distinct 'schema too new' state). No behavior change: every head still effectively v1; ships fleet-wide before anything prunes.",
          "Slice 1 — manifest + path-stable spine.lock + segment-aware store, SINGLE segment: manifest.json (implicit one-segment for existing spines, O(1) migration of the 93MB file with no rewrite), (segment,offset) index, generation/identity epoch detection, change_token(); migrate the 3 size-token sites (store/killswitch/session) to change_token(). Byte-identical reads, no rotation yet — the big mechanical slice, independently reviewable as 'make the store segment-capable with one segment'.",
          "Slice 2 — rotation (retain-all) + gzip-on-seal + tail() spans segments (B3) + seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2): reclaims disk via compression and bounds file size/read latency with ZERO tamper-model change; all 34 consumers stay transparent. THE SAFE STOPPING POINT if the operator declines hard-prune.",
          "Slice 3 — snapshot record + folded state + the ~9 state-bearer consumer edits, but NO deletion: write a snapshot folding an EMPTY pruned range (base stays 0) so SnapshotState.load + every seed⊕retained consumer path is exercised and tested end-to-end BEFORE any record is removed (de-risks the highest-severity failure mode).",
          "Slice 4 — hard-prune + v2 head re-sign + cutover.json journal/recovery + C2 Merkle: prune() baseline computation (retention floor + open-workflow floor + verifier-version gate + referential-integrity refusal), lock-free scan/gzip/merkle, bounded locked cutover, drop segments; verify()/classify_head/verify_head switch to snapshot-aware absolute-count slicing. This is where base_prev_hash/entry_count-absolute/merkle_root land.",
          "Slice 5 — C1 durable floor.json (monotonic external floor + meta-chain prev_head_hash check) + honest pruned-seq resolution (resolve()/410 Gone in ui/bridge/mcp episodic_range) + authenticated first-sync floor seeding at device pairing. The anti-rollback hardening + honest-contract layer on top of a working prune."
        ],
        "risks": [
          "A missed state-bearer edit silently under-counts a SECURITY floor (device nonce replay, account.create cap, device authorization) — the chain still verifies, so no integrity check flags it. Highest-severity failure; mitigated by Slice 3 exercising the seed path with an empty snapshot before any deletion + a per-consumer test.",
          "FS-write attacker on the host defeats floor.json AND the FS-resident owner key together; hard-prune WIDENS the blast radius vs retain-all (a rolled-back short (spine,head) pair now reads as legitimate to a fresh verifier, where the absolute entry_count would previously have caught it).",
          "A truly cold verifier bootstrapping off an untrusted mirror cannot distinguish current from replayed-old pruned pair from data alone — fundamental; only the out-of-band pairing channel closes it, and a device that never pairs stays exposed.",
          "Merkle root binds pruned records for proof VERIFICATION, but --archive=delete makes reconstruction impossible; and vectors/index.py reset() after a prune permanently loses re-embeddable text for pruned records (steady-state fine since Qdrant persists, only a policy-change reset loses recall).",
          "Coupled head+manifest cutover: a journal/recovery bug yields a false-TAMPERING window (scenario 3) or a lost ack (scenario 4); mitigated by idempotent forward-recovery from a fully-signed journal and unlink-strictly-last.",
          "The heavy lock-free scan/gzip/merkle races concurrent appends to the range it is folding — but it only reads IMMUTABLE sealed segments below K and appends stay above K, so the fold is stable; still, the baseline K must be fixed before the scan and re-validated at cutover.",
          "verify_chain genesis_prev: a bug seeding the wrong base_prev_hash would accept a forged rebase; base_prev_hash is signed in the head, cross-checked against the manifest boundary AND the retained entries[0].prev_hash, so a mismatch fails closed — but this is now load-bearing where a hard-coded constant used to be."
        ],
        "invariants_at_risk": [
          "Invariant 4 (signed-head entry_count anti-truncation) — MOST STRESSED: redefined to ABSOLUTE (base_count+live). It stays a real gate only because entry_count/base_count are both signed and monotonic; the C2 slice change (classify_head slices only entries[:entry_count-base_count]) means the pruned prefix is no longer re-hashed, so its integrity now rests ENTIRELY on the signed base_prev_hash chain-accumulator + merkle_root. Residual risk: if merkle_root/base_prev_hash binding is ever computed wrong, a pre-archival edit becomes unprovable.",
          "C1 durable anti-rollback floor — the decisive residual: closed for warm/same-host/returning-paired verifiers via floor.json + meta-chain; UNCLOSABLE from data alone for a cold untrusted-mirror verifier.",
          "C2 Merkle commitment — now an INVARIANT not a nice-to-have: the snapshot's committed digest must bind every pruned record because the live-only slice removes the only other check.",
          "Invariant 14 (cross-writer lock covering the compactor) — highest DURABILITY risk: path-stable spine.lock touches every one of ~15 append sites; the old flock-on-target is inode-unsound once os.replace enters.",
          "Invariant 15 (monotonic-state-by-full-scan) — highest OPERATIONAL risk: 9-11 consumer edits, any one missed under-counts security state silently.",
          "A1 (closed signed head wire schema) — a genuine flag day: old binaries cannot be retrofitted, so the first prune is gated on fleet upgrade; failure = silent un-anchored (SpineTailer) or crash (verify_checkpoint) until hardened.",
          "Invariant 5 (monotonic last_seq) — preserved by keeping seq ABSOLUTE and only relocating the genesis prev; the renumber-to-0 trap (which would false-trip _high_water and make truncation indistinguishable from rebase) is explicitly avoided.",
          "Invariant 16 (referential integrity across a prune) — protected by the retention floor + archivist.superseded_seqs carry + a compactor refusal, but a policy-window bug could dangle a parent_id/supersedes_id into a deleted-and-uncommitted target.",
          "D1 (no heavy copy under the append lock) — satisfied by lock-free immutable-record scan/gzip/merkle + a bounded O(1)+O(tail) locked cutover, so a kill-switch PANIC append is never blocked; a regression that pulls the scan under the lock re-opens the worst-case (late panic)."
        ]
      },
      {
        "name": "SEGMENT-ROTATION (retain-all, API-preserving): spine grows into size-bounded segments behind a byte-identical SpineStore API",
        "one_line": "Bound per-file size and get()-latency by rotating the single spine.jsonl into a manifest-linearized set of immutable sealed segments plus one path-stable active segment; retain every record so entry_count stays absolute, the chain stays genesis-rooted, the signed head is untouched, and all 34 consumers read one contiguous genesis chain through an unchanged API.",
        "retains_all_records": true,
        "reclaims_disk_how": "gzip-on-seal of COLD sealed segments (Slice 4) is the only real disk win. Rotation alone (Slices 0-3) bounds per-file size and get() latency but reclaims NO disk (retain-all). Sealed segments are immutable, so a lock-free background pass rewrites seg-A-B.jsonl -> seg-A-B.jsonl.gz (hash-chain JSONL compresses ~4-7x in practice: seq/hashes are high-entropy, but repeated field keys and payload text compress well). A WARM WINDOW (default: newest 2 sealed segments + the active) stays uncompressed so hot full-scan consumers (budget.py per-decision, dashboard, audit) mostly hit uncompressed bytes; only cold history pays decompression on a full scan. Honest disk-vs-CPU trade: gzip makes O(file) full scans of cold data slower. No hard-delete, no cold-archive move; every record stays online and get()-resolvable.",
        "touches_tamper_model": false,
        "head_schema_change": "NONE. Rotation is CONTENT-PRESERVING: moving records between files does not change the set of records, so store.entries() yields a byte-identical ChainEntry sequence before and after any rotation (same seqs, cert_digests, prev_hash/entry_hash linkage, same genesis root). sign_head (chain.py:56-63) still signs entry_count=total physical records and last_seq=true tip; classify_head (checkpoint.py:87-101), verify_head (chain.py:66-80) and the `n<signed_n` truncation alarm (checkpoint.py:93) are UNCHANGED and keep passing. SignedChainHead/ChainEntry stay extra=\"forbid\" with schema_version=1 (models.py:56-64) untouched, so A1's breaking-migration and the two-consumers-fail-differently hazard NEVER ARISE. No head is even re-signed AT rotation (the logical chain is identical across a seal), so the manifest swap and the head are decoupled and crash-scenario-3 (crash between segment cutover and head swap) cannot occur.",
        "spinestore_api_changes": "All public SIGNATURES stay byte-identical: iter_records(since_seq=-1), get(seq), tail(n), count(), entries(), verify(), next_seq, append(kind,source,actor,payload,parent_id,supersedes_id,ts). Behavior deltas, all internal:\n- iter_records: resolves the segment set from the MANIFEST (never a glob), concatenates sealed segments in manifest order (decompressing .gz) then the active segment, yielding records in seq order; per-segment range-filter (skip rec.seq < segment.first_seq) closes the crash-window seam. since_seq<0 => byte-0 of segment-0 => verify_chain sees ONE contiguous genesis chain (invariant 12). since_seq>=0 => index -> (segment,offset), start mid-segment, continue across later segments.\n- get: index -> (segment,offset); open that segment. Active/sealed-uncompressed = O(1) seek; sealed-gz = open+scan to seq, O(segment) bounded (rare deep reads).\n- tail(n): now spans active then walks back into prior sealed segment(s) if active holds < n (fixes B3); bounded seek-from-end per file.\n- count(): sum(seg.record_count for sealed segments in manifest) + scan(active). Equals len(entries()) exactly, so classify_head's len-vs-entry_count stays exact.\n- next_seq: resolves tip from the ACTIVE segment; if active is empty, from the manifest active.first_prev_hash/last sealed boundary (last_seq+1), NEVER treating an empty active as genesis (invariants 13,19); invalidated on manifest generation change (fixes A3 for long-lived instances).\n- entries()/verify(): concatenate all segments -> verify_chain over the whole -> semantics unchanged.\n- append: resolves the active segment via the manifest under the path-stable lock; seeds append_entry from the active tip, or from the manifest boundary when active is empty; torn-tail-truncate on the active segment ONLY (BLOCK-1 preserved); triggers rotation after a successful append when the active file crosses threshold.\nBYTE-IDENTICAL and untouched: append record shape + cert_digest (store.py:146-150), entry_hash/prev_hash derivation (chain.py:15-34), verify_chain/verify_head/sign_head/classify_head, verify_record, SpineTailer (it re-reads entries(), now segment-spanning, transparently). A back-compat fallback: if no manifest exists, SpineStore treats its path arg as the sole active segment (single-file mode) so tests and custom-path constructions keep working.",
        "storage_layout": "Everything under ~/.sigil/spine/ (config.py:50-55; SPINE_PATH=spine.jsonl, HEAD_PATH=head.json, KEYS_DIR=keys):\n  spine.jsonl                      # THE ACTIVE segment, path-stable NAME (inode changes at each seal). Appends always land here.\n  manifest.json                    # sole linearization point (segment set + seq ranges + boundary hashes + generation)\n  spine.lock                       # dedicated path-stable lockfile, NEVER renamed/unlinked (the flock target)\n  head.json                        # unchanged single signed head (checkpoint.py), signs the full concatenated chain\n  segments/\n    seg-00000000-00040000.jsonl.gz # sealed, immutable, gzipped (cold)\n    seg-00040001-00058000.jsonl    # sealed, immutable, uncompressed (warm window)\n    seg-*.idx                       # optional per-segment seq->offset sidecar (Slice 5) for O(1) cold-start get\n  keys/                            # unchanged\n  trash/                           # superseded sealed files awaiting reader-grace unlink (D2)\nThe CURRENT 93MB spine.jsonl becomes segment-0 with ZERO rewrite: Slice 0 simply writes a manifest naming ~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl as the single active segment (id 0, first_seq 0, first_prev_hash=GENESIS). No move, no copy, byte-identical. The FIRST rotation is what renames it into segments/ (via hardlink, O(1)), not the introduction of the manifest.",
        "manifest_and_lockfile": "MANIFEST (manifest.json), written ONLY via checkpoint._atomic_write_text (reused verbatim: temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync, checkpoint.py:33-60). NOT independently owner-signed — by design. Authority stays with the two EXISTING signed/verified artifacts: verify_chain's prev_hash linkage + seq contiguity (chain.py:40-45) catches a missing/reordered/corrupt INTERIOR segment at the seam; the unchanged signed head catches a dropped TAIL segment via n<signed_n + head_hash/last_seq (checkpoint.py:93-98).
