A forged manifest that drops/reorders segments therefore produces a chain that FAILS verify_chain or a head that fails classify_head — so no new signed artifact is needed (this is why touches_tamper_model=false). Schema:\n{schema_version:1, generation:<u64 monotonic, ++ on every manifest swap — THE unified epoch/index-invalidation token>, scope:<SCOPE>, segments:[\n  {id, path (relative), compressed:bool, first_seq, last_seq (null if active/open), first_prev_hash (=GENESIS for id 0, else prior seg last_entry_hash — the append-seam link), last_entry_hash, record_count, byte_size (uncompressed logical, for the offset index), file_sha256 (integrity of the sealed file AT REST; not a chain digest), active:bool}\n]}\nLOCKFILE (spine.lock): a dedicated file created once, NEVER renamed/replaced/unlinked — so its inode is stable (invariant 14 / D3). BOTH append and rotation fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX) it BEFORE reading the manifest to resolve the active segment. This replaces today's fcntl.flock on the spine file itself (store.py:158-160), which binds to the inode that the seal-rename swaps and thus cannot exclude a cross-process appender across a rotation. The in-process spine_lock (RLock, store.py:37-47) is re-keyed from the resolved active-file path to the STABLE spine-dir path so re-entrancy (the nonce gate, envelope.py:129) and the RLock key survive a seal; because the active NAME is always \".../spine.jsonl\", callers passing store.path as the lock key (envelope.py) need no edit.",
        "cutover_algorithm": "Trigger checked opportunistically after a successful append when active byte_size >= threshold. Rotation seals the active segment via a HARDLINK-before-commit ordering so the sealed content exists at its final path BEFORE the commit and the old name is dropped only AFTER — no crash window can lose an ack'd record. Bounded LOCKED critical section (all O(1) except an ~8KB seek-from-end torn-tail scan; NO O(file) work — satisfies D1):\n 1. flock spine.lock; acquire spine_lock RLock (same order as append).\n 2. Re-read manifest -> active = spine.jsonl, first_seq A. If active has 0 records, abort (nothing to seal).\n 3. SEAL-TIME torn-tail truncate: _last_valid_boundary(spine.jsonl) (store.py:83-121), truncate any torn tail, fsync -> (last_seq B, last_entry_hash H). (A2: the sealed segment is guaranteed to end on a valid record; the append-path truncate can never reach a sealed file.)\n 4. os.link(spine.jsonl, segments/seg-A-B.jsonl); fsync dir. Sealed content now exists under its final name AND still under spine.jsonl (shared inode).\n 5. Build new manifest: append sealed seg-A-B (A..B, first_prev_hash=prior boundary, last_entry_hash=H, compressed:false), set active=spine.jsonl (first_seq=B+1, first_prev_hash=H, last_seq=null), generation++. Write temp -> fsync -> os.replace(manifest) -> dir-fsync.  <-- COMMIT POINT.\n 6. Drop the old active name: os.unlink(spine.jsonl) (seg-A-B keeps the inode+content), then leave spine.jsonl ABSENT (absent == empty active). dir-fsync.\n 7. Release locks.\nBecause the lock is HELD across steps 5->6, no concurrent appender ever sees the post-commit-pre-unlink double-content; only a crash can. CRASH RECOVERY (startup reconciler under the lock): if the active file's first record seq < manifest.active.first_seq, it is a pre-unlink leftover whose records are provably committed in the sealed segment -> truncate active to 0. If manifest names a sealed path that is absent but a same-range file exists un-manifested, drop the orphan. Crash BEFORE step 5 commit = intact pre-rotation state (orphan hardlink cleaned up). Crash AFTER commit = post-rotation state (reconciler truncates the stale active tail). The signed head is NOT touched at rotation (logical chain identical), so no head/manifest co-commit is needed.\nDEFERRED, LOCK-FREE (gzip, Slice 4): compress seg-A-B.jsonl -> .gz (temp->fsync->replace->dir-fsync), then a SECOND O(1) locked manifest swap flips compressed:true/path and generation++, then move the superseded uncompressed file to trash/ (not immediate unlink).\nREADER GRACE (D2): a reader opens fds for ALL manifest-named segments IMMEDIATELY after reading the manifest and before yielding any record (POSIX keeps an unlinked-but-open inode alive), so a concurrent swap+unlink cannot ENOENT mid-read; superseded files go to trash/ and a reaper deletes trash entries older than a grace window strictly greater than any read.",
        "index_changes": "_offsets becomes seq -> (segment_id, byte_offset_in_uncompressed_stream) (invariant 9's (segment,offset) key). Sealed segments are IMMUTABLE, so each one's seq->offset sub-index is built once and cached (optionally persisted as seg-*.idx for O(1) cold start, Slice 5); the ACTIVE segment keeps today's extend-on-grow logic (store.py:345-346: size>_scan_pos => extend; append-only within the active segment => offsets never move). Change-detection moves from SIZE-ONLY to EPOCH/IDENTITY: the store caches the manifest `generation` (and st_dev/st_ino of the active file as a belt); each targeted read cheaply re-reads generation — on change it rebinds the active path, rebuilds the active index, invalidates next_seq (A3), and keeps sealed sub-indexes (still valid, keyed by immutable segment id). This replaces store.py:335,345-351's size-only extend/rebuild, whose shrink-branch would otherwise MISFIRE reading a rotation as an in-place truncation attack, and whose same-or-larger-inode-swap blind spot (crash scenario 8) it closes. A4 UNIFIED: the same `store.epoch()` (generation) replaces the three independent st_size change-tokens — store._ensure_index (store.py:335), killswitch.py:56-57 (verdict-cache key), gesture/session.py:121 (kill-switch growth detector) — so a same-size rotation can never serve a stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction.",
        "rotation_trigger": "Seal fires when the ACTIVE segment's byte size crosses a threshold, checked opportunistically after each successful append (cheap fstat already in hand). Default policy: seal at 16 MB active size (=> ~7-8k records at current ~2KB/record; bounds per-file size and keeps the active-segment offset index and torn-tail scans small) with a secondary record-count cap (default 25k) whichever hits first. Also seal-on-demand: `sigil spine rotate` (manual) and an OPTIONAL seal-at-checkpoint hook so `sigil sign` boundaries align with segment boundaries (nice for reasoning, not required). Rotation NEVER fires from a read path and never mid-append; only after an ack, under the lock, and it aborts if the active segment is empty. Thresholds are config values; a warm-window count governs which sealed segments stay uncompressed.",
        "consumer_transparency": "All 34 stay working because iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/verify/next_seq present ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain (invariant 12) and retain-all means no seq is ever unresolvable. The 23 FULL-SCANNERS (graph/rebuild, revise, audit, dashboard, mcp ingest_status, killswitch/promotion/budget/actor_scope scans, approvals.pending, envelope device_nonce_highwater, mesh/registry authorized_devices, bridge/daemon dedup, recall, consolidate/queries B1, etc.) just iterate the manifest-ordered concatenation — every historical record is still physically present and read, so the security-relevant monotonic scans (nonce high-water, warden_checkpoint high-water, account.create cap, device authorize/revoke, kill-switch latch, promotion grants, archivist supersession+promotion-key ledger, open threads/commitments/contradictions) are exactly preserved. The 9 OLD-SEQ RESOLVERS (mcp episodic_range, actor_gate.action_approved get(seq)+forward-iter, perception/egress get(seq), bridge/server + ui/server GET /api/record/<seq>, operator/actor execute/undo, graph anchor citations) keep resolving because get(old_seq) reaches into sealed segments — no prune, so no 404/empty-window regression and question-5's honest 'compacted below N' contract NEVER needs to fire. ONLY 3 consumers must change, and all 3 already reached PAST the SpineStore API into store.path (they were never API-transparent): (1) killswitch.py:56-57 st_size verdict-cache key -> store.epoch()/generation (A4; note: today every append grows the active file and a seal SHRINKS it -> shrink already forces a rescan, so this is correctness-hardening, not a live danger); (2) gesture/session.py:121 st_size growth detector -> generation (A4); (3) cli.py --reset SPINE_PATH.unlink -> clear the whole spine dir (all segments + manifest + lockfile + trash), else a reset leaves stale sealed segments. Optional NON-required efficiency edits: budget.py/actor_scope full scans could take a since_seq bound to avoid O(total) per decision (rotation makes the O(total) scan span more files), but correctness does not require them.",
        "c1_handling": "C1 DOES NOT ARISE. Retain-all keeps entry_count ABSOLUTE (checkpoint.py:92, chain.py:60), so classify_head's `n < signed_n` truncation alarm (checkpoint.py:93) stays a LIVE guard for the entire history — it is never converted into a no-op for any pruned range, because nothing is pruned. The design therefore does NOT weaken today's anti-rollback posture one bit: the durable floor remains exactly what it is now — the owner-signed head's entry_count/last_seq/head_hash on disk (checkpoint.py:82-84) plus SpineTailer's in-memory monotonic _high_water (tail.py:60,90-93). No new external floor is needed, and none is added. C1 (a durable non-replayable floor OUTSIDE the compactable data, needed so a cold/fresh verifier can distinguish a legit compaction baseline from a replayed old short-spine/low-count pair) is specific to HARD-PRUNE, which this design deliberately declines. Hard-prune — with its C1 floor, C2 Merkle commitment, snapshot state-folding, and the breaking head migration — is a tamper-evidence tradeoff that is an explicit OPERATOR decision, not something this autonomous, minimal-blast-radius design takes.",
        "un_prunable_state_handling": "Retain-all => the ~9 monotonic state-bearer classes are UNTOUCHED; none is folded into any snapshot because no snapshot exists. Each is reconstructed by a full scan that now spans all segments via the transparent iter_records, so each reconstruction reads exactly the same record set as today: (1) per-device nonce replay high-water (envelope.py:88-104); (2) newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey / anti-rollback high-water (cli.py:283-287); (3) account.create / web.actor.step creation-cap count (actor_scope.py:49, actor.py:139,247); (4) latest device authorize/revoke per key — the bridge authorization ledger (mesh/registry.py:66-76, capability_map:42-50); (5) kill-switch engage/release latch (killswitch.py:75 _scan_engaged); (6) standing promotion grants latest-wins (promotion.py:48-55); (7) unresolved queued approvals (approvals.py:62 pending); (8) archivist supersession chain + promotion-key idempotency ledger (revise.py:16-25, promotion_ledgers); (9) open threads/commitments/contradictions + their source_seq/conflicting_seq/verified_seq citations (consolidate/queries.py — the B1 first-class MCP surface). The SOLE requirement is that these full scans reach every segment, which the segment-spanning iter_records delivers by construction. This is the decisive advantage over hard-prune, where each of these would have to be enumerated into a signed snapshot payload or silently corrupt.",
        "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "At seal, step 3 runs _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83-121) on the active spine.jsonl UNDER THE LOCK and truncates any torn tail BEFORE the hardlink (A2: the append-path truncate at store.py:168-171 only ever touches the ACTIVE file, so without a seal-time truncate a segment sealed just after a torn interrupted write would end on a garbage line that iter_records/_scan_from skip (store.py:209-216,372-378) and verify_chain turns into a permanent false seq gap (chain.py:44) at the seam). The truncate yields the clean boundary (last_seq B, last_entry_hash H), recorded in the manifest as the sealed segment's last_entry_hash and as the new active segment's first_prev_hash. The next append to the fresh (empty) active segment seeds append_entry([boundary]) from H at seq B+1 — carrying seq AND entry_hash across the seam so verify_chain's prev_hash linkage (chain.py:40) and strict seq contiguity (chain.py:44) never break. Because the two files are read separately (per-file `for raw in f`), the within-file torn+new merge hazard that BLOCK-1 fixes does not recur across the seam; only the skipped-line-becomes-gap hazard does, and the seal-time truncate closes it. next_seq/append also handle the empty-active case by seeding from the manifest boundary, never from genesis (invariants 13,19).",
        "slice_plan": [
          "Slice 0 — Manifest + path-stable lockfile, NO rotation. Write manifest.json naming the current spine.jsonl as the single active segment (id 0, first_seq 0, first_prev_hash=GENESIS) with ZERO file move; create spine.lock; move append's flock from the spine file to spine.lock and re-key the RLock to the spine dir; SpineStore resolves the active segment via the manifest. One segment => behavior byte-identical to today; default-safe; ships the D3 lock-soundness fix and the generation-epoch foundation. Reviewable in isolation.",
          "Slice 1 — Segment-spanning READ API. iter_records/get/entries/count/verify/tail concatenate manifest-ordered segments; segment-aware, epoch-invalidated (segment,offset) index; next_seq/append seed from the manifest boundary when the active is empty. Still one segment in practice => still byte-identical, but validated against a synthetic multi-segment fixture (hand-built 3-segment chain) proving verify()/count()/get() span seams with no gap/dup.",
          "Slice 2 — A4 unified epoch token + reset. Migrate killswitch.py and gesture/session.py off store.path.stat().st_size to store.epoch()/generation; make cli.py --reset clear the whole spine dir. The ONLY consumer edits in the program.",
          "Slice 3 — Rotation (seal-on-threshold). The O(1) hardlink+manifest cutover, seal-time torn-tail truncate, boundary carry, startup reconciler, held-lock commit->unlink, reader open-all-fds grace. Retain-all, sealed segments UNCOMPRESSED. This is where per-file size and get() latency get bounded; touches_tamper_model still false. Verify: rotate under concurrent appends + a simulated kill-switch panic append and assert the panic is not blocked (D1) and verify() stays green across the seam.",
          "Slice 4 — gzip-on-seal disk reclaim. Lock-free async compression of cold sealed segments outside the warm window; second O(1) manifest swap; trash/ + reaper unlink. The disk win; last because it is the only slice with a full-scan CPU trade-off, and it is independently revertible.",
          "Slice 5 (optional) — Persisted seg-*.idx sidecars for O(1) cold-start get() into sealed segments; crash-fuzz harness (kill -9 at each cutover step) asserting the reconciler always yields a verify()-clean, non-double-counted chain."
        ],
        "risks": [
          "Seam double-content crash window: a crash between the manifest commit (step 5) and the old-active unlink (step 6) leaves records A..B in BOTH seg-A-B and spine.jsonl. Mitigated by the startup reconciler (truncate active records whose seq < manifest.active.first_seq — provably committed in the sealed segment) AND a defensive per-segment reader range-filter. Residual: a reconciler bug could over-truncate, but only records provably duplicated in the committed sealed file.",
          "os.link is POSIX/same-filesystem; on non-POSIX (no fcntl anyway) fall back to copy-then-fsync for the seal. Low risk on this Linux/Kali local-first target.",
          "gzip-on-seal adds decompression CPU to the 23 full-scanners, worst for budget.py (O(total) per decision). Mitigated by the warm uncompressed window; residual CPU on deep/cold full scans. Recommend (non-blocking) since_seq bounds on the hottest scanners.",
          "get() into a sealed .gz segment is O(segment) not O(1) (gzip has no random seek). Bounded by segment size and rare (deep-history get). Slice 5 .idx sidecars only help within the decompressed stream, not around it; keeping the warm window uncompressed keeps recent get() O(1).",
          "Long-lived store instances (gesture daemon, agent runner) must poll manifest generation on targeted reads to catch a rotation done by another process (A3) — one extra cheap manifest stat per targeted read; full scans re-read the manifest anyway.",
          "tail() spanning into sealed .gz segments adds decompression to the bridge arm-dedup path; bounded to the dedup window and already backstopped by ARM_FRESHNESS, so safe but a small latency add.",
          "Reader-grace correctness depends on readers opening ALL segment fds before yielding; a future refactor that lazily opens a segment mid-iteration would reopen the D2 unlink race. Enforce with a single _open_segments() choke point + a test."
        ],
        "invariants_at_risk": [
          "Invariant 14 (cross-writer serialization / path-stable lockfile, D3) — MOST stressed: moving the flock from the inode-bound spine file to spine.lock touches every one of the ~15 append call sites' durability guarantee; a miss reopens the os.replace-vs-appender lost-ack race (crash scenario 4). Residual risk: an appender that resolves the active path but doesn't re-validate under the lock could write a stale fd; closed by resolving-active-under-lock only.",
          "Invariant 9 (index correctness under external mutation, + A4) — the (segment,offset) key and generation-based invalidation replace size-only detection across THREE sites; residual risk is a site still trusting size (crash scenario 8's equal-or-larger inode swap) if the A4 unification is incomplete.",
          "Invariant 12 (read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain) — the concatenation seam must be gap/dup-free; stressed by the crash-window double-content and by manifest-vs-glob discipline (a glob would double-count during the trash window). Residual: any reader deriving the set from a directory listing instead of the manifest.",
          "Invariants 13 + 19 (durable tip / genesis-vs-empty) — the empty-active-is-not-genesis trap: append/next_seq MUST seed from the manifest boundary, or a fresh active segment reads as an empty/new spine (last_seq->0, seq reuse, chain fork). Stressed at every seal.",
          "Invariant 7 / A2 (torn-tail truncate at SEAL time) — must run on the sealed segment under the lock; a miss = permanent false verify() gap at the seam.",
          "NOT at risk (deliberately): invariant 4 (entry_count absolute), the head schema (A1), C1 durable floor, C2 Merkle commitment — all untouched because retain-all never prunes, which is precisely why this is the minimal-blast-radius path and hard-prune's tamper-evidence tradeoffs are left as an explicit operator decision."
        ]
      },
      {
        "name": "Retain-all segment rotation with gzip-on-seal (phased; hard-prune deferred to an explicit operator decision)",
        "one_line": "Bound spine per-file size and read latency by rotating the single spine.jsonl into an ordered set of sealed+gzipped segments named by one atomically-swapped manifest, reassembled into ONE genesis-anchored chain by a segment-spanning SpineStore read API — retaining every record so entry_count stays absolute, the signed head is untouched, and no tamper-model/head-schema/state-folding change is needed; hard-prune (record deletion) is a separate deferred tier gated on an operator threat-model decision.",
        "retains_all_records": true,
        "reclaims_disk_how": "gzip-on-seal + per-file-size bounding + optional cold-archive MOVE; NO hard-delete. Every record is retained, so total logical history is retained — be honest that the win is compression and file-size/read-latency bounding, not record removal. JSONL compresses ~5-10x (scope/kind/source keys and the 64-hex hashes are highly repetitive); the current 97 MB / 43,412-record spine.jsonl becomes seg-00000 and, once sealed and gzipped, drops to ~10-18 MB, with each subsequent sealed segment ~3-4 MB (.gz). The uncompressed ACTIVE segment is bounded to the seal threshold (~32 MiB). Read latency is bounded because full scans stream fixed-size segments and get()/old-seq resolution touches exactly ONE segment resolved by the manifest seq range (not the whole 43k+ file). Slice 4 (optional, still retain-all) MOVEs old sealed .gz segments into an archive/ dir the manifest still references — relocation for cheaper/slower storage, fully readable, chain-complete, zero deletion. Actual hard record-deletion for further reclaim is the DEFERRED hard-prune tier (operator decision), out of scope for what ships now.",
        "touches_tamper_model": false,
        "head_schema_change": "None. Retain-all rotation requires ZERO change to SignedChainHead or ChainEntry (reuse/models.py:47-64, both extra=\"forbid\"), no schema_version bump, and no A1 migration/flag-day. Because rotation moves no record, alters no seq, and changes neither the tip seq nor the tip entry_hash, the EXISTING owner-signed head.json (checkpoint.py:79-84) stays valid over the reassembled chain: head_hash == entry_hash(tip), last_seq == tip seq, entry_count == total records — all identical after reassembly. Old verifiers (mobile clients, `sigil verify`, mcp ingest_status via verify_checkpoint at checkpoint.py:104-109, SpineTailer._resolve_head at tail.py:110-119) read byte-identical head bytes and, via the segment-spanning entries(), see the identical genesis-anchored chain, so classify_head (checkpoint.py:87-101) still passes with no code change. This sidesteps the A1 asymmetric-failure trap entirely (SpineTailer swallows a bad head silently, verify_checkpoint crashes with no try/except) — that trap only fires for a v2 snapshot-bearing head, which belongs to the DEFERRED hard-prune tier, not this iteration.",
        "spinestore_api_changes": "All public signatures stay identical; semantics transparently span the manifest segment set in seq order. (1) iter_records(since_seq): resolve the start segment from the manifest seq ranges, seek within it, then continue into each subsequent segment in manifest order, decompressing .gz on the fly, yielding ONE contiguous seq-ordered stream. Full scan (since_seq<0) = concat of every segment in order = the exact record list produced today. (2) get(seq): O(1) manifest range-lookup (seq_lo<=seq<=seq_hi) picks the one owning segment, then byte-offset index (active/uncompressed sealed) or a single-segment stream-scan (gzipped) — bounded to one segment, never the whole spine; returns the identical record or None. (3) tail(n): now walks segments backward from the active one across the seam until n records are collected (FIXES B3 — today store.py:244-268 seeks only self.path, so tail(2048) silently shrinks right after a rotation). (4) next_seq / _read_last_entry (store.py:270-272,305-312): resolve the tip from the ACTIVE segment (the unsealed one, invariant 13); if the active segment is freshly-rotated and empty, seed seq/prev_hash from the prior segment's manifest boundary (seq_hi, boundary_hash) so append_entry links across the seam. (5) append (store.py:141-194): content shape, cert_digest, entry_hash derivation, fsync-before-ack all BYTE-IDENTICAL; only the framing changes — it flocks the path-stable lockfile (not the segment fd), resolves the active segment under that lock, runs _last_valid_boundary truncate-before-append on the ACTIVE segment only (invariant 7), derives the tip from the active segment (or the seam boundary when empty), and after the write may trigger rotation if over threshold. (6) NEW read-only helper change_token() -> (manifest_generation, active_dev_ino, active_size) — the unified epoch token (below). BYTE-IDENTICAL and untouched: the JSONL record format, verify()/verify_chain, entries(), count(), verify_record, checkpoint()/classify_head/verify_checkpoint, the head, and all SpineTailer logic (it consumes iter_records + entries()).",
        "storage_layout": "~/.sigil/spine/ gains: spine.lock (NEW, zero-byte, fixed path, never replaced/unlinked — the inode-stable flock target, invariant 14); manifest.json (NEW, the sole authoritative ordered segment set, swapped via checkpoint._atomic_write_text); segments/ dir holding seg-00000.jsonl[.gz] ... seg-NNNNN.jsonl (the highest-numbered, unsealed one is the ACTIVE append target, always uncompressed); head.json UNCHANGED (single signed commit point); keys/ UNCHANGED; archive/ (NEW, empty until the optional Slice-4 cold-move). The current single 93/97 MB spine.jsonl becomes segment 0 WITHOUT a rewrite: a one-time migration os.rename(spine.jsonl -> segments/seg-00000.jsonl) (atomic on the same filesystem — the spine dir) then writes a single-segment manifest naming seg-00000 as the active segment. No 97 MB copy. Back-compat: if manifest.json is absent (pre-migration or a rolled-back state), the store treats a bare spine.jsonl as an implicit single active segment 0, so an un-migrated install still reads. config.py gains SEGMENTS_DIR, MANIFEST_PATH, LOCKFILE_PATH, ARCHIVE_DIR constants (the natural home; config.py itself does no I/O). store.path becomes \"the resolved active segment path\" (dynamic), so the two raw-stat consumers that read store.path.stat() must move to change_token() (see index_changes/A4).",
        "manifest_and_lockfile": "MANIFEST (manifest.json), a JSON object: {schema_version:1, generation:<int monotonic epoch, ++ on every cutover — this is the index/cache invalidation token>, segments:[ {id:<int>, file:\"segments/seg-00001.jsonl.gz\", compression:\"gzip\"|\"none\", seq_lo:<int>, seq_hi:<int|null>, count:<int|null>, first_prev_hash:<hex — must equal the previous segment's boundary_hash; for id 0 it is _GENESIS_PREV>, boundary_hash:<hex = entry_hash of seq_hi, null while active>, sealed:<bool>} ... ]}. The single active segment has seq_hi/count/boundary_hash = null and sealed=false. SIGNED? No — for retain-all the manifest is an availability/ordering POINTER, not a trust root: the signed head still anchors the whole reassembled chain (entry_count absolute) and verify_chain independently re-derives every seam linkage from the actual records, so a malicious manifest reorder/drop is caught by verify() failing (a dropped TAIL segment is caught by classify_head's n<signed_n). Manifest order is the CANDIDATE set; the CHAIN proves it (matches \"order must come from the chain, not filenames\"). A reserved optional manifest_sig field stays null now and becomes owner-signed only in the deferred hard-prune tier (where the manifest attests pruned seq ranges and thus becomes trust-bearing). LOCKFILE (spine.lock): a fixed-path, never-replaced, never-unlinked zero-byte file. PROTOCOL — both append() and rotation, in order: (a) acquire the process-wide re-entrant spine_lock RLock now keyed on the LOCKFILE path (store.py:37-47, key change), (b) open spine.lock and fcntl.flock(fd, LOCK_EX) on ITS stable inode, (c) ONLY THEN read manifest -> resolve active segment -> re-validate generation -> do work, (d) release on close. Because the lockfile inode never moves, flock is sound across os.replace of segments and the manifest (fixes invariant 14 / D3: today's flock on the target file binds to an inode os.replace swaps away, so an appender holding an old a+b fd would write to the unlinked old inode and lose an ack'd record).",
        "cutover_algorithm": "Rotation = seal the active segment k and open segment k+1; gzip is the ONLY heavy step and is done LOCK-FREE (D1). PHASE 1 SEAL-SWAP (bounded locked critical section — flock spine.lock): run _last_valid_boundary on seg-k and truncate any torn tail (A2 seal-time clean); read tip -> seal_seq_hi, boundary_hash=tip.entry_hash; create empty seg-(k+1).jsonl, fsync its fd + fsync segments/ dir; write a new manifest marking seg-k sealed (compression still \"none\", seq_hi/count/boundary_hash filled) and adding seg-(k+1) active (first_prev_hash=boundary_hash); _atomic_write_text(manifest) (temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync, checkpoint.py:33-60); generation++; release lock. This section is tiny: a seek-from-end truncate check + one create+fsync + one manifest atomic-write. Appends now land in seg-(k+1) with NO lock contention. PHASE 2 COMPRESS (LOCK-FREE, no spine.lock held): gzip seg-k.jsonl -> seg-k.jsonl.gz.tmp, fsync it. seg-k is now immutable (below the sealed tip) so this needs no lock — a kill-switch PANIC append to seg-(k+1) is never blocked (D1). PHASE 3 GZ-SWAP (bounded locked section): os.replace(seg-k.jsonl.gz.tmp -> seg-k.jsonl.gz), fsync dir; write a new manifest updating seg-k file->.gz, compression->gzip; _atomic_write_text(manifest); generation++; release. PHASE 4 READER-GRACE + UNLINK (D2): defer unlink of the now-superseded seg-k.jsonl behind a reader grace (readers snapshot ALL segment fds immediately after reading the manifest, so an in-flight verify()/iter_records holds the old inode to completion; unlink only files unreferenced by the current manifest AND older than a fixed grace, e.g. 60 s, or via a refcount), then unlink + dir-fsync. ORDERING across {segments, manifest, head}: the HEAD IS NOT TOUCHED by rotation (retain-all keeps the same records, tip seq, and head_hash), so there is NO head/manifest crash-consistency coupling — crash scenario 3 (segment vs head disagreement) cannot arise. The manifest is the sole linearization point; the head stays a separate, untouched commit. CRASH SAFETY: crash before Phase-1 replace = pre-rotation state, all records present; crash after Phase-1 = seg-k sealed-uncompressed + seg-(k+1) active, fully readable, .gz.tmp is orphan garbage; crash after Phase-3 = manifest points at .gz, the uncompressed seg-k is an orphan (readers use the manifest, never a glob, so no double-count); crash during Phase-4 = orphan file only. Startup sweep unlinks any segments/ file not referenced by the current manifest after confirming the referenced set is chain-valid at every seam. entries()/count() derive the set solely from the manifest, never a directory glob.",
        "index_changes": "Make the seq->offset index segment-aware and replace size-only detection with an epoch/identity token (invariant 9). (1) _offsets becomes seq -> (segment_id, byte_offset) — for gzipped segments the offset is into the DECOMPRESSED stream. Sealed segments are immutable, so their per-segment index is computed once and cached for the process lifetime; the manifest seq_lo/seq_hi ranges give an O(1) seq->segment map with NO scan, so get(seq) resolves the owning segment for free and then offset-seeks (active/uncompressed) or single-segment stream-scans (gz, bounded to ~12k records). (2) EPOCH/IDENTITY invalidation replaces store.py:335,345-347 size-only logic: change detection keys on change_token() = (manifest_generation, active_segment_dev_ino, active_segment_size). generation change -> the active segment rotated -> refresh the segment set from the manifest (sealed indices stay valid, they are immutable) and rebuild the active-segment index; active_size GREW at same generation -> extend the active index (append-only within the active segment, offsets never move); active_size SHRANK at same generation -> genuine truncation attack on the active file -> rebuild + surface (unchanged detection, now correctly scoped to the active segment rather than misfiring on rotation, which was risk #7/scenario-8). (3) A4 UNIFIED across all three st_size sites: expose change_token() and swap store._ensure_index (store.py:335), killswitch.py:57 (Path(store.path).stat().st_size verdict-cache key), and gesture/session.py:121 (kill-switch growth detector) ALL onto the SAME token. Today a same-size rotation would serve a STALE kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction (dangerous); one shared (generation, dev_ino, size) token closes all three at once — and it is a correctness fix regardless of rotation because size-only was already unsound across os.replace.",
        "rotation_trigger": "Seal the active segment when it reaches count >= 12,000 records OR size >= 32 MiB, whichever first (12k records at the current ~2.2 KB/record density is ~26 MB, so the two bounds are comparable). DEFAULT POLICY: evaluate the trigger at checkpoint()/`sigil sign` time (checkpoint.py:79), i.e. OFF the hot append path and coupled to the owner's daily anchor — a natural safe point where the just-signed head anchors the exact chain and, if the deferred tier is ever enabled, the owner key is already in hand. Plus a manual `sigil spine rotate` command. An opportunistic post-append trigger fires only when the active segment runs FAR over threshold (e.g. >= 2x) so a burst between checkpoints cannot grow one segment unbounded. gzip-on-seal defaults ON; cold-archive (Slice 4) defaults OFF.",
        "consumer_transparency": "Because every read primitive spans segments to present ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain and NO record is removed, 32 of 34 consumers work UNCHANGED. The 23 full-scanners keep total history: graph/rebuild.py full replay, revise.py archivist supersession + promotion-key ledgers, killswitch _scan_engaged latch, promotion.is_promoted, budget.spent, actor_scope.creation_allowed cap-count, mesh/registry authorized_devices + capability_map, envelope.device_nonce_highwater replay watermark, recall, audit, dashboard, consolidate/queries.py (B1) threads/commitments/contradictions — all get a complete iter_records() across all segments, so no monotonic reconstruction regresses and no security watermark drops. The 9 old-seq resolvers stay correct because every acked seq still resolves: get(old_seq) via manifest range -> owning segment -> present record (actor_gate, egress, operator, actor, bridge/server /api/record, ui/server /api/record, mcp episodic_range, consolidate/gate get(), bridge notifier cursor); iter_records(since_seq=old) seeks into the right segment and spans forward. EXACTLY TWO files need edits, both mechanical correctness fixes: (a) killswitch.py:55-57 and gesture/session.py:121 swap raw store.path.stat().st_size for store.change_token() (the A4 unified token) — required because store.path now names a dynamic active segment; (b) cli.py --reset (SPINE_PATH.unlink at cli.py:22) generalizes to clear the whole segments/ dir + manifest.json + spine.lock, else --reset leaves stale rotated segments. tail()-based dedup in bridge/daemon.py is fixed transparently by the segment-spanning tail() (B3). No consumer makes a seq==line or 0-start assumption, so nothing else changes.",
        "c1_handling": "C1 DOES NOT ARISE in this design. C1 is \"no durable anti-rollback floor lives outside the compactable data,\" which only bites once records are DELETED so that a short-spine + low-count-head pair becomes replayable as a legitimate compaction baseline. In retain-all rotation, entry_count stays ABSOLUTE (checkpoint sign_head over entries() still counts every record, chain.py:60) and the on-disk reassembled chain is always >= the signed count, so classify_head's `n < signed_n` truncation alarm (checkpoint.py:93) remains a LIVE guard — a replayed old (short, low-count) head is still caught because the current reassembled chain is longer. The durable anti-rollback floor therefore stays exactly where it lives today: the persisted signed head's entry_count/last_seq/head_hash vs the full reassembled chain (plus SpineTailer's in-memory high-water, tail.py:60,90). Rotation weakens none of it. C1 becomes decisive ONLY at the DEFERRED hard-prune tier, where shrinking is legitimate and a fresh/cold verifier loses the \"longer chain\" discriminator — and closing it there needs a floor OUTSIDE the compactable data (per-verifier local last-accepted last_seq and/or authenticated head delivery a mirror cannot roll back). That is the exact operator threat-model decision this design refuses to make autonomously.",
        "un_prunable_state_handling": "Untouched — retain-all means every monotonic state-bearer class is reconstructed intact by full scans that now simply span all segments, so NO folding into a signed snapshot is needed. Each of the ~9 classes stays exact: per-device nonce replay high-water (envelope.py device_nonce_highwater), newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py warden_anchor_get), account.create / web.actor.step creation-cap counts (actor_scope.creation_allowed, actor.py), latest device authorize/revoke per key (mesh/registry.authorized_devices — the authorization ledger the whole bridge trusts), kill-switch engage/release latch (killswitch _scan_engaged), standing promotion grants (promotion.is_promoted), unresolved queued approvals (approvals.pending), open archivist supersession chain + promotion-key idempotency ledger (revise.py), and open threads/commitments/contradictions + their source_seq/verified_seq citations (consolidate/queries.py, B1). Because the records that carry them are all still present and the scans reach every segment, every reconstruction floor is preserved with zero new schema. State-folding into a signed snapshot payload is a DEFERRED hard-prune requirement (question 4), not part of what ships.",
        "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "A sealed segment is guaranteed clean because the SEAL step (Phase 1 of cutover, under the bounded lock) runs _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83-121) on the segment and truncates any torn tail BEFORE marking it sealed and BEFORE gzipping it (A2) — the append-path truncate (store.py:168-171) can never reach a sealed file since it is no longer the active target, so without this a crash on the rotation-triggering record would leave a permanent false verify() gap at the seam (iter_records skips a torn line, store.py:209-216, and verify_chain turns a skip into a fatal seq gap, chain.py:44). Since seal fires at checkpoint (a clean point after a successful fsync'd append), a torn tail is unlikely, but we run the truncate unconditionally (cheap seek-from-end). seq/entry_hash are carried across the seam via the manifest: the sealed segment records boundary_hash = tip.entry_hash and seq_hi = tip.seq; the next segment's first_prev_hash = boundary_hash, and the first record appended there derives its prev_hash from that boundary (append_entry seeded from the manifest seam when the active segment is empty), so verify_chain's strict prev_hash linkage (chain.py:40) and seq contiguity (chain.py:44) hold unbroken across every segment boundary. gzip only ever compresses an already-truncated-clean sealed segment, so a .gz never contains a torn tail.",
        "slice_plan": [
          "Slice 0 — Manifest + lockfile scaffolding, no behavior change. Add SEGMENTS_DIR/MANIFEST_PATH/LOCKFILE_PATH consts; one-time migration os.rename(spine.jsonl -> segments/seg-00000.jsonl) + write a single-segment active manifest (no 97MB copy); store resolves the active segment from the manifest (bare-spine.jsonl back-compat if no manifest); append flocks the path-stable spine.lock instead of the segment fd. Still ONE segment, no rotation — reads are byte-identical, full test suite green. Proves the plumbing with zero rotation risk.",
          "Slice 1 — Segment-spanning read API. iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/next_seq span an ORDERED multi-segment set from the manifest (still one real segment; exercised by tests that hand-craft a 2-segment manifest). Segment-aware index (seq->(segment,offset)), manifest-range lookup for get(), internal change_token() replacing size-only detection. Pure read-path, independently reviewable.",
          "Slice 2 — A4 unified change-token + reset. Swap killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 raw st_size for store.change_token(); generalize cli.py --reset to clear segments/ + manifest.json + spine.lock. Small mechanical consumer-side correctness fixes, independently testable.",
          "Slice 3 — Rotation/seal cutover (the crash-safety-critical slice). Seal-swap (bounded lock) + lock-free gzip + gz-swap + reader-grace unlink + startup orphan sweep + seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2). Trigger at checkpoint() over threshold + manual `sigil spine rotate`. Gets the heaviest review and a crash-injection matrix over the 10 durability scenarios + a seam-verify test. After this the spine actually rotates and gzips; disk and per-file size bounded.",
          "Slice 4 (optional, still retain-all) — Cold-archive MOVE of old sealed .gz segments into archive/ referenced by the manifest (cheaper/slower storage), fully readable, chain-complete, zero deletion.",
          "DEFERRED TIER (NOT autonomous — surface ONE explicit operator decision): hard-prune record deletion — signed SnapshotAnchor, v2 SignedChainHead schema (A1 migration + flag-day), state-folding of all ~9 monotonic bearers, C1 durable external anti-rollback floor, C2 Merkle commitment over the pruned prefix, and the honest 'compacted below seq N (see snapshot X)' resolve contract (question 5). Operator prompt: 'Retain-all rotation + gzip + cold-archive is the autonomous ceiling; do you accept trading total-recall tamper-evidence of old records for disk reclamation beyond gzip? If yes we build the signed-snapshot tier.'"
        ],
        "risks": [
          "Slice 3 cutover is the crux: a bug in seal-swap/manifest ordering or the seam boundary could break prev_hash linkage or seq contiguity at a segment boundary -> false verify() gap across the whole spine. Mitigate with the 10-scenario crash-injection matrix plus a dedicated seam-verify test (reassemble a 3-segment chain and assert verify()==today).",
          "gzip is not block-seekable, so get() on a deep-old gzipped segment is O(segment) stream-decompress, not O(1). Bounded to ~12k records/segment and only the 9 rare old-seq resolvers hit it, but a pathological repeated-deep-get consumer pays each time. Mitigate: LRU-cache the decompressed segment or persist a per-sealed-segment .idx sidecar (seq->decompressed-offset); or adopt bgzip/per-record-member framing for true random access (flag as an efficiency option, not required for slice 3).",
          "D2 reader-grace correctness: a long verify()/iter_records(-1) scan must not be ENOENT'd mid-read when rotation unlinks a superseded segment. Mitigate by snapshotting ALL segment fds right after reading the manifest and deferring unlink behind a fixed grace + generation check (never unlink a manifest-referenced file).",
          "A3 stale cache in long-lived store instances (gesture daemon, agent runner): a rotation done by another process must invalidate a cached next_seq/_last and the offset index. change_token() forces active-segment re-resolution on generation change; residual risk if any consumer caches next_seq without consulting the token — audit the ~8 next_seq readers.",
          "Migration rename (Slice 0) is atomic only within one filesystem; if segments/ ever lands on a different mount the rename degrades to a 97MB copy. Keep segments/ under the spine dir (it is). Also guard the migration itself under the lockfile so no append races the rename.",
          "Every one of the ~15 store.append call sites inherits the path-stable-lockfile change (invariant 14/D3); getting the lock key wrong (still keying on the segment path) silently reopens the cross-process fork window. This is the single highest-risk durability change — state it as a precondition of Slice 0, not a later mitigation."
        ],
        "invariants_at_risk": [
          "invariant 12 (read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain) — MOST stressed: the multi-segment reassembly must be exactly seq-ordered with no gap/dup at any seam or every full-scan consumer and verify() see a false break. Residual risk: an off-by-one at a boundary.",
          "invariant 14 + D3 (path-stable lockfile / flock binds to inode not path) — all cross-process append correctness now rests on spine.lock being flocked before resolving the active segment; residual risk if any writer bypasses the lockfile or keys the RLock on the old segment path.",
          "invariant 7 + A2 (torn-tail truncate on ACTIVE only / seal-time truncate on the sealed segment) — a sealed segment that ends torn is a permanent false verify() gap the append path can never repair; residual risk if seal-time truncate is skipped or races the last append.",
          "invariant 9 + A4 (index epoch/identity, unified across store/killswitch/session) — size-only detection is unsound across os.replace and lives in three places; residual risk if any of the three is missed or a same-size rotation slips a stale kill-switch verdict through.",
          "D1 (no append lock across the O(file) heavy copy) — gzip MUST stay lock-free so a kill-switch PANIC append is never blocked; residual risk if the seal and compress steps are collapsed into one long locked section.",
          "D2 (live-reader vs unlink race across the manifest indirection) — residual ENOENT risk if readers glob or re-open segments lazily instead of snapshotting fds after the manifest read.",
          "NOTE — deliberately NOT stressed (the whole point of phasing): C1 (durable external floor), C2 (Merkle prefix commitment), A1 (head-schema migration), invariants 4/15/16 state-folding — retain-all keeps entry_count absolute, the head untouched, and every state-bearer intact, so these are carried by the existing code unchanged and are deferred to the operator-gated hard-prune tier."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "judged": [
      {
        "design": {
          "name": "RETAIN-ALL SEGMENT ROTATION with gzip-on-seal + cold-archive MOVE (no prune, no C1, no head-schema change)",
          "one_line": "Bound spine growth by rotating the append target into sealed, gzip-compressed, optionally cold-archived segments behind ONE atomic manifest, retaining every record so entry_count stays absolute and the chain stays genesis-rooted; verify_chain/classify_head/the head schema are never touched, and all 34 consumers keep working via a segment-spanning SpineStore read API.",
          "retains_all_records": true,
          "reclaims_disk_how": "gzip-on-seal + optional cold-archive MOVE. Each sealed (immutable) segment is compressed lock-free (JSONL of hex digests + repeated keys compresses ~4-8x with gzip -6); the active segment (<= threshold, default 32 MiB) stays uncompressed. 93 MB -> ~12-20 MB local once all sealed segments compress. Optional MOVE of old sealed .gz to archive/ (a separate/cheaper mount) bounds the HOT spine dir to (active <=32 MiB + a few recent sealed .gz), independent of total history; total retained footprint grows at ~1/5-1/8 the raw byte rate. Archived segments decompress/fetch on demand (rare deep-history path). No hard-delete of any record, ever. Setting thresholds to infinity disables rotation (pure passthrough, byte-identical to today).",
          "touches_tamper_model": false,
          "head_schema_change": "None. SignedChainHead and ChainEntry (reuse/models.py:47-64, extra=\"forbid\") are unchanged: no base_seq/base_prev_hash/base_count/superseded_head_hash/snapshot_hash fields are added, so the A1 breaking-migration problem (SpineTailer._resolve_head swallow at tail.py:118 vs verify_checkpoint crash at checkpoint.py:108) never arises. No schema_version bump. sign_head/verify_head/verify_chain/classify_head (chain.py:37-80, checkpoint.py:87-101) are byte-identical, and re-used verbatim. Because retain-all keeps entry_count == the true absolute physical record count and keeps entries[0].prev_hash == _GENESIS_PREV, classify_head's n<signed_n gate (checkpoint.py:93) and verify_chain's genesis-prefix seed (chain.py:38) are fed exactly what they are fed today. checkpoint() at consolidate/pipeline.py:97 re-signs over the full segment-spanning entries() with n==entry_count, so classify_head never false-trips. Old mobile clients / `sigil verify` / mcp ingest_status verify the same head format they do now.",
          "spinestore_api_changes": "Public contract (return types and values) stays byte-identical; only the internal backing becomes segment-spanning. iter_records(since_seq=-1): concatenates segments in id order (decompressing gz on the fly), seeking to the segment whose [first_seq,last_seq] contains since_seq+1 via the manifest, then streaming across boundaries; yields the same contiguous genesis->tip SpineRecord stream (store.py:197-218 generalized). get(seq): segment-aware index -> (segment_id, offset) -> open/decompress that segment -> seek/scan -> same SpineRecord|None (store.py:220-242); retain-all means EVERY historical seq resolves (no 404 for anything that ever existed). tail(n): seek-from-end of the active segment then walk backwards into prior sealed segments until n collected -- FIXES B3 (today store.py:244-268 only reads self.path and returns <n after rotation). count(): sum(manifest sealed.records) + live-scan(active); identical physical-count semantics as store.py:274-275. entries()/verify(): built on iter_records, so automatically feed ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain to verify_chain (store.py:278-303) -- byte-identical verdict. next_seq: self._last.seq+1 but self._last re-resolved on a manifest-generation change (A3). append(...): identical signature/return; internally resolves the active segment under the stable lock, checks the rotation trigger, seals+rotates if needed, then derives the entry from the true tip and writes+fsyncs to the active segment. NO new public methods are strictly required (retain-all makes a 'compacted-below' response unnecessary); get() honestly resolves all seqs. reuse.* (canonical/chain/models) and SpineRecord are untouched.",
          "storage_layout": "Under ~/.sigil/spine/ (config.py:52-55): manifest.json (authoritative segment set + tip; the sole linearization point), spine.lock (path-stable zero-byte lockfile, never renamed/unlinked), head.json (UNCHANGED, single signed head), keys/ (UNCHANGED), segments/ (new sealed segments seg-{id:08d}.jsonl[.gz]), archive/ (optional cold-move target, may be a different mount). The current 93 MB spine.jsonl becomes segment-0 with ZERO byte movement: on first open with no manifest, synthesize a manifest naming the existing spine.jsonl as segment id 0, state=active, first_seq=0, last_seq=T, tip=(T,H,...) read from its own tail via _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83-121). No rename, no rewrite. On first rotation seg-0 (spine.jsonl) stays in place, is marked sealed in the manifest, and the new active becomes segments/seg-00000001.jsonl (created empty); the 93 MB file is never moved, only later gzipped in place to spine.jsonl.gz (lock-free) and its manifest path/codec flipped. Each segment records an explicit path so segment 0 keeps its legacy name while new segments use seg-NNN. The active segment is always uncompressed and always the highest id.",
          "manifest_and_lockfile": "MANIFEST (manifest.json, written via checkpoint._atomic_write_text pattern: temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync, checkpoint.py:33-60). Fields: schema_version:int; generation:int (monotonic; THE index/epoch token for invariant 9/A3/A4); created_ts (informational); scope; tip:{seq,prev_hash,cert_digest,entry_hash} (the global chain tip as of last cutover, for invariant-13 next_seq when the active segment is freshly empty); active_id:int; segments:[{id, path, state:active|sealed, codec:none|gzip, location:local|archive, first_seq, last_seq, first_prev_hash, last_entry_hash, records, bytes}]. Segments are strictly ordered and contiguous: segments[i].first_seq == segments[i-1].last_seq+1, and segments[i].first_prev_hash == segments[i-1].last_entry_hash (the carried seam). NOT signed: an unsigned manifest cannot cause an undetected tamper because any set manipulation is already caught by the existing verify path -- dropping the front segment fails verify_chain's genesis check (chain.py:40), dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks prev_hash linkage + seq contiguity at the seam (chain.py:41,44), and dropping the tail segment fails the signed head's entry_count/last_seq/head_hash (checkpoint.py:93, chain.py:73). The manifest's boundary hashes are advisory (index/fast-path only); full verify() recomputes every hash from the actual segment bytes, so a doctored manifest field at worst fail-closes. Signing the manifest is an explicit deferred option, not needed for tamper-evidence. LOCKFILE (spine.lock, invariant 14/D3): a zero-byte file created once (O_CREAT), NEVER os.replace'd/renamed/unlinked. Both append and rotation: (1) acquire the in-process RLock keyed on the STABLE lock path (spine_lock, store.py:37-47, keyed on spine.lock not the rotating active segment); (2) flock(spine.lock fd, LOCK_EX) -- cross-process exclusion bound to a stable inode, so it is sound across os.replace (fixes the flock-binds-to-inode-not-path hazard, D3); (3) while holding both, read manifest -> resolve active -> do tip-read/truncate/append or the cutover; (4) release. SpineStore.path stays a STABLE identity/lock token (= the canonical spine.jsonl path) so envelope.py:129 spine_lock(store.path) needs no edit even though the physical active target rotates.",
          "cutover_algorithm": "Trigger: at the top of append(), under the lock, if the active segment size/count exceeds threshold. BOUNDED LOCKED CRITICAL SECTION (all O(1)-metadata / O(tail), never an O(file) copy, so a kill-switch PANIC append is not blocked -- D1): R1 acquire spine_lock RLock + flock(spine.lock). R2 seal-time torn-tail truncate on the ACTIVE segment: clean_end,tip=_last_valid_boundary(active) (store.py:83-121); if size>clean_end: truncate; fsync fd (A2 -- the only chance to clean a sealed segment, since the append-path truncate never revisits it). R3 read tip=(T,H,prev,cert) from the clean boundary. R4 create fresh empty next segment segments/seg-{active_id+1:08d}.jsonl via O_CREAT|O_EXCL; fsync fd; fsync segments/ dir. (The current manifest-referenced active segment is NEVER renamed or removed -- this is what eliminates the false-truncation crash window.) R5 build manifest gen+1: old active flipped state=sealed with records/bytes/first_prev_hash/last_entry_hash=H filled in, new segment state=active first_seq=T+1 first_prev_hash=H, active_id=+1, tip=(T,H,...). Write manifest.json.tmp; fsync. R6 os.replace(manifest.json.tmp, manifest.json) <-- THE atomic cutover instant; fsync spine dir. R7 release locks. LOCK-FREE POST-PROCESSING (O(file), no lock, on an IMMUTABLE sealed segment -- D1): P1 gzip sealed segment -> .gz.tmp, fsync, os.replace to .gz, dir-fsync. P2 brief-locked manifest update flipping that segment codec=gzip/path=.gz. P3 after a READER-GRACE (D2: readers snapshot ALL segment fds immediately after reading the manifest; unlink deferred behind a refcount/grace timer) unlink the plaintext. P4 optional MOVE .gz to archive/ (fsync both dirs), brief-locked manifest location=archive flip, reader-grace, done. CRASH-CONSISTENCY: crash before R6 = pre-rotation manifest gen G intact, active segment intact with [S..T], orphan empty next-segment ignored (not in manifest G) and GC'd on next open -> zero loss, no false truncation. Crash after R6 = post-rotation set consistent. HEAD coupling: no head swap is part of the cutover (entry_count stays absolute, so entries() still equals the signed count) -- there is NO head/manifest crash-consistency window to manage, unlike the prune case. RECOVERY at open: if an orphan seg-{len(sealed):08d} exists that is not in the manifest, delete it (roll-back) -- deterministic and idempotent.",
          "index_changes": "Segment-aware + epoch-invalidated (invariant 9, A3, A4). (1) _offsets becomes dict[int, tuple[int,int]] mapping seq -> (segment_id, byte_offset_within_segment). Sealed segments are immutable -> indexed once (for gz/archived segments, get() decompresses the one owning segment (bounded to the seal threshold, a few MB) and linear-scans rather than byte-offset-seeking -- avoids gz random-access complexity; deep-history get() is rare). The active segment keeps the current O(1) byte-offset index (store.py:130-138,187-192,353-382). (2) IDENTITY/EPOCH invalidation replaces size-only (fixes the store.py:335,345-347 size-only heuristic): the store caches manifest.generation + active (st_dev,st_ino,size). _check_epoch() runs before any indexed read: if manifest.generation changed OR active (dev,ino) changed -> REBIND (re-resolve segments, invalidate active offsets, reset self._last for A3, reset active scan pos); elif active size grew -> EXTEND (append-only within active, offsets never move); elif active size shrank WITHOUT a generation change -> genuine truncation attack -> REBUILD + surface (stays detectable exactly as today, because rotation ALWAYS bumps generation and so never masquerades as an in-place shrink). (3) A4 UNIFICATION: a single SpineStore.epoch() -> (generation, active_dev, active_ino, active_size). killswitch.py:57 (verdict-cache key) and gesture/session.py:121 (kill-switch growth detector) replace their raw store.path.stat().st_size with store.epoch(); the store's own _ensure_index uses the same token. One shared definition across all three size-as-change-token sites, so a same-size rotation changes generation -> epoch differs -> a stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction is impossible.",
          "rotation_trigger": "Checked at the top of append() under the lock (cheap fstat + manifest.records): seal the active segment when active size >= SPINE_SEGMENT_MAX_BYTES (default 32 MiB) OR active record-count >= SPINE_SEGMENT_MAX_RECORDS (default 50,000), whichever first. Plus a MANUAL trigger (`sigil spine rotate` / store.rotate()) and an opportunistic hook at checkpoint() time (rotate-then-sign, so a freshly sealed segment is immediately covered by the new signed head). Default policy: size-based 32 MiB -> the existing 93 MB spine yields ~3 sealed segments after the first run. Thresholds are configurable in config.py; set to infinity to disable rotation entirely (byte-identical passthrough to today).",
          "consumer_transparency": "All 34 keep working because the SpineStore read API presents ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain spanning all segments (invariant 12).
