The 9 old-seq resolvers (actor_gate get(seq)+iter L22/27, actor L193/203, operator L241/391, perception/egress L34/39, bridge/server /api/record L322, ui/server /api/record L161, mcp episodic_range L88, consolidate/gate.py:93) resolve ANY historical seq because retain-all keeps every seq present (archived -> decompress/fetch); no 404 for any record that ever existed, honoring the cite-its-seq contract. EDITS REQUIRED (they reach PAST the read API): killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 -> store.epoch() (A4); cli.py:22 SPINE_PATH.unlink on --reset -> SpineStore.reset() clearing all segments+manifest+lockfile+head. bridge/daemon.py tail(2048) needs no edit (tail() is fixed inside the store, B3). transcript.py:97/102 parent_id references stay resolvable (retain-all). Everything else: zero edits.",
          "c1_handling": "C1 does not arise. The durable non-replayable anti-rollback floor is only NEEDED when hard-pruning turns classify_head's n<signed_n truncation alarm (checkpoint.py:93) into a no-op for a removed range. Retain-all removes no record from the logical chain: entries() still yields all N records, entry_count stays the true absolute physical count, and n<signed_n remains a LIVE guard exactly as today. Replaying an old (short-spine, low-count-head) pair is still caught precisely because the current spine remains longer/higher-count than that old head -- byte-identical to today's behavior. So the anti-rollback floor stays exactly where it is now: IMPLICITLY in the invariant (physical chain length >= signed entry_count) that retain-all preserves, plus the persisted signed head's last_seq/head_hash and SpineTailer's monotonic high-water (tail.py:60,89-93). No per-verifier persisted floor and no authenticated head-delivery channel are introduced or needed, because no new rollback surface is opened. A fresh/cold verifier judges a compacted store identically to how it judges the store today.",
          "un_prunable_state_handling": "Untouched. Because retain-all keeps every record and every consumer's full scan spans all segments via iter_records(), NONE of the ~9 monotonic state-bearer classes need folding into any snapshot: per-device nonce high-water (envelope.py:92-104), newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py:283), account.create creation-cap counts (actor_scope.py:49, actor.py:139/247), latest device authorize/revoke per key (mesh/registry.py:66-76), kill-switch latch (killswitch _scan_engaged:75), standing promotion grants (promotion.py:48), unresolved queued approvals (approvals.py:62), archivist supersession chain + promotion-key ledger (revise.py:16-25), and open threads/commitments/contradictions + their source_seq/verified_seq citations (consolidate/queries.py, B1). The ONLY obligation is that their scans reach every segment -- guaranteed since they all route through the segment-spanning store.iter_records(). No snapshot record, no state schema, no fold logic. This is the central safety win of the conservative path.",
          "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "At seal (rotation step R2, before the segment is marked sealed and while it is still the active target under the lock), run _last_valid_boundary on it (store.py:83-121) and truncate(clean_end) any torn tail, then fsync. This guarantees a sealed segment ends exactly on a valid newline-terminated record; since no future append ever revisits a sealed file, and iter_records/_scan_from SKIP a torn line (store.py:209-216,372-378) turning it into a fatal verify_chain seq gap (chain.py:44), the seal-time truncate is the only chance to keep it clean -- this is precisely the A2 fix (the append-path BLOCK-1 truncate at store.py:168-171 only ever protects the active segment). The clean boundary yields the tip entry (seq T, entry_hash H, prev_hash, cert_digest), written into manifest.tip AND into the sealed segment's last_seq/last_entry_hash AND into the next segment's first_seq=T+1/first_prev_hash=H. The fresh active segment starts empty; its first append derives seq=T+1, prev_hash=H from manifest.tip (invariant 13), so verify_chain's strict seq contiguity + prev_hash linkage never break at the seam (chain.py:41,44). gzip is byte-preserving, so hashes recomputed after decompress are identical -> verify() unaffected by compression.",
          "slice_plan": [
            "Slice 0 -- Manifest + stable lockfile + zero-touch migration (rotation OFF/passthrough): introduce manifest.json + spine.lock, route the store's single active segment through the manifest indirection, move the RLock+flock to spine.lock (keyed on the stable path). On first open with no manifest, synthesize one naming the existing spine.jsonl as sole active segment (zero data touch). Golden test: verify()/entries()/count()/get()/iter_records()/tail()/next_seq byte-identical over the real 93 MB spine. Pure plumbing; behaves exactly as today.",
            "Slice 1 -- Segment-spanning read API + segment-aware epoch index: iter_records/get/tail/count/entries span an ordered multi-segment set; index keyed (segment_id,offset); invalidate on manifest generation / active dev-ino change (invariant 9, A3); add store.epoch(). Exercised by tests that hand-craft a 2-segment manifest; byte-identical outputs on the real spine.",
            "Slice 2 -- A4 unification: migrate killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 to store.epoch(); add SpineStore.reset() and switch cli --reset (cli.py:22) to it. No rotation yet; the size-only sites are gone. Independently reviewable security fix.",
            "Slice 3 -- Crash-safe rotation cutover, UNCOMPRESSED: bounded locked critical section (seal-time torn-tail A2, create next segment, atomic manifest swap, carry tip across seam), the open-time recovery/GC of an interrupted rotation, and the trigger policy. Crash tests (kill -9 at each fsync/replace barrier). Growth is now segmented; disk not yet reclaimed.",
            "Slice 4 -- gzip-on-seal (lock-free) + manifest path/codec flip + reader-grace unlink (D2): compress sealed segments off the lock, flip codec via a brief-locked manifest update, defer plaintext unlink behind a reader refcount/grace. This is where disk is actually reclaimed; get()/iter_records transparently decompress.",
            "Slice 5 -- Cold-archive MOVE + honest deep-read: optional location=archive move of old sealed .gz to a separate mount, decompress/fetch-on-read, `sigil spine status|archive`, retention/report. Deep-history reads pull from archive."
          ],
          "risks": [
            "invariant 13 (durable tip -> next_seq): the freshly-empty active segment must resolve its tip from manifest.tip, not from build_chain-at-genesis; a bug here would reset next_seq to 0 and reuse a live seq -> chain fork. Mitigation: tip = (active tail read if non-empty else manifest.tip); heavy test around the first append after a rotation.",
            "invariant 14 / D3 (path-stable lock migration): moving the lock from the target file to spine.lock is the single highest-risk durability change and every ~15 append site inherits it. Mitigation: Slice 0 does ONLY this, with a cross-process append-vs-rotate race test and a re-validate-active-under-lock check.",
            "invariant 9 / A3 / A4 (epoch index): rotation MUST bump generation so it never reads as an in-place shrink (which must stay a detectable truncation attack). Residual: a bug that failed to bump generation would let a rotation mask a truncation. Mitigation: the generation bump and the manifest swap are the same atomic write; the shrink-without-generation-change -> REBUILD+flag path is retained.",
            "A2 (seal-time torn tail): if seal forgets the truncate, a sealed segment ending torn is a permanent false verify() gap that no future append can fix. Mitigation: the seal-time _last_valid_boundary truncate is R2, the first step of the critical section, with a crash-during-rotation test.",
            "D2 (reader vs unlink): a very long-running lock-free reader can pin a plaintext segment so its disk is not reclaimed until it finishes. Bounded and acceptable (correctness preserved via fd snapshot + grace); worst case is delayed reclaim, never a torn read.",
            "verify() I/O cost grows once segments are archived/compressed (it must fetch+decompress every segment). Acceptable: verify is off the hot path (sigil verify / ingest_status), not per-append; note it in the runbook."
          ],
          "invariants_at_risk": [
            "invariant 13 (durable-tip-determines-next_seq): most stressed -- the empty-active-after-rotation case must read the tip from manifest.tip or it forks the chain. Residual risk LOW with tip=max(active-tail, manifest.tip) and a dedicated first-append-post-rotation test.",
            "invariant 14 + D3 (cross-writer serialization / path-stable lock): the flock must move to spine.lock because os.replace of the manifest and rotating active files makes flock-on-the-target inode-unsound. Residual risk MEDIUM until Slice 0's race test passes; this is the highest-risk change.",
            "invariant 9 + A3 + A4 (index correctness under external mutation / epoch invalidation): size-only detection must become generation+dev/ino based, unified across store/killswitch/session. Residual risk LOW-MEDIUM: rotation always bumps generation so a real shrink stays a detectable truncation attack.",
            "A2 (sealed-segment torn-tail): seal-time truncate is the only cleanup path for a sealed file. Residual risk LOW with R2 ordering + crash test.",
            "invariant 12 (one contiguous genesis chain across seams): a boundary gap/dup or wrong segment order would false-TAMPER; mitigated because ordering rests on prev_hash linkage + seq contiguity (chain.py:41,44) recomputed from bytes, not on a directory glob. Residual risk LOW.",
            "D1 (append lock not held across heavy copy): satisfied by design (gzip is lock-free on an immutable segment, locked section is O(1)); residual risk LOW -- only the O(tail) seal-time fstat/truncate runs under the lock."
          ]
        },
        "verdicts": [
          {
            "design_name": "RETAIN-ALL SEGMENT ROTATION (gzip-on-seal + optional cold-archive, no prune, no C1, no head-schema change)",
            "invariant_preservation": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "The retain-all CHOICE makes the load-bearing invariants trivially safe: seq immutability (inv 3 - nothing renumbered, segment-0 keeps seqs 0..T verbatim), absolute entry_count (inv 4 - entries() spans all segments = full N, so classify_head's n<signed_n gate at checkpoint.py:93 is fed exactly today's values and never false-trips), genesis-rooting (inv 1 - the front segment is never dropped, so entries()[0].prev_hash stays _GENESIS_PREV for chain.py:38-40), monotonic-last_seq (inv 5), referential integrity (inv 16), and the ~9 monotonic state-bearers (inv 15 - reconstructed over the complete record set because scans span segments, no folding needed). Critically, a rotation is structurally distinguishable from a rollback: tip seq and count only ever GROW, so the 'prune reads as truncation' conflict never arises. The hard cases are correctly identified and handled at design level: inv 13/19 (empty-active-after-rotation must derive next_seq from manifest.tip, not build_chain-at-genesis - note _read_last_entry at store.py:308 currently reads _last_nonempty_line(self.path)=segment-0's tail=seq T, which would freeze next_seq and cause massive seq reuse unless reworked to consult the manifest active/tip); inv 14/D3 (flock moved to a path-stable spine.lock because flock binds to inode not path across os.replace); inv 9/A3/A4 (size-only epoch replaced by generation+dev/ino, unified across store/killswitch:57/session:121 - actually STRICTER than today, which misses an equal-size os.replace). I dock one point because two FAILURE-MODE seams are left unspecified and are concrete silent-break paths: (a) after Slice-4 gzips segment 0 and unlinks spine.jsonl, every self.path.exists() guard (store.py:203,222,251,308,335) reads the spine as EMPTY unless every site is generalized - inv 12/13/19; (b) the design never states that a manifest-referenced-but-unreadable segment must RAISE, so a naive skip-missing yields a shorter chain that verify() fails-closed on (seq gap, safe) but non-verifying state-scanners under-scan (inv 15, unsafe - see fatal flaws)."
            },
            "consumer_transparency": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "Strong: all 34 consumers keep working via a segment-spanning read API presenting ONE contiguous genesis->tip chain (inv 12), with only ~3 edits (killswitch:57 and gesture/session:121 to store.epoch() for A4, cli --reset to SpineStore.reset()). tail() is actively FIXED (today store.py:244-268 only reads self.path and returns <n after rotation - B3). The 9 old-seq resolvers keep working because retain-all keeps every seq present. Two real transparency gaps keep this off a 5: (1) the self.path-as-data-file landmine - self.path is simultaneously the stable lock/identity token AND segment-0's plaintext, which Slice 4 unlinks; if any read method's self.path.exists() guard isn't generalized, the API silently returns zero records to all 34 consumers, and the Slice-0 passthrough golden test CANNOT catch it because segment 0 isn't unlinked until Slice 4; (2) in the Slice-5 archive tier, get(archived_seq) with the cold mount offline returns None - a bare 404 for a record that provably exists, which is exactly the dishonest 'bare 404' the brief's question 5 warns against, and it conflates 'temporarily unavailable' with 'never existed'. Also every frequent full-scanner (nonce gate, killswitch latch) now decompresses gzipped segments on each scan - a perf regression, not a correctness one, but it widens the surface."
            },
            "durability_crash_safety": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "The strongest dimension. The cutover is exemplary and mirrors checkpoint._atomic_write_text (checkpoint.py:33-60): write+fsync the new EMPTY next-segment (R4), write+fsync manifest.tmp (R5), os.replace as the single atomic instant (R6), dir-fsync, and only lock-free post-processing (gzip P1+) touches immutable sealed bytes. Crash analysis is loss-free at every barrier: crash before R6 leaves gen G intact with the active segment intact and the orphan empty next-segment GC'd on open; crash after R6 is consistent; because the new active is EMPTY at cutover, no acked record ever lives only in an unreferenced segment. The decisive durability WIN over hard-prune: entry_count stays absolute so NO head swap is part of the cutover, eliminating the head/manifest dual-swap crash window that a prune design must manage (inv 18). D1 is genuinely satisfied - the locked section is O(window) seal-truncate (_last_valid_boundary reads an 8KB tail, store.py:83-121) + O(1) manifest write + O(1) record write; the O(file) gzip is lock-free on an immutable segment, so a kill-switch PANIC append is never blocked. D3 is fixed correctly by binding flock to the never-replaced spine.lock inode. Acked-append fsync (store.py:183) and seal-time truncate+fsync (A2, the only cleanup path for a sealed torn tail per inv 7) are preserved. Minor nit: the triggering record's write must be explicitly inside the locked section after R6, and segments/ dir-fsync after O_CREAT|O_EXCL must not be dropped - both stated but easy to miss."
            },
            "tamper_evidence_integrity": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "The tamper model is literally untouched and reused verbatim: verify_chain/verify_head/sign_head/classify_head (chain.py:37-80, checkpoint.py:87-101) are byte-identical, and SignedChainHead/ChainEntry (reuse/models.py:47-64, extra='forbid') gain no fields - so the A1 breaking-migration problem (SpineTailer swallow at tail.py:118 vs verify_checkpoint crash at checkpoint.py:108) never arises. C1 opens NO new rollback surface: a matched old-manifest+old-head replay is isomorphic to today's old-spine+old-head replay (both need a validly-signed old head to pass classify_head; a manifest-only rollback is caught because the current head anchors N > the rolled-back count, n<signed_n -> TAMPERING). C2 does not arise: verify() re-hashes EVERY record from actual segment bytes (including decompressed gzip, which is byte-preserving), so there is no committed-but-not-re-hashed prefix. Leaving the manifest UNSIGNED is sound: dropping the front segment fails the genesis check (chain.py:40), dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks prev_hash linkage + seq contiguity at the seam (chain.py:41,44), dropping the tail fails the signed head (checkpoint.py:93, chain.py:73) - all recomputed from bytes, manifest fields advisory-only, so a doctored manifest fails closed. A same-size rotation bumps generation so a stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction is impossible (A4). One honesty nit only: the c1_handling sub-claim that an old low-count-head replay is 'still caught' over-implies protection that does not exist even today when the head is ALSO rolled back - but the net 'no regression vs today' is correct. The fail-open state-scanner risk (see flaws) is NOT a tamper-evidence gap: verify()/classify_head fail-closed on the resulting seq gap."
            },
            "implementability_blast_radius": {
              "score": 3,
              "rationale": "Implementable and honestly staged (6 slices, each independently testable, highest-risk lock migration isolated in Slice 0), but the blast radius INSIDE SpineStore is large and several landmines sit exactly where tests are weakest. Every read method is reworked to be manifest/segment-aware (iter_records, get, tail, count, entries, next_seq, the epoch index, _read_last_entry, append's tip resolution), the RLock+flock moves to spine.lock across ~15 append sites, and self.path must be fully decoupled from segment-0's data file (audit every self.path.{exists,stat,open}). Three under-tested hazards: (1) the Slice-0 golden test is passthrough and cannot exercise the segment-0-gzipped-unlinked case that only appears in Slice 4 - the exact case that would silently empty the read API; (2) missing/offline-segment failure semantics are unspecified (fail-open risk for state-scanners); (3) frequent full-scanners decompress every scan. The design correctly rates the lock migration MEDIUM and inv-13 tip resolution as top risks. It is careful surgery on the single most safety-critical file in the system, with a couple of landmines in the failure paths - hence a middling score despite a clean architecture."
            },
            "fatal_flaws": [
              "SELF.PATH-EMPTINESS LANDMINE (inv 12/13/19): self.path (=spine.jsonl) is simultaneously the stable lock/identity token AND segment-0's plaintext data file, which Slice 4 gzips to spine.jsonl.gz and then unlinks after reader-grace. Every store method still guarding on self.path.exists() (iter_records store.py:203, get :222, tail :251, _read_last_entry :308, _ensure_index :335) then treats the spine as EMPTY -> the read API silently returns zero records to all 34 consumers and next_seq resets toward genesis (seq reuse / chain fork). The design SAYS these are 'generalized' but the Slice-0 passthrough golden test cannot catch a miss because segment 0 is not unlinked until Slice 4. Fixable by making the manifest the sole existence/emptiness authority + a Slice-4 golden test with segment-0 gzipped and its plaintext unlinked, but as SPECIFIED this silent-empty path is open.",
              "MISSING-SEGMENT FAIL-OPEN FOR STATE-SCANNERS (inv 15): the design never specifies that a manifest-referenced but unreadable segment (Slice-5 archive mount offline, or a corrupt/prematurely-unlinked local .gz) must RAISE. If iter_records degrades to skip-missing=empty, verify()/classify_head fail CLOSED on the resulting seq gap (safe), but the non-verifying monotonic state-bearers reconstruct over an incomplete record set and can fail OPEN - e.g. mesh/registry.py:66-76 misses a device REVOKE that lived in the unreachable segment and treats a revoked device as authorized, or killswitch _scan_engaged:75 misses the engage latch. This is the one path by which retain-all's central inv-15 safety claim ('scans span all segments') can be silently violated; it needs iter_records to treat the manifest as a hard contract (any referenced segment unreadable -> raise) plus a live-scan floor keeping any state-bearer's history out of the offline-capable archive tier."
            ],
            "weighted_verdict": "SHIPPABLE SAFELY for the CORE (Slices 0-4: manifest + path-stable lock + segment-spanning reads + epoch index + crash-safe rotation + gzip-on-seal), which reclaims the disk (93MB -> ~15-20MB) with ZERO change to the tamper model and every one of the 19 invariants + C1/C2 preserved - a strictly conservative win over hard-prune. Retain-all is the correct scope precisely because it needs NONE of {C1 durable floor, snapshot state-folding, breaking head-schema migration}: entry_count stays absolute, the chain stays genesis-rooted, and a rotation is structurally distinguishable from a rollback (tip and count only grow). The two heaviest-weighted dimensions for a purist - tamper-evidence and durability/crash-safety - are 5s, and the C1/C2 no-regression analysis holds under harsh scrutiny. The design is downgraded from a clean pass only by failure-mode SEAMS, not architecture: the self.path-emptiness landmine and the missing-segment fail-open are both real silent-break paths that the SPEC leaves implicit. Ship 0-4 only AFTER pinning those two semantics; treat Slice 5 (cold-archive) as a separate, later, off-by-default OPERATOR decision, since it turns an offline mount into a hard availability dependency for every full-scanner and is where the fail-open bites hardest. PRUNE remains out of scope and rightly so - that is where C1 + state-folding + a breaking head migration live.",
            "one_change_that_would_improve_it": "Make the manifest the SOLE authority for existence, emptiness, and the segment set, and make any divergence between the manifest and the readable bytes FAIL LOUD: (a) replace every self.path.exists()/stat() guard in store.py (:203,222,251,308,335) with a manifest-based check so a gzipped+unlinked segment-0 can never read as an empty spine, and derive next_seq/_last from manifest.tip (or the active tail), never from _last_nonempty_line(self.path); (b) make iter_records RAISE on any manifest-referenced segment that is unreadable (offline archive, corrupt .gz, prematurely-unlinked plaintext) rather than silently yielding a shorter chain; and (c) add a Slice-4 golden test that gzips segment 0, unlinks its plaintext, and asserts verify()/entries()/iter_records()/get()/next_seq are byte-identical. This single discipline closes BOTH surviving fatal flaws at once - the reads-go-silently-empty landmine (inv 12/13/19) and the state-bearer fail-open on an under-scan (inv 15) - which are the only paths by which this otherwise airtight retain-all design can silently break an invariant."

            "design_name": "RETAIN-ALL SEGMENT ROTATION with gzip-on-seal + cold-archive MOVE (no prune, no C1, no head-schema change)",

              "rationale": "The hard invariants hold under retain-all: seq stays immutable (no renumber), entry_count stays the true absolute physical count so classify_head's n<signed_n gate (checkpoint.py:93) is fed exactly today's value, entries()[0].prev_hash stays _GENESIS_PREV (chain.py:38-40), and the seam carries seq T->T+1 with prev_hash=H so verify_chain's contiguity+linkage (chain.py:41,44) never break — and crucially verify() recomputes every hash from segment BYTES (store.py:300-303), so the manifest's advisory boundary hashes can only fail-closed, never mask tamper. The one invariant that is genuinely load-bearing on implementation is #13/#14: append() MUST re-resolve the active segment from the manifest UNDER the flock on EVERY append and derive the entry from a fresh disk tip (as today's store.py:168 does via _last_valid_boundary), NEVER from cached self._last. The brief's A3 keeps self._last cached for next_seq and only re-resolves 'on a manifest-generation change' — if that cached tip ever leaks into the write path, or the epoch check has a gap, an append writes T+1' into the now-SEALED prior segment and forks the chain (two seq T+1) — an unrecoverable corruption of an immutable file. The friction is real but bounded: (a) store.path becomes a pure identity/RLock-key token that after gzip may not EXIST as a file, yet five methods short-circuit on self.path.exists() (iter_records:203, get:222, tail:251, _read_last_entry:308, _ensure_index:335) — every one must be de-pathed to the manifest or count()/entries() silently return empty -> classify_head reads n=0<signed_n -> FALSE TAMPERING; (b) the A4 store.epoch() unification must land at killswitch.py:56 (which stats store.path.st_size and keys on store.path.resolve()) and gesture/session.py:121, or a same-size rotation serves a stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction. Both are handled in the plan; the blast radius is the deduction."

            "durability_crash_safety": {
              "score": 3,
              "rationale": "The spine of the cutover is sound and I could NOT construct a crash point or reader/appender race that loses or double-counts an ACKED record, nor one that blocks a CONCURRENT kill-switch panic. Data-before-pointer ordering is correct (R4 fsyncs the new segment+its dir before R6 commits the manifest via _atomic_write_text's temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync); a crash before R6 leaves gen G intact with the orphan next-segment GC'd; a crash after R6 with the triggering record unwritten loses only a NOT-yet-acked append; and — the design's best durability property — retain-all removes the head/manifest crash-consistency window entirely, because rotation never changes what entries() returns, so a crash between rotate-commit and any head re-sign is benign-stale (n>=signed_n), never a false-TAMPER (this is exactly what dooms prune designs and is avoided here). A reader racing the cutover sees a consistent [0..T] under either the pre- or post-rotation manifest. HOWEVER several medium concurrency defects survive: (1) rotate-at-TOP-of-append front-loads R2-R6 (a torn-tail fstat/truncate+fsync, a create+fsync+dir-fsync, a manifest write+fsync+replace+dir-fsync) AHEAD of the triggering record's own write+fsync — when the triggering append IS the panic, its pre-durability window is materially longer than today's (BLOCK-1 truncate -> write -> fsync), so a crash mid-rotation drops a panic the operator believes they fired; write-then-rotate would eliminate this at zero cost. (2) R4's O_CREAT|O_EXCL of seg-N+1 collides with an orphan left by a peer process that crashed mid-rotation (SIGIL runs multiple appenders: bridge, gesture daemon, envelope) if that orphan is only GC'd 'at open' and the surviving long-lived process never re-opens -> append raises -> a stuck/failing panic append until someone re-opens; rotation must GC a not-in-manifest orphan next-segment INLINE, not only at open. (3) D2's reader-vs-unlink 'grace timer' is best-effort, not a correctness mechanism: a reader that read the pre-P2 manifest (names plaintext) then stalls (GC/swap) past the grace before opening its fd hits ENOENT after P3 unlinks — POSIX makes an already-open fd safe, but the read-manifest->open gap is unprotected; without a manifest-reread-on-ENOENT retry this yields a loud read error or a skipped-segment false-TAMPER (not a silent acked loss). (4) 'cold-archive MOVE' to a separate mount cannot be os.rename (EXDEV); it must be copy->fsync->manifest-flip(commit)->grace->unlink-source or a crash mid-copy is mishandled. None fatal, but four real gaps keep this at a 3."
            },

C1 genuinely does not apply — retain-all removes no record from the logical chain, so n<signed_n stays a LIVE anti-rollback guard exactly as today and no durable external floor is needed.             "weighted_verdict": "SHIPPABLE for its full retain-all scope, and materially safer than any prune variant — but only after fixing one spec contradiction and closing three medium concurrency gaps. The design passes the hardest durability tests I could throw at it: no crash point or reader/appender interleaving I constructed loses or double-counts an ACKED record, and no concurrent kill-switch panic is blocked (the O(file) gzip/MOVE is genuinely lock-free on an immutable segment; only bounded O(1)/O(tail) work runs under the flock). Its structural win is real and load-bearing — by keeping entry_count absolute and the chain genesis-rooted, retain-all deletes the head/manifest crash-consistency window and the C1 anti-rollback-floor problem outright, which is precisely where prune designs become unsafe. The tamper model is untouched (5/5) and consumer transparency is strong (4/5). What holds it back to a durability 3/5 is a cluster of fixable-but-real concurrency defects: (1) the RLock-key contradiction above [must-fix before Slice 0 merges]; (2) rotate-at-top-of-append lengthens the panic's pre-durability window vs today — write+fsync+ack the triggering record to the current active FIRST, THEN rotate; (3) rotation's O_EXCL on the next segment must GC a peer-crash orphan INLINE, not only at open, or a multi-process crash can stick a panic append; (4) the D2 reader grace must be backed by a manifest-reread-on-ENOENT retry, since a timer is best-effort, not correctness; and (5) cross-mount archive MOVE must be copy->fsync->manifest-commit->grace->unlink (os.rename EXDEVs). None are fatal; all are localized. Ship it slice-by-slice with these fixes and a cross-process append-vs-rotate race test as the Slice-0 gate.",
            "one_change_that_would_improve_it": "Reorder the triggering append to WRITE-THEN-ROTATE: under the lock, write+fsync+ack the incoming record to the CURRENT active segment first (letting it transiently exceed the soft threshold by one record — harmless), and only then perform the seal+cutover. This single change makes a crash during rotation provably unable to affect ANY in-flight append — including a kill-switch panic — because the record is already durable and acked before rotation begins; it removes the panic-durability-window regression that rotate-first introduces, and it means the seal always operates on a segment whose tip is already committed, simplifying the seam-tip derivation (manifest.tip == the record just fsync'd) and eliminating the empty-active-after-rotation edge that most stresses invariant 13.\""

          "one_line": "Rotate the single 93MB spine.jsonl into hash-linked segments, gzip-on-seal, then hard-prune whole sealed segments below an owner-signed snapshot whose base_prev_hash is the dropped boundary's entry_hash (a chain accumulator over all pruned records) plus a signed Merkle root; entry_count stays ABSOLUTE (base_count+live) so tail-truncation is still caught, a durable external floor.json + head meta-chain close anti-rollback for warm/paired verifiers, and the ~9 monotonic state-bearers seed from a folded-state snapshot record.",
          "retains_all_records": false,
          "reclaims_disk_how": "Two stacked wins. (1) gzip-on-seal (retain-all, no tamper-model change): each sealed segment is gzip-compressed in place; JSONL spine compresses ~5-10x, so 93MB retained -> ~12-18MB, reads stay transparent via a gzip-aware segment reader. This alone bounds per-file size + read latency and is the safe stopping point (slices 0-2). (2) Hard-prune (aggressive, operator-gated): whole sealed segments whose seq_hi < baseline K are UNLINKED after the cutover. The pruned records' bytes are removed from hot disk entirely; the archive of dropped segments is handled per operator flag --archive={cold,delete,keep} (default cold): `cold` moves snap-K-<gen>.jsonl.gz off the hot mount (keeps membership-proof material), `delete` hard-deletes it (max reclaim; you keep the signed merkle_root so a proof someone ELSE presents is still verifiable, but you can no longer reconstruct pruned records), `keep` leaves it hot (audit-heavy). Net after aggressive prune: hot disk ~= the retention window only (e.g. 20k records ~= 40MB raw / ~6MB gzipped) with zero pruned bytes on the hot path.           "touches_tamper_model": true,
          "head_schema_change": "YES — SignedChainHead -> schema_version=2, adding six fields with never-pruned defaults so a v1 head still validates under the v2 model: base_seq:int=0, base_prev_hash:str=_GENESIS_PREV, base_count:int=0, merkle_root:str=\"\", snapshot_seq:int=-1, prev_head_hash:str=\"\" (the meta-chain link = the immediately-prior accepted head_hash). model_config stays extra=\"forbid\" (defaults make old heads forward-valid; new heads are not backward-valid). REDEFINED semantics, all ABSOLUTE: last_seq = absolute tip seq (front-prune leaves it unchanged), entry_count = base_count + len(live entries) = absolute total records EVER (stays monotonic; this is what preserves invariant 4 as a real anti-truncation gate), head_hash = live tip entry_hash (= base_prev_hash if live is empty). sign_head is refactored to sign_head_fields(head_hash,last_seq,entry_count,base_seq,base_prev_hash,base_count,merkle_root,snapshot_seq,prev_head_hash,...) so the cutover never rescans O(file) to build a head. Migration (A1): (phase 1 / Slice 0) deploy schema-v2-capable code EVERYWHERE while still emitting effectively-v1 heads (all new fields at defaults) — the new model reads every existing v1 head unchanged, so nothing breaks and nothing prunes; harden the two divergent failure sites — checkpoint.verify_checkpoint gets a try/except around model_validate_json (today it crashes `sigil verify` + mcp ingest_status on an unparseable head, checkpoint.py:108) returning (False,'head unreadable — upgrade sigil'), and SpineTailer._resolve_head (tail.py:113-119, today silently swallows -> un-anchored) gates on head.schema_version<=MAX_SUPPORTED and surfaces a DISTINCT loud 'head schema too new — upgrade' state instead of silent un-anchored. A verifier rejects head.schema_version>MAX_SUPPORTED fail-closed rather than crashing. (phase 2) the FIRST prune emits a head with base_count>0; the compactor REFUSES to prune until every known device pubkey has checked in running a v2-capable version (recorded on-spine) OR the owner explicitly overrides — because old binaries cannot be retrofitted (they model_validate_json before they can read schema_version), the prune is a genuine flag day gated on fleet upgrade.",
          "spinestore_api_changes": "Constructor SpineStore(path=SPINE_PATH) unchanged; path.parent becomes SPINE_DIR; if no manifest.json exists the store treats the single file as an implicit one-segment manifest (base=genesis) so every existing deployment is byte-identical until rotation is enabled (default-OFF). Deltas: (1) iter_records(since_seq) concatenates manifest segments in seq order; since_seq<0 => byte0 of segment[0] through active EOF spanning ALL segments; since_seq>=0 => (segment,offset) index seek. Presents ONE contiguous base_prev-anchored chain; BYTE-IDENTICAL yield for a single-segment store. (2) get(seq) segment-aware index; UNCHANGED signature/return (SpineRecord|None) for back-compat, but seq<base_seq returns None. NEW resolve(seq)->SpineRecord|PrunedRef|None where PrunedRef={pruned:True, base_seq, snapshot_seq, archive_ref} — the honest Q5 answer; UI/bridge/mcp switch to resolve(). (3) tail(n) walks segments backward active->sealed until n collected (B3 fix; today seeks only self.path). (4) count() = physical RETAINED count spanning segments; NEW logical_count()=base_count+retained (=head.entry_count absolute total); mcp ingest_status reports 'retained (of logical total; base_count compacted)'. (6) verify() calls verify_chain(entries, genesis_prev=manifest.base_prev_hash) and cross-checks manifest.base_prev_hash==head.base_prev_hash; honest msg 'N retained link cleanly from base seq K (M compacted, committed by merkle_root ...)'. (7) next_seq from ACTIVE segment tip (invariant 13), invalidated on generation change (A3). (8) append() resolves active segment from manifest UNDER spine.lock, re-validates dev/ino, torn-tail-truncate on the ACTIVE segment only, seeds seq/prev from the sealed boundary when the active segment is empty.           "storage_layout": "$SIGIL_HOME/spine/ (parent of today's spine.jsonl):\n  spine.jsonl                     # THE ORIGINAL 93MB FILE, bytes untouched; becomes segment id=0 with zero rewrite — migration only writes a manifest naming it {id:0,path:'spine.jsonl',seq_lo:0,seq_hi:<current tip>,first_prev_hash:'0'*64,last_entry_hash:<current tip entry_hash>,sealed:false}\n  segments/seg-<NNNNNNNN>.jsonl[.gz]  # all sealed+active segments minted after migration; sealed ones gzipped; id = zero-padded ordinal\n  manifest.json                   # THE authoritative pointer, atomic-swapped (temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync)\n  spine.lock                      # path-stable, inode-stable cross-process lockfile, created once, NEVER os.replaced (fixes flock-binds-to-inode, invariant 14 / D3)\n  cutover.json                    # transient crash-recovery journal (holds the fully-signed new head + full new manifest during a prune commit); absent in steady state\n  head.json                       # HEAD_PATH, now v2 SignedChainHead, atomic-swapped exactly as today (checkpoint._atomic_write_text)\n  keys/                           # unchanged owner keypair\n  archive/snap-<baseSeq>-<gen>.jsonl.gz  # gzipped dropped-segment records for cold membership proofs (moved off-mount / deleted / kept per --archive)\n$SIGIL_HOME/floor.json            # C1 durable external monotonic floor (0600), OUTSIDE spine/ so `--reset` of the spine does NOT delete it\nThe current single 93MB spine.jsonl becomes segment-0 WITHOUT a rewrite: migration is O(1) — write a one-entry manifest + create spine.lock; no copy of 93MB. It stays the active segment (base=genesis) until the first rotation seals it (then optionally gzips it to segments/seg-00000000.jsonl.gz).",
          "manifest_and_lockfile": "MANIFEST (unsigned, but every claim cross-checked against the SIGNED head — keeps invariant 18's single commit point): {schema_version:1, generation:int (epoch, bumped every cutover — the SHARED index/killswitch/session token, A4), scope, active_segment:path, segments:[{id, path, seq_lo, seq_hi, first_prev_hash, last_entry_hash (the sealed boundary carried across the seam), sealed:bool, gz:bool, sha256, record_count, bytes, dev, ino}], base_seq, base_prev_hash (= segments[0].first_prev_hash; the synthetic genesis), base_count, snapshot_seq (latest kind='snapshot' state record), head_hash_at_swap (the head_hash this generation is crash-consistent with)}. WHY UNSIGNED: segment ORDER/linkage self-verifies via prev_hash+seq contiguity in verify_chain (a missing/reordered interior segment breaks the seam link -> caught); a DROPPED TAIL segment is caught by the signed head's absolute entry_count/last_seq; a DROPPED FRONT segment below base is caught by base_prev_hash + head. So the manifest is a performance pointer and verify() re-derives base_prev_hash + boundary hashes from actual segment bytes and reconciles against the signed head; a lying manifest cannot survive verify(). It is bound to the head via generation/head_hash_at_swap for crash-consistency. LOCKFILE PROTOCOL (invariant 14): spine.lock is opened O_RDWR|O_CREAT 0600 ONCE and never replaced; both append and compaction do fcntl.flock(lock_fd,LOCK_EX) BEFORE resolving the active segment from the manifest, then release on completion. In-process spine_lock RLock is re-keyed on SPINE_DIR (not the segment path). Because the flock target is a stable inode that os.replace never swaps, cross-process exclusion is sound even across a manifest/segment cutover; appends additionally re-validate the opened active segment's (dev,ino) against the manifest under the lock and retry on mismatch. This replaces today's flock-on-spine.jsonl which binds to the inode and cannot exclude an appender after an os.replace.",
          "cutover_algorithm": "ROTATION (seal+new active, retain-all, cheap — happens often): bounded locked section only. flock spine.lock -> re-read active tip, run _last_valid_boundary and truncate any torn tail of the active segment NOW + fsync (A2 seal-time truncate, since the append-path truncate can never reach a sealed file) -> create empty new active segment (fsync file + dir) -> write new manifest marking old active sealed (seq_hi,last_entry_hash,record_count cheap; sha256+gzip done lock-free afterward and patched) and naming the new active -> _atomic_write_text(manifest) -> bump generation -> release. No head change (entry_count/last_seq unchanged). PRUNE (space reclaim — the aggressive path): PRECONDITION (lock-free): pick baseline K = seq_lo of a retained segment s.t. K<=tip-RETENTION_RECORDS AND K<=oldest-open-workflow-seq (unresolved approvals / un-consumed steps / pending egress, so old-seq resolvers never dangle) AND K within RETENTION_DAYS AND all device pubkeys v2-capable (A1) OR owner override; REFUSE if any RETAINED record's parent_id/supersedes_id references a pruned seq not resolved by folded_state (invariant 16). HEAVY, LOCK-FREE (D1 — immutable sealed records never move; a kill-switch PANIC append is NOT blocked): scan dropped segments [0..K-1] to build folded_state (all 9 classes) + merkle_root over pruned leaves (leaf=entry_hash) ; gzip dropped records -> archive/snap-K-<gen>.jsonl.gz, fsync, compute archive_sha256; precompute the snapshot record content + cert_digest. BOUNDED LOCKED CRITICAL SECTION (small, O(1)+O(tail) — no O(file)): flock spine.lock -> re-read+torn-truncate active tip -> APPEND the kind='snapshot' record (payload=base ptr+merkle_root+folded_state+archive_ref) : normal O(1) append, fsync -> its seq S becomes the new tip; compute head v2 fields O(1) (head_hash=snapshot entry_hash, last_seq=S, entry_count=prev_entry_count+1, base_seq=K, base_prev_hash=dropped boundary last_entry_hash, base_count=K, merkle_root, snapshot_seq=S, prev_head_hash=old head_hash), sign_head_fields (Ed25519 ~us) -> build new manifest (drop segs<K, set base_*, snapshot_seq, generation+1, head_hash_at_swap=new head_hash) -> WRITE cutover.json journal (temp->fsync->replace->dir-fsync) holding the FULL signed head + FULL new manifest -> os.replace(head.json) -> os.replace(manifest.json) -> dir-fsync -> delete cutover.json -> release lock. AFTER lock, behind reader grace (D2): unlink/cold-move dropped segments. RECOVERY on startup: if cutover.json exists, RE-APPLY idempotently (re-replace head + manifest from the journal, dir-fsync, delete journal) — deterministic because the journal holds the fully-signed head + full manifest; dropped segments not-yet-unlinked are simply absent from the manifest so readers (manifest-only, never glob) don't see them; orphan segments/archive GC'd next start. Crash before journal = no prune happened (old head+manifest intact, appended snapshot record reads as benign 'stale', archive is an orphan). Crash after journal = forward-completed. FSYNC/REPLACE ORDERING across {segments,manifest,head}: archive fsync'd and snapshot record fsync'd BEFORE the journal; journal fsync'd before either swap; head swapped before manifest (both captured in the journal so order within the pair is recovery-idempotent); dir-fsync before journal delete; unlink strictly last. D2 reader grace: readers open ALL segment fds immediately after reading the manifest (POSIX keeps an unlinked-but-open inode readable to completion), and the compactor defers unlink behind a bounded grace after the manifest swap.",
          "index_changes": "Replace size-only detection (store.py:335,345-347) with GENERATION + IDENTITY. _offsets becomes seq->(segment_path,byte_offset). Sealed segments are immutable -> indexed once, never re-scanned. The ACTIVE segment keeps size-grew->extend / (dev,ino changed or size<scan_pos)->rebuild, but rebuild is now driven by an EPOCH check not a bare size compare: the store caches manifest.generation and the active segment (dev,ino,size); on each targeted read it cheaply re-stats manifest — if generation changed it REBINDS (reloads segment set, invalidates offsets for changed segments, re-seeds _last/next_seq from the new active tip, fixing A3's stale cached next_seq on a long-lived cross-process-compacted store instance). This makes a same-size rotation (an equal-or-larger inode swap, the core hazard the size heuristic cannot see) a detected epoch change, not a silent stale-index extend. A4 UNIFICATION: expose SpineStore.change_token() = hash(generation, active_dev, active_ino, active_size); the THREE size-as-change-token sites all switch to it — store._ensure_index (store.py), killswitch.py:57 (`self.store.path.stat().st_size` cache key -> change_token()), gesture/session.py:121-124 (`store.path.stat().st_size` kill-switch growth detector -> change_token()). One shared epoch token replaces all three, closing the same-size-rotation-serves-stale-killswitch-verdict hole in the un-halting (guarded) direction. Invariant 9 met: identity/epoch-based, segment-aware ((segment,offset)), rotation modeled explicitly so a genuine truncation attack (unexpected shrink of a sealed segment / active shrink without a generation bump) stays a detectable rebuild-and-flag, never masked as a benign rotation.",
          "rotation_trigger": "Two independent triggers. ROTATION (seal active, retain-all, always safe): default when the active segment reaches SEGMENT_MAX_BYTES=8MB (~4-5k records) OR SEGMENT_MAX_RECORDS=5000, whichever first; also opportunistically on a `sigil sign` checkpoint boundary (natural seam). Default policy = rotation ENABLED once slice-2 code is deployed (bounds per-file size, read latency, index size; reclaims via gzip-on-seal). PRUNE (space reclaim, tamper-model tradeoff): OPT-IN, never autonomous. Triggered by `sigil spine prune [--keep-records R] [--keep-days D] [--archive={cold,delete,keep}]` or a policy daemon that MUST hold the owner key. Default retention = keep last max(RETENTION_RECORDS=20000, RETENTION_DAYS=90) and never above the oldest-open-workflow-seq, pruning only WHOLE sealed segments below that floor. Default = prune DISABLED. This matches the strategic doctrine: rotation is autonomous/safe; hard-prune (C1 + state-folding + breaking head migration) is an OPERATOR decision, gated behind the owner key + the A1 fleet-upgrade flag day.",
          "consumer_transparency": "The 23 full-scanners keep working via segment-spanning iter_records for the RETAINED range — automatically, no edit — for the coverage-only ones: audit.py, dashboard.py (head from next_seq, recent window by seq arithmetic — gap-safe), perception/recall.py, agents/bastion.py _resolutions, vectors/index.py (cursor in Qdrant, sparse seq set already), notifier.py, budget.py (today-only filter, most prune-tolerant), graph/rebuild.py (full replay — loses pruned history into the graph but doesn't undercount security state). The 9 old-seq resolvers work for retained seqs via segment-aware get()/iter(since_seq); recent-workflow resolvers (operator.py, actor.py, perception/egress.py, approvals.py, actor_gate.py) are protected by the retention floor (K never above oldest-open-workflow-seq) so their seqs always resolve; deep-history resolvers (mcp episodic_range, ui/bridge /api/record/<seq>, graph/query.py + consolidate/queries.py citations) switch from a silent 404/empty to resolve()'s honest 'compacted below seq N (see snapshot S)' (Q5, 410 Gone). CONSUMERS THAT NEED EDITS (the honest cost of the aggressive stance — exactly the ~9 monotonic state-bearers that fold FULL history, invariant 15): each changes from fold-over-iter_records() to state=SnapshotState.load(store); fold(state_seed, iter_records()) — bridge/envelope.py device_nonce_highwater (replay floor), cli.py cmd_warden_anchor_get (newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey), agents/actor_scope.py creation_allowed (account.create cap count), mesh/registry.py authorized_devices+capability_map (latest-wins device auth), governor/killswitch.py _scan_engaged (latch), governor/promotion.py is_promoted (standing grants), agents/approvals.py pending (unresolved — normally empty via floor, defense-in-depth), consolidate/revise.py (supersession closure + promotion-key idempotency ledger), consolidate/queries.py (open threads/commitments/contradictions + source_seq citations, B1). Composition is exact because the snapshot folds ONLY the pruned [0..K-1] contribution and all retained [K..now] records are still physically scanned live — max()/count()/latest-wins-by-seq compose seed⊕retained without double-count. A missed state-bearer edit is the highest-severity failure (silent under-count of nonce floor / creation cap / device authz); Slice 3 de-risks by exercising the seed path with an EMPTY snapshot (base still 0) before any record is ever deleted.",
          "c1_handling": "The durable non-replayable floor lives in $SIGIL_HOME/floor.json (0600), OUTSIDE spine/ so a spine `--reset` does not delete it, atomic-written like the head. Monotonic content {schema_version,last_seq,base_seq,base_count,head_hash,ts}, updated ONLY-upward after a successful full anchor verification. classify_head/verify_checkpoint reject any head/snapshot with last_seq<floor.last_seq OR base_count<floor.base_count OR entry_count<floor.entry_count, and enforce the meta-chain: head.prev_head_hash must equal a previously accepted head_hash (floor.head_hash) so the sequence of heads is an unbroken chain even as records are pruned. This is the persisted, cross-restart, cross-process version of tail.py:60's in-memory _high_water (which resets to -1 each process and cannot carry the guarantee across a compaction run in a separate process) and it replaces the DORMANT prev_highwater on the checkpoint path (checkpoint.py:96 -> chain.py:78). HONEST THREAT BOUNDARY: C1 is CLOSED for warm same-host verifiers, a restarted host, and a returning paired device (each holds a monotonic floor that rejects a replayed old (short-spine, low-count, validly-owner-signed) pair — the pair hard-prune makes otherwise indistinguishable from current, because prune turns classify_head's n<signed_n alarm into a no-op for the pruned range). C1 is NOT closable from data alone for a truly COLD verifier bootstrapping trust off an UNTRUSTED MIRROR with no prior floor — it can only trust 'validly owner-signed' and cannot distinguish current from replayed-old; this is a FUNDAMENTAL limit of any prunable log (you cannot prove the absence of pruned records to a party that never observed them). Mitigation, not a proof: a cold device seeds its initial floor over the AUTHENTICATED device-pairing channel (WireGuard + device key, SIGIL Phase 9) directly from the live host — never from a mirror — so first-sync is out-of-band; thereafter it is monotonic. Residual honest caveat: an attacker with filesystem write to the host defeats floor.json AND the owner private key together (both FS-resident, the accepted local-first simplification checkpoint.py:9 already makes for the key), so the floor does not need to exceed the key's protection — but hard-prune widens the blast radius (a rolled-back short pair now reads as legitimate to a fresh verifier where retain-all would have caught it via the absolute count). A TPM/secure-element monotonic counter is the named upgrade path for hosts that have one.",
          "un_prunable_state_handling": "All folded into one kind='snapshot' spine record (appended at the tip in the prune's locked section, so it binds+chains normally per invariant 10, is covered transitively by the signed head, and is retained), payload.folded_state folding ONLY the pruned range [0..K-1]; consumers compose seed⊕retained-live-scan. Schema: device_nonce_highwater:{device_pubkey:max_nonce} (envelope.py replay floor; consumer seeds hi from this then max() over retained); warden_checkpoints:{pubkey:{count,seq,head_hash}} (cli.py newest-per-pubkey anti-rollback high-water); creation_counts:{service_label_or_origin:int} (actor_scope.py account.create cap; seed+count retained); device_auth:{device_pubkey:{state,seq,ts}} (mesh/registry latest-wins; retained higher-seq overrides); killswitch:{latched:bool,seq} (killswitch.py latch as-of K; retained engage/release override by seq); promotion_grants:{grant_key:{state,seq}} (promotion.py standing grants latest-wins); open_approvals:[{target_seq,proposer,seq,payload_digest}] (approvals.py unresolved — normally empty because the retention floor keeps unresolved workflow records unpruned; carried as defense-in-depth); archivist:{superseded_seqs:[...],promotion_keys:{key:seq},live_entities:[{seq,digest}]} (revise.py supersession closure so a pruned superseding record can't resurrect a stale fact, + promotion-key idempotency ledger so a pruned promotion can't be re-promoted); open_loops:{threads:[...],commitments:[...],contradictions:[...],citations:{...}} (queries.py open set + source_seqs so the 'never forget a loop' promise survives, B1). Referential integrity (16): the precondition scan REFUSES to prune if any retained record's parent_id/supersedes_id points into the pruned range unresolved by archivist.superseded_seqs; retained records' parent/supersedes are NEVER mutated (would break their binding). SnapshotState.load(store) reads the latest snapshot record (via manifest.snapshot_seq) and returns these maps for the ~9 consumer edits.",
          "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "A2 is closed by running the SAME _last_valid_boundary truncate at SEAL time, inside rotation's locked section, against the segment being sealed, then fsync BEFORE marking it sealed=true in the manifest. This is required because the append-path truncate (store.py:168-171) only ever protects the ACTIVE append target (self.path); once a segment is sealed no future append touches it, so a crash on the record that triggered rotation would otherwise leave a torn line that iter_records/_scan_from skip (store.py:209-216,372-378) and verify_chain turns into a permanent false seq-gap at the seam (chain.py:44). Sealing therefore guarantees every sealed segment ends on a valid newline-terminated record. Seq/entry_hash carried across the seam: the sealed segment's (seq_hi, last_entry_hash) is written into the manifest; the freshly-created active segment is EMPTY, so append's first write on it must NOT call build_chain (which would restart at seq 0) — _active_tip() seeds seq/prev from the manifest's prior-segment boundary (seq_hi+1, prev_hash=last_entry_hash) when the active segment has no valid records. Thus append_entry([boundary], cert_digest) yields seq_hi+1 with prev_hash=last_entry_hash, so verify_chain's strict seq contiguity (chain.py:44) and prev_hash linkage (chain.py:40) hold unbroken across every segment boundary and the concatenated stream reads as one chain.",
          "slice_plan": [
            "Slice 0 — schema-v2-capable + inert (the A1 flag day): add the six snapshot fields to SignedChainHead with never-pruned defaults, bump MAX_SUPPORTED_SCHEMA, add genesis_prev param to verify_chain (default _GENESIS_PREV), refactor sign_head -> sign_head_fields, harden verify_checkpoint (try/except) and SpineTailer (loud distinct 'schema too new' state). No behavior change: every head still effectively v1; ships fleet-wide before anything prunes.",
            "Slice 1 — manifest + path-stable spine.lock + segment-aware store, SINGLE segment: manifest.json (implicit one-segment for existing spines, O(1) migration of the 93MB file with no rewrite), (segment,offset) index, generation/identity epoch detection, change_token(); migrate the 3 size-token sites (store/killswitch/session) to change_token(). Byte-identical reads, no rotation yet — the big mechanical slice, independently reviewable as 'make the store segment-capable with one segment'.",
            "Slice 2 — rotation (retain-all) + gzip-on-seal + tail() spans segments (B3) + seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2): reclaims disk via compression and bounds file size/read latency with ZERO tamper-model change; all 34 consumers stay transparent. THE SAFE STOPPING POINT if the operator declines hard-prune.",
            "Slice 3 — snapshot record + folded state + the ~9 state-bearer consumer edits, but NO deletion: write a snapshot folding an EMPTY pruned range (base stays 0) so SnapshotState.load + every seed⊕retained consumer path is exercised and tested end-to-end BEFORE any record is removed (de-risks the highest-severity failure mode).",
            "Slice 4 — hard-prune + v2 head re-sign + cutover.json journal/recovery + C2 Merkle: prune() baseline computation (retention floor + open-workflow floor + verifier-version gate + referential-integrity refusal), lock-free scan/gzip/merkle, bounded locked cutover, drop segments; verify()/classify_head/verify_head switch to snapshot-aware absolute-count slicing. This is where base_prev_hash/entry_count-absolute/merkle_root land.",
            "Slice 5 — C1 durable floor.json (monotonic external floor + meta-chain prev_head_hash check) + honest pruned-seq resolution (resolve()/410 Gone in ui/bridge/mcp episodic_range) + authenticated first-sync floor seeding at device pairing. The anti-rollback hardening + honest-contract layer on top of a working prune."
          ],
          "risks": [
            "A missed state-bearer edit silently under-counts a SECURITY floor (device nonce replay, account.create cap, device authorization) — the chain still verifies, so no integrity check flags it. Highest-severity failure; mitigated by Slice 3 exercising the seed path with an empty snapshot before any deletion + a per-consumer test.",
            "FS-write attacker on the host defeats floor.json AND the FS-resident owner key together; hard-prune WIDENS the blast radius vs retain-all (a rolled-back short (spine,head) pair now reads as legitimate to a fresh verifier, where the absolute entry_count would previously have caught it).",
            "A truly cold verifier bootstrapping off an untrusted mirror cannot distinguish current from replayed-old pruned pair from data alone — fundamental; only the out-of-band pairing channel closes it, and a device that never pairs stays exposed.",
            "Merkle root binds pruned records for proof VERIFICATION, but --archive=delete makes reconstruction impossible; and vectors/index.py reset() after a prune permanently loses re-embeddable text for pruned records (steady-state fine since Qdrant persists, only a policy-change reset loses recall).",
            "Coupled head+manifest cutover: a journal/recovery bug yields a false-TAMPERING window (scenario 3) or a lost ack (scenario 4); mitigated by idempotent forward-recovery from a fully-signed journal and unlink-strictly-last.",
            "The heavy lock-free scan/gzip/merkle races concurrent appends to the range it is folding — but it only reads IMMUTABLE sealed segments below K and appends stay above K, so the fold is stable; still, the baseline K must be fixed before the scan and re-validated at cutover.",
            "verify_chain genesis_prev: a bug seeding the wrong base_prev_hash would accept a forged rebase; base_prev_hash is signed in the head, cross-checked against the manifest boundary AND the retained entries[0].prev_hash, so a mismatch fails closed — but this is now load-bearing where a hard-coded constant used to be."
          ],
          "invariants_at_risk": [
            "Invariant 4 (signed-head entry_count anti-truncation) — MOST STRESSED: redefined to ABSOLUTE (base_count+live). It stays a real gate only because entry_count/base_count are both signed and monotonic; the C2 slice change (classify_head slices only entries[:entry_count-base_count]) means the pruned prefix is no longer re-hashed, so its integrity now rests ENTIRELY on the signed base_prev_hash chain-accumulator + merkle_root. Residual risk: if merkle_root/base_prev_hash binding is ever computed wrong, a pre-archival edit becomes unprovable.",
            "C1 durable anti-rollback floor — the decisive residual: closed for warm/same-host/returning-paired verifiers via floor.json + meta-chain; UNCLOSABLE from data alone for a cold untrusted-mirror verifier.",
            "C2 Merkle commitment — now an INVARIANT not a nice-to-have: the snapshot's committed digest must bind every pruned record because the live-only slice removes the only other check.",
            "Invariant 14 (cross-writer lock covering the compactor) — highest DURABILITY risk: path-stable spine.lock touches every one of ~15 append sites; the old flock-on-target is inode-unsound once os.replace enters.",
            "Invariant 15 (monotonic-state-by-full-scan) — highest OPERATIONAL risk: 9-11 consumer edits, any one missed under-counts security state silently.",
            "A1 (closed signed head wire schema) — a genuine flag day: old binaries cannot be retrofitted, so the first prune is gated on fleet upgrade; failure = silent un-anchored (SpineTailer) or crash (verify_checkpoint) until hardened.",
            "Invariant 5 (monotonic last_seq) — preserved by keeping seq ABSOLUTE and only relocating the genesis prev; the renumber-to-0 trap (which would false-trip _high_water and make truncation indistinguishable from rebase) is explicitly avoided.",
            "Invariant 16 (referential integrity across a prune) — protected by the retention floor + archivist.superseded_seqs carry + a compactor refusal, but a policy-window bug could dangle a parent_id/supersedes_id into a deleted-and-uncommitted target.",
            "D1 (no heavy copy under the append lock) — satisfied by lock-free immutable-record scan/gzip/merkle + a bounded O(1)+O(tail) locked cutover, so a kill-switch PANIC append is never blocked; a regression that pulls the scan under the lock re-opens the worst-case (late panic)."
          ]
        },
        "verdicts": [
          {
            "design_name": "SEGMENT+SNAPSHOT-ANCHOR spine (aggressive hard-prune behind a signed, Merkle-committed, externally-floored snapshot)",
            "invariant_preservation": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "All 19 invariants + C1/C2 are addressed EXPLICITLY, and the hardest ones are handled correctly: entry_count stays ABSOLUTE (base_count+live) so it never decreases on a prune and remains a real tail-truncation gate (inv 4); last_seq stays absolute tip (inv 5); seqs are never renumbered, the renumber-to-0 trap is explicitly avoided (inv 3,5); base_prev_hash = the dropped boundary's entry_hash IS a genuine hash-accumulator that transitively commits genesis-rooting (inv 1) because entry_hash_{K-1} recursively folds prev_hash_{K-1}=entry_hash_{K-2}...down to _GENESIS_PREV; the seam is handled by seeding append seq/prev from the manifest boundary rather than build_chain (inv 2,12,13). Two residual gaps keep it off 5. (a) verify_chain's new genesis_prev seeding checks entries[0].prev_hash==base_prev_hash via the seed but NOTHING asserts entries[0].seq==base_seq — the retained chain's STARTING seq relative to the pruned count is trusted from the honest compactor, not self-verified; verify_chain only checks contiguity for i>0 (chain.py:44), so the anti-truncation gate at the boundary rests on base_count being honestly related to the seqs (exploitable only WITH the owner key, so a defense-in-depth gap not an attack, but it means 'trust-the-compactor' still sits on the tamper-critical path). (b) C1 anti-rollback is GENUINELY weakened for a cold verifier bootstrapping off an untrusted mirror — a prune turns the n<signed_n alarm into a no-op for the pruned range and an absolute count from an old validly-signed head is indistinguishable from current. The design is honest this is fundamental to any prunable log and gates it behind warm/paired verifiers + an out-of-band pairing floor, but it IS a real weakening of inv 4/5's rollback protection for that class, so full preservation cannot be claimed for hard-prune."
            },
            "consumer_transparency": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "Retain-all (rotation+gzip) is fully transparent: iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/verify/next_seq span segments and are byte-identical for any single-segment store (every deployment until first rotation), so all 34 consumers keep working unedited. get(seq) preserves signature (SpineRecord|None), the 23 full-scanners work automatically over the retained range, and the honest resolve()/410-Gone contract for deep-history queries is the correct answer to Q5. The honest cost is well-enumerated: exactly ~9 monotonic state-bearers (envelope nonce floor, warden checkpoints, account.create cap, mesh device-auth, killswitch latch, promotion grants, approvals, archivist supersession+promotion-key ledger, open-loops) need fold-over-iter -> seed(SnapshotState)+fold-retained edits, and the composition is provably exact (disjoint pruned[0..K-1] vs retained[K..now] ranges, all monoid ops max/sum/latest-wins). Three blemishes keep it at 4: the 'snapshot' record kind is not in KINDS (spine/models.py:9-17) and flows unmodeled to every full-scanner (tailer text()/vectors embed/dashboard); gz-sealed random-access get() is O(segment-decompress) not the claimed O(1) index seek (bounded to 8MB, but the '(segment,byte_offset) O(1)' claim is misleading for gz members); and '~9 consumer edits, any one missed silently under-counts a SECURITY floor with the chain still green' is a real, non-trivial transparency cost even though Slice 3's empty-snapshot exercise de-risks it well."
            },
            "durability_crash_safety": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "The strongest dimension. Acked appends are never lost: seal-time _last_valid_boundary truncate closes A2 (a torn record that triggered rotation would otherwise be stranded on a sealed segment that no future append can truncate -> permanent false verify() gap at chain.py:44); torn-tail-before-append is preserved on the ACTIVE segment. The path-stable spine.lock (opened O_RDWR|O_CREAT once, NEVER os.replaced) correctly fixes the latent flock-binds-to-inode bug — today's flock on f.fileno() of the append target (store.py:160) becomes unsound the moment os.replace enters, and this is caught (inv 14). The cutover uses a write-ahead cutover.json journal holding the FULL signed head + FULL manifest, applied idempotently forward on restart, with a rigorous fsync/replace ordering (archive+snapshot fsync -> journal fsync -> head replace -> manifest replace -> dir-fsync -> journal delete -> unlink STRICTLY last) and D2 reader-grace (open all segment fds off the manifest, defer unlink). D1 is satisfied: the O(file) scan/gzip/merkle runs lock-free over immutable sealed records so a kill-switch PANIC append is never blocked; only a bounded O(1)+O(tail) critical section takes the lock. Off 5 for one concrete unclosed edge: the coupled two-file swap (head first, manifest second) leaves a live LOCK-FREE reader able to observe new-head+old-manifest between the two os.replace calls; base_prev_hash/base_count won't match the old all-records manifest slice, so verify() flaps to a transient FALSE-TAMPERING. It's microsecond-bounded, self-heals, and errs in the SAFE direction (never makes tamper look clean), and head_hash_at_swap/generation are present to detect it — but the reader-side 'read manifest first, require head.head_hash==manifest.head_hash_at_swap, retry-on-skew' protocol is not actually specified, so the window is real."
            },
            "tamper_evidence_integrity": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "The core tamper model is correctly transformed, not naively discarded. Retain-all changes NOTHING about tamper-evidence. For hard-prune: the absolute entry_count remains a real anti-truncation gate; the pruned prefix's integrity is re-anchored on the owner-SIGNED base_prev_hash accumulator PLUS a signed merkle_root over pruned leaves (C2 correctly elevated from nice-to-have to invariant, because once classify_head slices only the live window the pruned prefix is no longer re-hashed and the ONLY remaining commitment is the accumulator+merkle); compaction stays owner-key-gated so a file-write attacker without the key cannot forge a rebase (a forged prune fails the Ed25519 head, inv 11). The honesty is exactly what a purist wants: it states plainly that hard-prune WIDENS the blast radius vs retain-all (a rolled-back short (spine,head) pair now reads as legitimate to a fresh verifier where absolute entry_count would previously have caught it), and that the FS-write attacker defeats floor.json AND the FS-resident owner key together. Two things cap it at 4: (1) the seam-seq is trusted-not-verified (see invariant score) so 'trust the compactor's base_count' remains on the tamper-critical path; (2) the pruned-prefix integrity now rests ENTIRELY on one correctly-computed base_prev_hash+merkle binding — a single bug there makes a pre-archival edit unprovable, a fragility the retain-all path never has. Critically it does NOT create any window where a TAMPER reads as CLEAN (all failure modes flag false-positive), which is the right direction for the residual risk."
            },
            "implementability_blast_radius": {
              "score": 3,
              "rationale": "Large, security-critical, high-coordination change, but staged intelligently. In its favor: the 93MB spine.jsonl becomes segment-0 with an O(1) manifest write (no 93MB rewrite); the store stays byte-identical until rotation is switched on (default-OFF); the 6-slice plan has a genuine SAFE STOPPING POINT at Slice 2 (retain-all, zero tamper-model change) and de-risks the highest-severity failure (a missed state-bearer under-counting a security floor) by exercising the seed path with an EMPTY snapshot in Slice 3 before any record is deleted. Against it: the surface is wide — a v2 head schema is a BREAKING wire migration (extra='forbid' means old binaries model_validate_json BEFORE they can read schema_version, so the first prune is a true fleet-upgrade FLAG DAY; the two head consumers even fail differently today — SpineTailer._resolve_head silently swallows -> un-anchored at tail.py:118, verify_checkpoint has no try/except -> crashes sigil verify + mcp ingest_status at checkpoint.py:108, both of which must be hardened first); the path-stable lockfile touches ~15 append sites; the epoch token unifies 3 size-as-change-token sites; ~9 consumers get security-sensitive fold edits; plus new manifest/journal/merkle/floor.json/gzip-reader/resolve() machinery across ui/bridge/mcp. It is buildable and well-sequenced, but the full hard-prune tail (Slices 4-5) is a coordinated flag-day change over tamper-critical and monotonic-security state where the cost of one missed detail is silent security under-count — a genuinely wide blast radius that only the staging keeps manageable."
            },
            "fatal_flaws": [
              "NON-FATAL but concrete (safe-direction): the coupled head-then-manifest cutover exposes a live lock-free reader to a new-head+old-manifest skew between the two os.replace calls; base_prev_hash/base_count won't match the old all-records manifest, so verify()/classify_head transiently reports FALSE TAMPERING to consumers like mcp ingest_status. It is microsecond-bounded, self-healing, and never makes a real tamper look clean, but the required reader-side coherence gate (read manifest first, require head.head_hash==manifest.head_hash_at_swap and matching generation, retry-on-skew) is gestured at via head_hash_at_swap but NOT actually specified, so the flapping window is real.",
              "NON-FATAL trust-gap: verify_chain is seeded with base_prev_hash so entries[0].prev_hash linkage is checked, but NOTHING asserts entries[0].seq == base_seq (== base_count). verify_chain only enforces contiguity for i>0 (chain.py:44). So the seam's seq-vs-absolute-count consistency is TRUSTED from the honest compactor rather than self-verified; a wrong base_seq alongside a correct base_prev_hash would pass verify() (reachable only via a compactor bug or the owner key, not a keyless attacker, hence non-fatal — but it leaves 'trust the compactor' on the anti-truncation boundary that inv 2/4 should make self-checking)."
            ],
            "weighted_verdict": "Shippable, but ONLY in the staged form the design itself proposes — and the scope split is the whole point. Slices 0-2 (segment rotation + path-stable lockfile + epoch index + seal-time A2 truncate + gzip-on-seal) preserve ALL 19 invariants plus C1/C2 with ZERO tamper-model change, keep every one of the 34 consumers transparent, reclaim real disk (~5-10x via compression), and even FIX a latent flock-inode durability bug that today's single-file spine is only accidentally safe from. Ship that subset broadly and unconditionally; it is the correct answer to 'bound growth WITHOUT weakening tamper-evidence.' Slices 3-5 (hard-prune: state-folding + absolute-count re-slice + base_prev_hash/merkle accumulator + breaking v2 head migration + floor.json) are a GENUINE, honestly-disclosed tamper-evidence tradeoff — they widen the rollback blast radius for cold/untrusted-mirror verifiers (a fundamental, unavoidable limit of any prunable log) and turn the first prune into a fleet-upgrade flag day. They are correctly gated behind the owner key + verifier-version gate + operator opt-in + a retention/open-workflow floor, and every residual failure mode flags in the SAFE direction (false-tamper, never false-clean). So: SHIP retain-all now; authorize hard-prune only as an explicit per-deployment operator decision that accepts the cold-verifier residual, and only after the two hardening fixes below land. No fatal flaw survives scrutiny — nothing here makes a tamper read as clean or silently corrupts a consumer in the unsafe direction; the two concrete issues both fail safe.",
            "one_change_that_would_improve_it": "Make the seam self-verifying and the read self-consistent, killing the last two 'trust-the-compactor' and 'trust-the-timing' gaps in one stroke: (1) in verify()/classify_head, assert entries[0].seq == base_seq AND base_seq == base_count alongside the existing entries[0].prev_hash == base_prev_hash cross-check, so the retained chain's starting position relative to the absolute count is independently proven rather than trusted from the honest compactor — this removes the compactor from the anti-truncation boundary of inv 2/4; and (2) specify the reader-side coherence protocol explicitly — every read snapshots the manifest FIRST, then requires head.head_hash == manifest.head_hash_at_swap and matching generation, retrying on skew — which closes the transient false-TAMPERING window from the coupled head+manifest swap. Both are small, both are load-bearing precisely because hard-prune moves genesis-rooting and anti-truncation onto signed metadata whose consistency was previously self-evident from re-hashing the whole log."
          },
          {
            "design_name": "SEGMENT+SNAPSHOT-ANCHOR spine (aggressive hard-prune behind a signed, Merkle-committed, externally-floored snapshot)",
            "invariant_preservation": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "The core anti-rollback math is sound and I could not break it. Active/sealed-uncompressed = O(1) seek; sealed-gz = open+scan to seq, O(segment) bounded (rare deep reads).\n- tail(n): now spans active then walks back into prior sealed segment(s) if active holds < n (fixes B3); bounded seek-from-end per file.\n- count(): sum(seg.record_count for sealed segments in manifest) + scan(active).
