Schema:\n{schema_version:1, generation:<u64 monotonic, ++ on every manifest swap — THE unified epoch/index-invalidation token>, scope:<SCOPE>, segments:[\n  {id, path (relative), compressed:bool, first_seq, last_seq (null if active/open), first_prev_hash (=GENESIS for id 0, else prior seg last_entry_hash — the append-seam link), last_entry_hash, record_count, byte_size (uncompressed logical, for the offset index), file_sha256 (integrity of the sealed file AT REST; not a chain digest), active:bool}\n]}\nLOCKFILE (spine.lock): a dedicated file created once, NEVER renamed/replaced/unlinked — so its inode is stable (invariant 14 / D3). BOTH append and rotation fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX) it BEFORE reading the manifest to resolve the active segment. This replaces today's fcntl.flock on the spine file itself (store.py:158-160), which binds to the inode that the seal-rename swaps and thus cannot exclude a cross-process appender across a rotation. The in-process spine_lock (RLock, store.py:37-47) is re-keyed from the resolved active-file path to the STABLE spine-dir path so re-entrancy (the nonce gate, envelope.py:129) and the RLock key survive a seal; because the active NAME is always \".../spine.jsonl\", callers passing store.path as the lock key (envelope.py) need no edit.",
          "cutover_algorithm": "Trigger checked opportunistically after a successful append when active byte_size >= threshold. Rotation seals the active segment via a HARDLINK-before-commit ordering so the sealed content exists at its final path BEFORE the commit and the old name is dropped only AFTER — no crash window can lose an ack'd record. Bounded LOCKED critical section (all O(1) except an ~8KB seek-from-end torn-tail scan; NO O(file) work — satisfies D1):\n 1. flock spine.lock; acquire spine_lock RLock (same order as append).\n 2. Re-read manifest -> active = spine.jsonl, first_seq A. If active has 0 records, abort (nothing to seal).\n 3. SEAL-TIME torn-tail truncate: _last_valid_boundary(spine.jsonl) (store.py:83-121), truncate any torn tail, fsync -> (last_seq B, last_entry_hash H). (A2: the sealed segment is guaranteed to end on a valid record; the append-path truncate can never reach a sealed file.)\n 4. os.link(spine.jsonl, segments/seg-A-B.jsonl); fsync dir. Sealed content now exists under its final name AND still under spine.jsonl (shared inode).\n 5. Build new manifest: append sealed seg-A-B (A..B, first_prev_hash=prior boundary, last_entry_hash=H, compressed:false), set active=spine.jsonl (first_seq=B+1, first_prev_hash=H, last_seq=null), generation++. Write temp -> fsync -> os.replace(manifest) -> dir-fsync.  <-- COMMIT POINT.\n 6. Drop the old active name: os.unlink(spine.jsonl) (seg-A-B keeps the inode+content), then leave spine.jsonl ABSENT (absent == empty active). dir-fsync.\n 7. Release locks.\nBecause the lock is HELD across steps 5->6, no concurrent appender ever sees the post-commit-pre-unlink double-content; only a crash can. CRASH RECOVERY (startup reconciler under the lock): if the active file's first record seq < manifest.active.first_seq, it is a pre-unlink leftover whose records are provably committed in the sealed segment -> truncate active to 0. If manifest names a sealed path that is absent but a same-range file exists un-manifested, drop the orphan. Crash BEFORE step 5 commit = intact pre-rotation state (orphan hardlink cleaned up). Crash AFTER commit = post-rotation state (reconciler truncates the stale active tail). The signed head is NOT touched at rotation (logical chain identical), so no head/manifest co-commit is needed.\nDEFERRED, LOCK-FREE (gzip, Slice 4): compress seg-A-B.jsonl -> .gz (temp->fsync->replace->dir-fsync), then a SECOND O(1) locked manifest swap flips compressed:true/path and generation++, then move the superseded uncompressed file to trash/ (not immediate unlink).\nREADER GRACE (D2): a reader opens fds for ALL manifest-named segments IMMEDIATELY after reading the manifest and before yielding any record (POSIX keeps an unlinked-but-open inode alive), so a concurrent swap+unlink cannot ENOENT mid-read; superseded files go to trash/ and a reaper deletes trash entries older than a grace window strictly greater than any read.",
          "index_changes": "_offsets becomes seq -> (segment_id, byte_offset_in_uncompressed_stream) (invariant 9's (segment,offset) key). Sealed segments are IMMUTABLE, so each one's seq->offset sub-index is built once and cached (optionally persisted as seg-*.idx for O(1) cold start, Slice 5); the ACTIVE segment keeps today's extend-on-grow logic (store.py:345-346: size>_scan_pos => extend; append-only within the active segment => offsets never move). Change-detection moves from SIZE-ONLY to EPOCH/IDENTITY: the store caches the manifest `generation` (and st_dev/st_ino of the active file as a belt); each targeted read cheaply re-reads generation — on change it rebinds the active path, rebuilds the active index, invalidates next_seq (A3), and keeps sealed sub-indexes (still valid, keyed by immutable segment id). This replaces store.py:335,345-351's size-only extend/rebuild, whose shrink-branch would otherwise MISFIRE reading a rotation as an in-place truncation attack, and whose same-or-larger-inode-swap blind spot (crash scenario 8) it closes. A4 UNIFIED: the same `store.epoch()` (generation) replaces the three independent st_size change-tokens — store._ensure_index (store.py:335), killswitch.py:56-57 (verdict-cache key), gesture/session.py:121 (kill-switch growth detector) — so a same-size rotation can never serve a stale kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction.",
          "rotation_trigger": "Seal fires when the ACTIVE segment's byte size crosses a threshold, checked opportunistically after each successful append (cheap fstat already in hand). Default policy: seal at 16 MB active size (=> ~7-8k records at current ~2KB/record; bounds per-file size and keeps the active-segment offset index and torn-tail scans small) with a secondary record-count cap (default 25k) whichever hits first. Also seal-on-demand: `sigil spine rotate` (manual) and an OPTIONAL seal-at-checkpoint hook so `sigil sign` boundaries align with segment boundaries (nice for reasoning, not required). Rotation NEVER fires from a read path and never mid-append; only after an ack, under the lock, and it aborts if the active segment is empty. Thresholds are config values; a warm-window count governs which sealed segments stay uncompressed.",
          "consumer_transparency": "All 34 stay working because iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/verify/next_seq present ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain (invariant 12) and retain-all means no seq is ever unresolvable. The 23 FULL-SCANNERS (graph/rebuild, revise, audit, dashboard, mcp ingest_status, killswitch/promotion/budget/actor_scope scans, approvals.pending, envelope device_nonce_highwater, mesh/registry authorized_devices, bridge/daemon dedup, recall, consolidate/queries B1, etc.) just iterate the manifest-ordered concatenation — every historical record is still physically present and read, so the security-relevant monotonic scans (nonce high-water, warden_checkpoint high-water, account.create cap, device authorize/revoke, kill-switch latch, promotion grants, archivist supersession+promotion-key ledger, open threads/commitments/contradictions) are exactly preserved. The 9 OLD-SEQ RESOLVERS (mcp episodic_range, actor_gate.action_approved get(seq)+forward-iter, perception/egress get(seq), bridge/server + ui/server GET /api/record/<seq>, operator/actor execute/undo, graph anchor citations) keep resolving because get(old_seq) reaches into sealed segments — no prune, so no 404/empty-window regression and question-5's honest 'compacted below N' contract NEVER needs to fire. ONLY 3 consumers must change, and all 3 already reached PAST the SpineStore API into store.path (they were never API-transparent): (1) killswitch.py:56-57 st_size verdict-cache key -> store.epoch()/generation (A4; note: today every append grows the active file and a seal SHRINKS it -> shrink already forces a rescan, so this is correctness-hardening, not a live danger); (2) gesture/session.py:121 st_size growth detector -> generation (A4); (3) cli.py --reset SPINE_PATH.unlink -> clear the whole spine dir (all segments + manifest + lockfile + trash), else a reset leaves stale sealed segments. Optional NON-required efficiency edits: budget.py/actor_scope full scans could take a since_seq bound to avoid O(total) per decision (rotation makes the O(total) scan span more files), but correctness does not require them.",
          "c1_handling": "C1 DOES NOT ARISE. Retain-all keeps entry_count ABSOLUTE (checkpoint.py:92, chain.py:60), so classify_head's `n < signed_n` truncation alarm (checkpoint.py:93) stays a LIVE guard for the entire history — it is never converted into a no-op for any pruned range, because nothing is pruned. The design therefore does NOT weaken today's anti-rollback posture one bit: the durable floor remains exactly what it is now — the owner-signed head's entry_count/last_seq/head_hash on disk (checkpoint.py:82-84) plus SpineTailer's in-memory monotonic _high_water (tail.py:60,90-93). No new external floor is needed, and none is added. C1 (a durable non-replayable floor OUTSIDE the compactable data, needed so a cold/fresh verifier can distinguish a legit compaction baseline from a replayed old short-spine/low-count pair) is specific to HARD-PRUNE, which this design deliberately declines. Hard-prune — with its C1 floor, C2 Merkle commitment, snapshot state-folding, and the breaking head migration — is a tamper-evidence tradeoff that is an explicit OPERATOR decision, not something this autonomous, minimal-blast-radius design takes.",
          "un_prunable_state_handling": "Retain-all => the ~9 monotonic state-bearer classes are UNTOUCHED; none is folded into any snapshot because no snapshot exists. Each is reconstructed by a full scan that now spans all segments via the transparent iter_records, so each reconstruction reads exactly the same record set as today: (1) per-device nonce replay high-water (envelope.py:88-104); (2) newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey / anti-rollback high-water (cli.py:283-287); (3) account.create / web.actor.step creation-cap count (actor_scope.py:49, actor.py:139,247); (4) latest device authorize/revoke per key — the bridge authorization ledger (mesh/registry.py:66-76, capability_map:42-50); (5) kill-switch engage/release latch (killswitch.py:75 _scan_engaged); (6) standing promotion grants latest-wins (promotion.py:48-55); (7) unresolved queued approvals (approvals.py:62 pending); (8) archivist supersession chain + promotion-key idempotency ledger (revise.py:16-25, promotion_ledgers); (9) open threads/commitments/contradictions + their source_seq/conflicting_seq/verified_seq citations (consolidate/queries.py — the B1 first-class MCP surface). The SOLE requirement is that these full scans reach every segment, which the segment-spanning iter_records delivers by construction. This is the decisive advantage over hard-prune, where each of these would have to be enumerated into a signed snapshot payload or silently corrupt.",
          "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "At seal, step 3 runs _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83-121) on the active spine.jsonl UNDER THE LOCK and truncates any torn tail BEFORE the hardlink (A2: the append-path truncate at store.py:168-171 only ever touches the ACTIVE file, so without a seal-time truncate a segment sealed just after a torn interrupted write would end on a garbage line that iter_records/_scan_from skip (store.py:209-216,372-378) and verify_chain turns into a permanent false seq gap (chain.py:44) at the seam). The truncate yields the clean boundary (last_seq B, last_entry_hash H), recorded in the manifest as the sealed segment's last_entry_hash and as the new active segment's first_prev_hash. The next append to the fresh (empty) active segment seeds append_entry([boundary]) from H at seq B+1 — carrying seq AND entry_hash across the seam so verify_chain's prev_hash linkage (chain.py:40) and strict seq contiguity (chain.py:44) never break. Because the two files are read separately (per-file `for raw in f`), the within-file torn+new merge hazard that BLOCK-1 fixes does not recur across the seam; only the skipped-line-becomes-gap hazard does, and the seal-time truncate closes it. next_seq/append also handle the empty-active case by seeding from the manifest boundary, never from genesis (invariants 13,19).",
          "slice_plan": [
            "Slice 0 — Manifest + path-stable lockfile, NO rotation. Write manifest.json naming the current spine.jsonl as the single active segment (id 0, first_seq 0, first_prev_hash=GENESIS) with ZERO file move; create spine.lock; move append's flock from the spine file to spine.lock and re-key the RLock to the spine dir; SpineStore resolves the active segment via the manifest. One segment => behavior byte-identical to today; default-safe; ships the D3 lock-soundness fix and the generation-epoch foundation. Reviewable in isolation.",
            "Slice 1 — Segment-spanning READ API. iter_records/get/entries/count/verify/tail concatenate manifest-ordered segments; segment-aware, epoch-invalidated (segment,offset) index; next_seq/append seed from the manifest boundary when the active is empty. Still one segment in practice => still byte-identical, but validated against a synthetic multi-segment fixture (hand-built 3-segment chain) proving verify()/count()/get() span seams with no gap/dup.",
            "Slice 2 — A4 unified epoch token + reset. Migrate killswitch.py and gesture/session.py off store.path.stat().st_size to store.epoch()/generation; make cli.py --reset clear the whole spine dir. The ONLY consumer edits in the program.",
            "Slice 3 — Rotation (seal-on-threshold). The O(1) hardlink+manifest cutover, seal-time torn-tail truncate, boundary carry, startup reconciler, held-lock commit->unlink, reader open-all-fds grace. Retain-all, sealed segments UNCOMPRESSED. This is where per-file size and get() latency get bounded; touches_tamper_model still false. Verify: rotate under concurrent appends + a simulated kill-switch panic append and assert the panic is not blocked (D1) and verify() stays green across the seam.",
            "Slice 4 — gzip-on-seal disk reclaim. Lock-free async compression of cold sealed segments outside the warm window; second O(1) manifest swap; trash/ + reaper unlink. The disk win; last because it is the only slice with a full-scan CPU trade-off, and it is independently revertible.",
            "Slice 5 (optional) — Persisted seg-*.idx sidecars for O(1) cold-start get() into sealed segments; crash-fuzz harness (kill -9 at each cutover step) asserting the reconciler always yields a verify()-clean, non-double-counted chain."
          ],
          "risks": [
            "Seam double-content crash window: a crash between the manifest commit (step 5) and the old-active unlink (step 6) leaves records A..B in BOTH seg-A-B and spine.jsonl. Mitigated by the startup reconciler (truncate active records whose seq < manifest.active.first_seq — provably committed in the sealed segment) AND a defensive per-segment reader range-filter. Residual: a reconciler bug could over-truncate, but only records provably duplicated in the committed sealed file.",
            "os.link is POSIX/same-filesystem; on non-POSIX (no fcntl anyway) fall back to copy-then-fsync for the seal. Low risk on this Linux/Kali local-first target.",
            "gzip-on-seal adds decompression CPU to the 23 full-scanners, worst for budget.py (O(total) per decision). Mitigated by the warm uncompressed window; residual CPU on deep/cold full scans. Recommend (non-blocking) since_seq bounds on the hottest scanners.",
            "get() into a sealed .gz segment is O(segment) not O(1) (gzip has no random seek). Bounded by segment size and rare (deep-history get). Slice 5 .idx sidecars only help within the decompressed stream, not around it; keeping the warm window uncompressed keeps recent get() O(1).",
            "Long-lived store instances (gesture daemon, agent runner) must poll manifest generation on targeted reads to catch a rotation done by another process (A3) — one extra cheap manifest stat per targeted read; full scans re-read the manifest anyway.",
            "tail() spanning into sealed .gz segments adds decompression to the bridge arm-dedup path; bounded to the dedup window and already backstopped by ARM_FRESHNESS, so safe but a small latency add.",
            "Reader-grace correctness depends on readers opening ALL segment fds before yielding; a future refactor that lazily opens a segment mid-iteration would reopen the D2 unlink race. Enforce with a single _open_segments() choke point + a test."
          ],
          "invariants_at_risk": [
            "Invariant 14 (cross-writer serialization / path-stable lockfile, D3) — MOST stressed: moving the flock from the inode-bound spine file to spine.lock touches every one of the ~15 append call sites' durability guarantee; a miss reopens the os.replace-vs-appender lost-ack race (crash scenario 4). Residual risk: an appender that resolves the active path but doesn't re-validate under the lock could write a stale fd; closed by resolving-active-under-lock only.",
            "Invariant 9 (index correctness under external mutation, + A4) — the (segment,offset) key and generation-based invalidation replace size-only detection across THREE sites; residual risk is a site still trusting size (crash scenario 8's equal-or-larger inode swap) if the A4 unification is incomplete.",
            "Invariant 12 (read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain) — the concatenation seam must be gap/dup-free; stressed by the crash-window double-content and by manifest-vs-glob discipline (a glob would double-count during the trash window). Residual: any reader deriving the set from a directory listing instead of the manifest.",
            "Invariants 13 + 19 (durable tip / genesis-vs-empty) — the empty-active-is-not-genesis trap: append/next_seq MUST seed from the manifest boundary, or a fresh active segment reads as an empty/new spine (last_seq->0, seq reuse, chain fork). Stressed at every seal.",
            "Invariant 7 / A2 (torn-tail truncate at SEAL time) — must run on the sealed segment under the lock; a miss = permanent false verify() gap at the seam.",
            "NOT at risk (deliberately): invariant 4 (entry_count absolute), the head schema (A1), C1 durable floor, C2 Merkle commitment — all untouched because retain-all never prunes, which is precisely why this is the minimal-blast-radius path and hard-prune's tamper-evidence tradeoffs are left as an explicit operator decision."
          ]
        },
        "verdicts": [
          {
            "design_name": "SEGMENT-ROTATION (retain-all, API-preserving)",
            "invariant_preservation": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "Grounded verification confirms the design preserves the invariants it declines to touch AND the storage-mechanics invariants it stresses. Genesis-rooting (inv.1): no prev_hash is ever rewritten, so concatenated segment-0 still presents entries[0].prev_hash==_GENESIS_PREV to verify_chain (chain.py:38-41). PASS. entry_count absolute (inv.4): retain-all means len(entries()) is unchanged across a seal, so classify_head's n<signed_n alarm (checkpoint.py:93) stays a LIVE guard over the whole history and no rotation is confusable with a rollback. seq-contiguity/one-genesis-chain (inv.2,12): manifest-ordered concat + boundary carry (B+1 seeded from H) + seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2) keep chain.py:44 contiguity intact across seams. The genesis-vs-empty trap (inv.13,19) is correctly identified as the single most dangerous point: after step-6 the active file is ABSENT, so store.py:174 `append_entry([last]...) if last else build_chain(...)` would FORK to seq0/GENESIS unless append/next_seq seed from the manifest boundary — the design mandates exactly this. Path-stable lockfile (inv.14/D3) correctly replaces the inode-bound flock (store.py:158-160). ONE real gap survives: invariant 8 (fsync durability). Because the active file is re-created from absent after EVERY seal, the first post-seal append hits append()'s create-without-dir-fsync path (store.py:157-183 fsyncs the fd but never the parent dir) — rotation converts the 'first-creation dir-fsync gap' the durability analysis flagged from once-ever into once-per-segment, so a crash can lose the directory entry of the acked boundary record seq B+1. The design cites the fsync invariant but does not carry this consequence. Docked one point for that plus the count()-trusts-manifest-record_count vs entries()-physical-scan consistency nit."
            },
            "consumer_transparency": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "The strongest possible transparency claim, and it holds against the code: rotation is content-preserving, so iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/verify yield a byte-identical ChainEntry sequence (same seqs, cert_digests, prev_hash/entry_hash linkage) before and after any seal. All 23 full-scanners keep reconstructing their monotonic state (nonce high-water envelope.py:88-104, warden high-water cli.py:283-287, account.create cap actor_scope.py:49, kill-switch latch killswitch.py:75, promotion grants promotion.py:48, approvals.pending, archivist supersession+promotion-key ledger revise.py:16-25, queries.py open threads/commitments — the B1 surface) because retain-all keeps every scanned record physically present. All 9 old-seq resolvers (mcp episodic_range, actor_gate.action_approved get(seq), bridge/ui GET /api/record/<seq>, graph anchors) keep resolving because get(old_seq) reaches into sealed segments — no prune, so question-5's '404/compacted-below-N' contract never fires. The critique's B3 tail() mislabel and B1 queries.py omission are both addressed. Only 3 consumers change and all 3 already reached PAST the API into store.path (killswitch.py:56-57, gesture/session.py:121, cli.py --reset) — honestly enumerated. Caveat (non-correctness, disclosed): gzip-on-seal adds decompression CPU to every default full scan, worst for budget.py which O(total)-scans per agent decision; correctness is untouched but this is a real latency regression left as an optional since_seq fix."
            },
            "durability_crash_safety": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "A well-constructed crash protocol that mostly mirrors checkpoint._atomic_write_text (checkpoint.py:33-60). The manifest is the sole linearization point, swapped via the verbatim temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync pattern; the hardlink-before-commit ordering (step4 os.link before step5 os.replace) means the sealed content exists at its final name before commit and the old name drops only after, so no crash window loses an acked record. D1 is genuinely satisfied: the O(file) gzip is lock-free over immutable sealed records, and the locked cutover is all O(1) except an ~8KB seek-from-end torn-tail scan, so a kill-switch panic append is never blocked. The decoupling of head from rotation elegantly dissolves crash-scenario-3 (no head/manifest co-commit needed because the logical chain is identical). Two things hold this at 4 rather than 5: (1) the invariant-8 dir-fsync gap on per-seal active re-creation (see invariant_preservation) is a real no-lost-ack regression; (2) the design introduces a NEW failure surface — the post-commit/pre-unlink double-content window where A..B live in both seg-A-B and spine.jsonl — mitigated by a startup reconciler (truncate active if first-seq < manifest.active.first_seq) AND a per-segment read range-filter, but the reconciler's over-truncation safety and the whole kill-9-at-each-step correctness are only exercised by the crash-fuzz harness in the OPTIONAL Slice 5. A crash-critical reconciler should not have its fuzz coverage relegated to optional."
            },
            "tamper_evidence_integrity": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "This is the design's strongest and best-earned claim: touches_tamper_model=false survives harsh scrutiny. The signed head, sign_head (chain.py:56-63), verify_head (chain.py:66-80), classify_head (checkpoint.py:87-101), verify_chain, verify_record, and the SignedChainHead/ChainEntry extra='forbid' schema (models.py:49,58) are ALL untouched and keep passing, so A1's breaking head migration and the two-consumers-fail-differently hazard never arise. The n<signed_n truncation alarm stays live over the ENTIRE history because entry_count is absolute (retain-all). The deliberate decision to NOT owner-sign the manifest survived my adversarial check: a forged manifest that drops a TAIL segment -> len(entries())<entry_count -> classify_head TAMPERING; drops/reorders an INTERIOR segment -> verify_chain prev_hash/seq-gap break (chain.py:40-45); corrupts sealed bytes -> entry_hash/binding break. The ONLY bypass is a full (spine+head) rollback to a prior owner-signed pair — but that is a PRE-EXISTING capability of any filesystem-write attacker on today's single-file spine, so rotation does not weaken the C1 cold-verifier floor one bit. C1/C2/state-folding are correctly scoped to hard-prune and explicitly declined as an operator decision. No prune means no prune-indistinguishable-from-rollback, which is precisely the purist's central fear, retired by construction."
            },
            "implementability_blast_radius": {
              "score": 3,
              "rationale": "The slice plan is genuinely well-staged and each slice ships default-safe: Slice 0 (manifest naming the current spine.jsonl as segment-0 with ZERO file move) and Slice 1 (segment-spanning read API validated on a synthetic 3-segment fixture) are byte-identical to today, and Slice 0 alone ships the D3 lock-soundness fix — real de-risking. But the aggregate blast radius on the system's single most safety-critical file is large: store.py's append/next_seq/get/tail/count/entries/iter_records/_read_last_entry all become segment/manifest-aware; the flock moves off the inode to spine.lock at every one of the ~15 append call sites (the design's own 'MOST stressed, highest-risk durability change'); the index goes from flat seq->offset to (segment,offset) with epoch invalidation replacing size-only detection across three sites; and the boundary-seeding, reconciler, reader-grace open-all-fds choke point, and long-lived-instance generation-poll (A3) must ALL be perfect simultaneously. Several of these are subtle fork/rollback traps (empty-active-as-genesis; a stale SpineTailer reading only the shrunk active segment trips check_anchor's false ROLLBACK at tail.py:90). With the crash-fuzz harness that would validate the delicate parts sitting in the OPTIONAL Slice 5, the total surface keeps this at 3 despite the good decomposition."
            },
            "fatal_flaws": [
              "Invariant-8 (acked-append durability) is breached once per seal: after step-6 leaves spine.jsonl ABSENT, the first post-seal append re-creates the file and fsyncs only the fd (store.py:182-183), never the parent directory, so a crash can lose the directory entry of the acked boundary record seq B+1. Not design-killing (narrow window, one record per seal, trivially fixable) but it is a concrete, unaddressed regression of the no-lost-ack guarantee that rotation reintroduces on a recurring basis.",
              "Lesser: count() summing manifest per-segment record_count can diverge from entries()' physical scan during the double-content crash window or under a torn/forged manifest; not a security break because classify_head uses entries() directly, but it violates the design's own 'count() equals len(entries()) exactly' claim unless count() also range-filters/physically reconciles."
            ],
            "weighted_verdict": "Shippable safely for its stated scope — retain-all rotation, NOT prune. It is the correct minimal-blast-radius path: it bounds per-file size and get() latency and (via gzip-on-seal) reclaims practical disk while genuinely NOT weakening tamper-evidence or append-only, which was the hard requirement. Tamper-evidence (5) and consumer transparency (5) are airtight and well-grounded in the code; the anti-rollback posture is preserved by construction because retain-all keeps entry_count absolute and the chain genesis-rooted, so no rotation is ever confusable with a rollback. The design correctly refuses to take on hard-prune's C1 floor / C2 Merkle commitment / breaking head migration, leaving those as an explicit operator decision — the right call. It is NOT flawless: one concrete invariant-8 durability gap (dir-fsync on per-seal active re-creation) and a new double-content crash window whose reconciler is only fuzz-covered in an optional slice must be closed before Slice 3 rotation is enabled. Gate Slice 3 on: (a) the dir-fsync fix, (b) promoting the Slice-5 crash-fuzz harness to mandatory. With those, ship the retain-all scope with confidence; do NOT extend it toward prune without re-opening the C1/A1 analysis.\"",
            "one_change_that_would_improve_it": "Make the cutover CREATE a fresh empty active spine.jsonl and dir-fsync it as the final step of the locked O(1) commit, instead of leaving the file ABSENT ('absent == empty active'). This single change closes the invariant-8 dir-fsync gap that rotation reintroduces once per seal, removes the absent-file special case from append/next_seq/_read_last_entry (the active segment then always exists, simplifying the genesis-vs-empty fork trap to a plain empty-file case the boundary-seed already handles), and gives every store.path.stat() consumer a real file to stat during the seal window — eliminating a whole class of ENOENT edge cases for the cost of one extra O(1) create+dir-fsync inside a critical section that is already dir-fsyncing the manifest.\""
          },
          {
            "design_name": "SEGMENT-ROTATION (retain-all, API-preserving)",

This sidesteps the A1 asymmetric-failure trap entirely (SpineTailer swallows a bad head silently, verify_checkpoint crashes with no try/except) — that trap only fires for a v2 snapshot-bearing head, which belongs to the DEFERRED hard-prune tier, not this iteration.",
          "spinestore_api_changes": "All public signatures stay identical; semantics transparently span the manifest segment set in seq order. Full scan (since_seq<0) = concat of every segment in order = the exact record list produced today. (2) get(seq): O(1) manifest range-lookup (seq_lo<=seq<=seq_hi) picks the one owning segment, then byte-offset index (active/uncompressed sealed) or a single-segment stream-scan (gzipped) — bounded to one segment, never the whole spine; returns the identical record or None. (3) tail(n): now walks segments backward from the active one across the seam until n records are collected (FIXES B3 — today store.py:244-268 seeks only self.path, so tail(2048) silently shrinks right after a rotation). (4) next_seq / _read_last_entry (store.py:270-272,305-312): resolve the tip from the ACTIVE segment (the unsealed one, invariant 13); if the active segment is freshly-rotated and empty, seed seq/prev_hash from the prior segment's manifest boundary (seq_hi, boundary_hash) so append_entry links across the seam. (5) append (store.py:141-194): content shape, cert_digest, entry_hash derivation, fsync-before-ack all BYTE-IDENTICAL; only the framing changes — it flocks the path-stable lockfile (not the segment fd), resolves the active segment under that lock, runs _last_valid_boundary truncate-before-append on the ACTIVE segment only (invariant 7), derives the tip from the active segment (or the seam boundary when empty), and after the write may trigger rotation if over threshold. (6) NEW read-only helper change_token() -> (manifest_generation, active_dev_ino, active_size) — the unified epoch token (below). BYTE-IDENTICAL and untouched: the JSONL record format, verify()/verify_chain, entries(), count(), verify_record, checkpoint()/classify_head/verify_checkpoint, the head, and all SpineTailer logic (it consumes iter_records + entries()).",
          "storage_layout": "~/.sigil/spine/ gains: spine.lock (NEW, zero-byte, fixed path, never replaced/unlinked — the inode-stable flock target, invariant 14); manifest.json (NEW, the sole authoritative ordered segment set, swapped via checkpoint._atomic_write_text); segments/ dir holding seg-00000.jsonl[.gz] ... seg-NNNNN.jsonl (the highest-numbered, unsealed one is the ACTIVE append target, always uncompressed); head.json UNCHANGED (single signed commit point); keys/ UNCHANGED; archive/ (NEW, empty until the optional Slice-4 cold-move). The current single 93/97 MB spine.jsonl becomes segment 0 WITHOUT a rewrite: a one-time migration os.rename(spine.jsonl -> segments/seg-00000.jsonl) (atomic on the same filesystem — the spine dir) then writes a single-segment manifest naming seg-00000 as the active segment. No 97 MB copy. Back-compat: if manifest.json is absent (pre-migration or a rolled-back state), the store treats a bare spine.jsonl as an implicit single active segment 0, so an un-migrated install still reads. config.py gains SEGMENTS_DIR, MANIFEST_PATH, LOCKFILE_PATH, ARCHIVE_DIR constants (the natural home; config.py itself does no I/O). store.path becomes \"the resolved active segment path\" (dynamic), so the two raw-stat consumers that read store.path.stat() must move to change_token() (see index_changes/A4).",
          "manifest_and_lockfile": "MANIFEST (manifest.json), a JSON object: {schema_version:1, generation:<int monotonic epoch, ++ on every cutover — this is the index/cache invalidation token>, segments:[ {id:<int>, file:\"segments/seg-00001.jsonl.gz\", compression:\"gzip\"|\"none\", seq_lo:<int>, seq_hi:<int|null>, count:<int|null>, first_prev_hash:<hex — must equal the previous segment's boundary_hash; for id 0 it is _GENESIS_PREV>, boundary_hash:<hex = entry_hash of seq_hi, null while active>, sealed:<bool>} ... ]}. The single active segment has seq_hi/count/boundary_hash = null and sealed=false. SIGNED? No — for retain-all the manifest is an availability/ordering POINTER, not a trust root: the signed head still anchors the whole reassembled chain (entry_count absolute) and verify_chain independently re-derives every seam linkage from the actual records, so a malicious manifest reorder/drop is caught by verify() failing (a dropped TAIL segment is caught by classify_head's n<signed_n). Manifest order is the CANDIDATE set; the CHAIN proves it (matches \"order must come from the chain, not filenames\"). A reserved optional manifest_sig field stays null now and becomes owner-signed only in the deferred hard-prune tier (where the manifest attests pruned seq ranges and thus becomes trust-bearing). LOCKFILE (spine.lock): a fixed-path, never-replaced, never-unlinked zero-byte file. PROTOCOL — both append() and rotation, in order: (a) acquire the process-wide re-entrant spine_lock RLock now keyed on the LOCKFILE path (store.py:37-47, key change), (b) open spine.lock and fcntl.flock(fd, LOCK_EX) on ITS stable inode, (c) ONLY THEN read manifest -> resolve active segment -> re-validate generation -> do work, (d) release on close. Because the lockfile inode never moves, flock is sound across os.replace of segments and the manifest (fixes invariant 14 / D3: today's flock on the target file binds to an inode os.replace swaps away, so an appender holding an old a+b fd would write to the unlinked old inode and lose an ack'd record).",
          "cutover_algorithm": "Rotation = seal the active segment k and open segment k+1; gzip is the ONLY heavy step and is done LOCK-FREE (D1). PHASE 1 SEAL-SWAP (bounded locked critical section — flock spine.lock): run _last_valid_boundary on seg-k and truncate any torn tail (A2 seal-time clean); read tip -> seal_seq_hi, boundary_hash=tip.entry_hash; create empty seg-(k+1).jsonl, fsync its fd + fsync segments/ dir; write a new manifest marking seg-k sealed (compression still \"none\", seq_hi/count/boundary_hash filled) and adding seg-(k+1) active (first_prev_hash=boundary_hash); _atomic_write_text(manifest) (temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync, checkpoint.py:33-60); generation++; release lock. This section is tiny: a seek-from-end truncate check + one create+fsync + one manifest atomic-write. Appends now land in seg-(k+1) with NO lock contention. PHASE 2 COMPRESS (LOCK-FREE, no spine.lock held): gzip seg-k.jsonl -> seg-k.jsonl.gz.tmp, fsync it. seg-k is now immutable (below the sealed tip) so this needs no lock — a kill-switch PANIC append to seg-(k+1) is never blocked (D1). PHASE 3 GZ-SWAP (bounded locked section): os.replace(seg-k.jsonl.gz.tmp -> seg-k.jsonl.gz), fsync dir; write a new manifest updating seg-k file->.gz, compression->gzip; _atomic_write_text(manifest); generation++; release. PHASE 4 READER-GRACE + UNLINK (D2): defer unlink of the now-superseded seg-k.jsonl behind a reader grace (readers snapshot ALL segment fds immediately after reading the manifest, so an in-flight verify()/iter_records holds the old inode to completion; unlink only files unreferenced by the current manifest AND older than a fixed grace, e.g. 60 s, or via a refcount), then unlink + dir-fsync. ORDERING across {segments, manifest, head}: the HEAD IS NOT TOUCHED by rotation (retain-all keeps the same records, tip seq, and head_hash), so there is NO head/manifest crash-consistency coupling — crash scenario 3 (segment vs head disagreement) cannot arise. The manifest is the sole linearization point; the head stays a separate, untouched commit. CRASH SAFETY: crash before Phase-1 replace = pre-rotation state, all records present; crash after Phase-1 = seg-k sealed-uncompressed + seg-(k+1) active, fully readable, .gz.tmp is orphan garbage; crash after Phase-3 = manifest points at .gz, the uncompressed seg-k is an orphan (readers use the manifest, never a glob, so no double-count); crash during Phase-4 = orphan file only. Startup sweep unlinks any segments/ file not referenced by the current manifest after confirming the referenced set is chain-valid at every seam. entries()/count() derive the set solely from the manifest, never a directory glob.",
          "index_changes": "Make the seq->offset index segment-aware and replace size-only detection with an epoch/identity token (invariant 9). (1) _offsets becomes seq -> (segment_id, byte_offset) — for gzipped segments the offset is into the DECOMPRESSED stream. Sealed segments are immutable, so their per-segment index is computed once and cached for the process lifetime; the manifest seq_lo/seq_hi ranges give an O(1) seq->segment map with NO scan, so get(seq) resolves the owning segment for free and then offset-seeks (active/uncompressed) or single-segment stream-scans (gz, bounded to ~12k records). (2) EPOCH/IDENTITY invalidation replaces store.py:335,345-347 size-only logic: change detection keys on change_token() = (manifest_generation, active_segment_dev_ino, active_segment_size). generation change -> the active segment rotated -> refresh the segment set from the manifest (sealed indices stay valid, they are immutable) and rebuild the active-segment index; active_size GREW at same generation -> extend the active index (append-only within the active segment, offsets never move); active_size SHRANK at same generation -> genuine truncation attack on the active file -> rebuild + surface (unchanged detection, now correctly scoped to the active segment rather than misfiring on rotation, which was risk #7/scenario-8). (3) A4 UNIFIED across all three st_size sites: expose change_token() and swap store._ensure_index (store.py:335), killswitch.py:57 (Path(store.path).stat().st_size verdict-cache key), and gesture/session.py:121 (kill-switch growth detector) ALL onto the SAME token. Today a same-size rotation would serve a STALE kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction (dangerous); one shared (generation, dev_ino, size) token closes all three at once — and it is a correctness fix regardless of rotation because size-only was already unsound across os.replace.",
          "rotation_trigger": "Seal the active segment when it reaches count >= 12,000 records OR size >= 32 MiB, whichever first (12k records at the current ~2.2 KB/record density is ~26 MB, so the two bounds are comparable). DEFAULT POLICY: evaluate the trigger at checkpoint()/`sigil sign` time (checkpoint.py:79), i.e. OFF the hot append path and coupled to the owner's daily anchor — a natural safe point where the just-signed head anchors the exact chain and, if the deferred tier is ever enabled, the owner key is already in hand. Plus a manual `sigil spine rotate` command. An opportunistic post-append trigger fires only when the active segment runs FAR over threshold (e.g. >= 2x) so a burst between checkpoints cannot grow one segment unbounded. gzip-on-seal defaults ON; cold-archive (Slice 4) defaults OFF.",
          "consumer_transparency": "Because every read primitive spans segments to present ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain and NO record is removed, 32 of 34 consumers work UNCHANGED. The 23 full-scanners keep total history: graph/rebuild.py full replay, revise.py archivist supersession + promotion-key ledgers, killswitch _scan_engaged latch, promotion.is_promoted, budget.spent, actor_scope.creation_allowed cap-count, mesh/registry authorized_devices + capability_map, envelope.device_nonce_highwater replay watermark, recall, audit, dashboard, consolidate/queries.py (B1) threads/commitments/contradictions — all get a complete iter_records() across all segments, so no monotonic reconstruction regresses and no security watermark drops. The 9 old-seq resolvers stay correct because every acked seq still resolves: get(old_seq) via manifest range -> owning segment -> present record (actor_gate, egress, operator, actor, bridge/server /api/record, ui/server /api/record, mcp episodic_range, consolidate/gate get(), bridge notifier cursor); iter_records(since_seq=old) seeks into the right segment and spans forward. EXACTLY TWO files need edits, both mechanical correctness fixes: (a) killswitch.py:55-57 and gesture/session.py:121 swap raw store.path.stat().st_size for store.change_token() (the A4 unified token) — required because store.path now names a dynamic active segment; (b) cli.py --reset (SPINE_PATH.unlink at cli.py:22) generalizes to clear the whole segments/ dir + manifest.json + spine.lock, else --reset leaves stale rotated segments. tail()-based dedup in bridge/daemon.py is fixed transparently by the segment-spanning tail() (B3). No consumer makes a seq==line or 0-start assumption, so nothing else changes.",
          "c1_handling": "C1 DOES NOT ARISE in this design. C1 is \"no durable anti-rollback floor lives outside the compactable data,\" which only bites once records are DELETED so that a short-spine + low-count-head pair becomes replayable as a legitimate compaction baseline. In retain-all rotation, entry_count stays ABSOLUTE (checkpoint sign_head over entries() still counts every record, chain.py:60) and the on-disk reassembled chain is always >= the signed count, so classify_head's `n < signed_n` truncation alarm (checkpoint.py:93) remains a LIVE guard — a replayed old (short, low-count) head is still caught because the current reassembled chain is longer. The durable anti-rollback floor therefore stays exactly where it lives today: the persisted signed head's entry_count/last_seq/head_hash vs the full reassembled chain (plus SpineTailer's in-memory high-water, tail.py:60,90). Rotation weakens none of it. C1 becomes decisive ONLY at the DEFERRED hard-prune tier, where shrinking is legitimate and a fresh/cold verifier loses the \"longer chain\" discriminator — and closing it there needs a floor OUTSIDE the compactable data (per-verifier local last-accepted last_seq and/or authenticated head delivery a mirror cannot roll back). That is the exact operator threat-model decision this design refuses to make autonomously.",
          "un_prunable_state_handling": "Untouched — retain-all means every monotonic state-bearer class is reconstructed intact by full scans that now simply span all segments, so NO folding into a signed snapshot is needed. Each of the ~9 classes stays exact: per-device nonce replay high-water (envelope.py device_nonce_highwater), newest warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py warden_anchor_get), account.create / web.actor.step creation-cap counts (actor_scope.creation_allowed, actor.py), latest device authorize/revoke per key (mesh/registry.authorized_devices — the authorization ledger the whole bridge trusts), kill-switch engage/release latch (killswitch _scan_engaged), standing promotion grants (promotion.is_promoted), unresolved queued approvals (approvals.pending), open archivist supersession chain + promotion-key idempotency ledger (revise.py), and open threads/commitments/contradictions + their source_seq/verified_seq citations (consolidate/queries.py, B1). Because the records that carry them are all still present and the scans reach every segment, every reconstruction floor is preserved with zero new schema. State-folding into a signed snapshot payload is a DEFERRED hard-prune requirement (question 4), not part of what ships.",
          "seal_time_and_torn_tail": "A sealed segment is guaranteed clean because the SEAL step (Phase 1 of cutover, under the bounded lock) runs _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83-121) on the segment and truncates any torn tail BEFORE marking it sealed and BEFORE gzipping it (A2) — the append-path truncate (store.py:168-171) can never reach a sealed file since it is no longer the active target, so without this a crash on the rotation-triggering record would leave a permanent false verify() gap at the seam (iter_records skips a torn line, store.py:209-216, and verify_chain turns a skip into a fatal seq gap, chain.py:44). Since seal fires at checkpoint (a clean point after a successful fsync'd append), a torn tail is unlikely, but we run the truncate unconditionally (cheap seek-from-end). seq/entry_hash are carried across the seam via the manifest: the sealed segment records boundary_hash = tip.entry_hash and seq_hi = tip.seq; the next segment's first_prev_hash = boundary_hash, and the first record appended there derives its prev_hash from that boundary (append_entry seeded from the manifest seam when the active segment is empty), so verify_chain's strict prev_hash linkage (chain.py:40) and seq contiguity (chain.py:44) hold unbroken across every segment boundary. gzip only ever compresses an already-truncated-clean sealed segment, so a .gz never contains a torn tail.",
          "slice_plan": [
            "Slice 0 — Manifest + lockfile scaffolding, no behavior change. Add SEGMENTS_DIR/MANIFEST_PATH/LOCKFILE_PATH consts; one-time migration os.rename(spine.jsonl -> segments/seg-00000.jsonl) + write a single-segment active manifest (no 97MB copy); store resolves the active segment from the manifest (bare-spine.jsonl back-compat if no manifest); append flocks the path-stable spine.lock instead of the segment fd. Still ONE segment, no rotation — reads are byte-identical, full test suite green. Proves the plumbing with zero rotation risk.",
            "Slice 1 — Segment-spanning read API. iter_records/get/tail/count/entries/next_seq span an ORDERED multi-segment set from the manifest (still one real segment; exercised by tests that hand-craft a 2-segment manifest). Segment-aware index (seq->(segment,offset)), manifest-range lookup for get(), internal change_token() replacing size-only detection. Pure read-path, independently reviewable.",
            "Slice 2 — A4 unified change-token + reset. Swap killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 raw st_size for store.change_token(); generalize cli.py --reset to clear segments/ + manifest.json + spine.lock. Small mechanical consumer-side correctness fixes, independently testable.",
            "Slice 3 — Rotation/seal cutover (the crash-safety-critical slice). Seal-swap (bounded lock) + lock-free gzip + gz-swap + reader-grace unlink + startup orphan sweep + seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2). Trigger at checkpoint() over threshold + manual `sigil spine rotate`. Gets the heaviest review and a crash-injection matrix over the 10 durability scenarios + a seam-verify test. After this the spine actually rotates and gzips; disk and per-file size bounded.",
            "Slice 4 (optional, still retain-all) — Cold-archive MOVE of old sealed .gz segments into archive/ referenced by the manifest (cheaper/slower storage), fully readable, chain-complete, zero deletion.",
            "DEFERRED TIER (NOT autonomous — surface ONE explicit operator decision): hard-prune record deletion — signed SnapshotAnchor, v2 SignedChainHead schema (A1 migration + flag-day), state-folding of all ~9 monotonic bearers, C1 durable external anti-rollback floor, C2 Merkle commitment over the pruned prefix, and the honest 'compacted below seq N (see snapshot X)' resolve contract (question 5). Operator prompt: 'Retain-all rotation + gzip + cold-archive is the autonomous ceiling; do you accept trading total-recall tamper-evidence of old records for disk reclamation beyond gzip? If yes we build the signed-snapshot tier.'"
          ],
          "risks": [
            "Slice 3 cutover is the crux: a bug in seal-swap/manifest ordering or the seam boundary could break prev_hash linkage or seq contiguity at a segment boundary -> false verify() gap across the whole spine. Mitigate with the 10-scenario crash-injection matrix plus a dedicated seam-verify test (reassemble a 3-segment chain and assert verify()==today).",
            "gzip is not block-seekable, so get() on a deep-old gzipped segment is O(segment) stream-decompress, not O(1). Bounded to ~12k records/segment and only the 9 rare old-seq resolvers hit it, but a pathological repeated-deep-get consumer pays each time. Mitigate: LRU-cache the decompressed segment or persist a per-sealed-segment .idx sidecar (seq->decompressed-offset); or adopt bgzip/per-record-member framing for true random access (flag as an efficiency option, not required for slice 3).",
            "D2 reader-grace correctness: a long verify()/iter_records(-1) scan must not be ENOENT'd mid-read when rotation unlinks a superseded segment. Mitigate by snapshotting ALL segment fds right after reading the manifest and deferring unlink behind a fixed grace + generation check (never unlink a manifest-referenced file).",
            "A3 stale cache in long-lived store instances (gesture daemon, agent runner): a rotation done by another process must invalidate a cached next_seq/_last and the offset index. change_token() forces active-segment re-resolution on generation change; residual risk if any consumer caches next_seq without consulting the token — audit the ~8 next_seq readers.",
            "Migration rename (Slice 0) is atomic only within one filesystem; if segments/ ever lands on a different mount the rename degrades to a 97MB copy. Keep segments/ under the spine dir (it is). Also guard the migration itself under the lockfile so no append races the rename.",
            "Every one of the ~15 store.append call sites inherits the path-stable-lockfile change (invariant 14/D3); getting the lock key wrong (still keying on the segment path) silently reopens the cross-process fork window. This is the single highest-risk durability change — state it as a precondition of Slice 0, not a later mitigation."
          ],
          "invariants_at_risk": [
            "invariant 12 (read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain) — MOST stressed: the multi-segment reassembly must be exactly seq-ordered with no gap/dup at any seam or every full-scan consumer and verify() see a false break. Residual risk: an off-by-one at a boundary.",
            "invariant 14 + D3 (path-stable lockfile / flock binds to inode not path) — all cross-process append correctness now rests on spine.lock being flocked before resolving the active segment; residual risk if any writer bypasses the lockfile or keys the RLock on the old segment path.",
            "invariant 7 + A2 (torn-tail truncate on ACTIVE only / seal-time truncate on the sealed segment) — a sealed segment that ends torn is a permanent false verify() gap the append path can never repair; residual risk if seal-time truncate is skipped or races the last append.",
            "invariant 9 + A4 (index epoch/identity, unified across store/killswitch/session) — size-only detection is unsound across os.replace and lives in three places; residual risk if any of the three is missed or a same-size rotation slips a stale kill-switch verdict through.",
            "D1 (no append lock across the O(file) heavy copy) — gzip MUST stay lock-free so a kill-switch PANIC append is never blocked; residual risk if the seal and compress steps are collapsed into one long locked section.",
            "D2 (live-reader vs unlink race across the manifest indirection) — residual ENOENT risk if readers glob or re-open segments lazily instead of snapshotting fds after the manifest read.",
            "NOTE — deliberately NOT stressed (the whole point of phasing): C1 (durable external floor), C2 (Merkle prefix commitment), A1 (head-schema migration), invariants 4/15/16 state-folding — retain-all keeps entry_count absolute, the head untouched, and every state-bearer intact, so these are carried by the existing code unchanged and are deferred to the operator-gated hard-prune tier."
          ]
        },
        "verdicts": [
          {
            "design_name": "Retain-all segment rotation with gzip-on-seal (phased; hard-prune deferred to an explicit operator decision)",
            "invariant_preservation": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "I could not find a single invariant this design SILENTLY breaks, and every stressed one is explicitly named with its residual risk. Genesis-rooting (inv 1): seg-00000 IS the original spine.jsonl via os.rename, so record 0 keeps prev_hash=_GENESIS_PREV and verify_chain (chain.py:38) still seeds prev=_GENESIS_PREV over the reassembled stream. Seq-contiguity + immutability (inv 2/3): os.rename renumbers nothing; appends continue from the seam seq_hi+1; chain.py:44 checks seq[i]==seq[i-1]+1 across the concatenation. Anti-truncation gate (inv 4): retain-all keeps entry_count ABSOLUTE, so classify_head's `n < signed_n` (checkpoint.py:93) stays a LIVE guard over the full reassembled chain. Monotonic last_seq (inv 5): tip seq never moves. Torn-tail (inv 7/A2): the design runs _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83) at SEAL time on the segment before sealing+gzip, closing the exact permanent-false-gap hole a sealed torn tail would otherwise leave (the append-path truncate at store.py:168-171 can never reach a sealed file). fsync (inv 8): reuses checkpoint._atomic_write_text (checkpoint.py:33-60) for the manifest and fsyncs new-segment+dir. Index epoch (inv 9): change_token=(generation,dev_ino,size) replaces the unsound size-only logic at store.py:335,345-347 and is segment-aware. Durable tip / next_seq (inv 13): correctly identifies that _read_last_entry (store.py:305) and next_seq (store.py:270) on an EMPTY freshly-rotated active segment would otherwise call build_chain->seq 0/genesis (store.py:174) and re-fork the chain, and mandates seeding from the seam boundary instead. Path-stable lockfile (inv 14): correctly re-keys the RLock (store.py:37-47) and flock onto a fixed spine.lock inode, since self.path is now a DYNAMIC active-segment path and os.replace of segments would otherwise swap the flock inode out. Monotonic-state-by-full-scan (inv 15) and referential integrity (inv 16): preserved trivially because NO record is removed and full scans span segments. Schema-freeze (inv 17/18): ChainEntry/SignedChainHead (reuse/models.py:47-64, both extra=forbid) are untouched, head stays the single commit point. The deferred invariants (C1, C2, A1 head migration, inv 4/15/16 state-folding) are genuinely N/A for retain-all because the record SET is preserved — they only bind once records are deleted, which the design explicitly refuses to do autonomously. The one wrinkle: the append seam seeds a new acked record's prev_hash/seq from the UNSIGNED manifest boundary field rather than the prior segment's actual last record, so an unsigned datum becomes load-bearing for the append hash-chain (detectable at next verify, but it under-realizes the design's own 'manifest is not a trust root' claim). That is a hardening note, not a design-level invariant break."
            },
            "consumer_transparency": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "All public SpineStore signatures stay identical and semantics span the manifest segment set in seq order, so the read API still presents ONE contiguous genesis-anchored chain (inv 12). 32/34 consumers are untouched: the 23 full-scanners (graph/rebuild, revise ledgers, killswitch latch, promotion, budget, actor_scope cap, mesh authorized_devices, envelope nonce high-water, consolidate/queries B1) each get a complete iter_records() across every segment, so no monotonic reconstruction (inv 15) regresses and no security watermark drops; the 9 old-seq resolvers still resolve because every acked seq still exists and get(seq) picks the owning segment by manifest range then re-checks rec.seq==seq (so a lying manifest range yields None+fallback, never a wrong record). The two edits are not regressions but REQUIRED correctness fixes that also improve soundness: killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 must move off raw store.path.stat().st_size (which now names a dynamic active segment and was already unsound across os.replace, A4) onto the shared change_token; cli.py:22 --reset must clear segments/+manifest+lock. tail() (store.py:244) is FIXED to span the seam (B3 — today it seeks only self.path and silently shrinks after a rotation). No consumer assumes seq==line or a 0-start offset, so nothing else moves."
            },
            "durability_crash_safety": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "The cutover is a proper multi-file atomic commit with the manifest as the sole linearization point, swapped by the already-proven temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync path (checkpoint.py:33-60). The heavy gzip is LOCK-FREE (D1): the bounded seal-swap critical section is a seek-from-end truncate + one create+fsync + one atomic manifest write, so a kill-switch PANIC append to the new active segment is never blocked. The path-stable spine.lock (inv 14/D3) is flocked on a never-replaced inode BEFORE resolving the active segment, closing the cross-process fork window that os.replace of segments would otherwise open. Reader-grace + generation-checked deferred unlink (D2) stops an in-flight verify()/iter_records(-1) from being ENOENT'd mid-scan; entries()/count() derive the set from the manifest, never a glob, so the unlink window can't double-count. Seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2) guarantees a sealed/gzipped segment is never permanently torn. Crucially, retain-all means rotation changes only physical layout, never the record SET, so the head/manifest crash-consistency COUPLING that would otherwise create a false-TAMPERING window simply cannot arise — the head stays a separate untouched commit. Every enumerated crash window leaves all records present and readable. Residual (non-fatal, detectable): the startup orphan sweep is a WRITE that, against a rolled-back/tampered manifest, could unlink a real segment file, turning recoverable-but-detectable into unrecoverable-but-detectable — tamper-EVIDENCE survives (count/genesis guards don't depend on the orphan file), but it deserves to run under the lockfile with a chain-valid precondition, which the design specifies."
            },
            "tamper_evidence_integrity": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "The keyed tamper model is untouched and now spans segments faithfully: the owner-signed head remains the sole authority (inv 11/18), verify()/verify_chain/classify_head/verify_checkpoint are byte-identical and re-derive every seam linkage from the ACTUAL records, never from the manifest. Critically for the purist question — does this make a prune indistinguishable from a rollback? — it does NOT, because it never produces a legitimately-short chain: a manifest that drops the FRONT segment is caught by genesis-rooting (seg-1's first record prev_hash != _GENESIS_PREV -> chain.py:40 break); an INTERIOR drop by seq-gap (chain.py:44); a REORDER by prev_hash linkage; a truncation to below the anchor by classify_head n<signed_n (checkpoint.py:93); a rewrite by the Ed25519 head signature. An unsigned manifest is therefore defensible here: it is a CANDIDATE ordering the chain proves, matching 'order must come from the chain, not filenames'. I dock one point for two unsigned-manifest load-bearing wrinkles the design leaves latent: (1) the append seam seeds a new ACKED record's prev_hash/seq from the unsigned manifest boundary_hash/seq_hi rather than the prior segment's real tail, so a manifest corruption can steer an acked record's linkage (detectable at next verify, but a trust surface the design claims not to have); (2) the manifest's convenience count/seq_hi fields are a trap if any future optimization ever shortcuts count()/entry_count to them while manifest_sig is null. Neither breaks the owner-signed evidence, so this stays a 4, not lower. The deferred tier correctly reserves manifest_sig + C1 external floor + C2 Merkle-prefix commitment for the moment the manifest actually becomes trust-bearing (i.e., attests pruned ranges)."
            },
            "implementability_blast_radius": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "Well-sliced: Slice 0 (manifest+lockfile scaffolding, cheap os.rename migration, no 97MB copy, bare-spine back-compat), Slice 1 (segment-spanning read API, independently reviewable), Slice 2 (mechanical A4 consumer fixes), Slice 3 (the crash-safety-critical cutover, flagged for the heaviest review + a 10-scenario crash-injection matrix + seam-verify test), Slice 4 optional cold-archive. Public signatures stay identical so 32/34 consumers need no touch, and the migration is O(rename) not O(copy). But the blast radius is real and high-consequence: a single off-by-one at ANY seam makes the whole spine's verify() read a false break (inv 12 is the most-stressed surface); the path-stable-lockfile change touches ~15 append call sites and the RLock re-key (store.py:37-47) is subtle — key it on the old segment path and you silently reopen the cross-process fork window; and the empty-active-segment seam seeding must be gotten right in BOTH append and _read_last_entry/__init__ (store.py:138,305) or the first record after every rotation restarts at seq 0/genesis. Intricate but bounded — exactly the hard-but-bounded storage-mechanics engineering the brief predicts, hence 4 not 5."

The purist test 'does it make a prune indistinguishable from a rollback?' is passed cleanly: it never produces a short chain, and any manifest-level fake-prune is caught by genesis-rooting + the n<signed_n count gate. The hard-prune tier (actual record deletion, where C1's durable external floor, C2's Merkle-prefix commitment, the A1 breaking head migration, and folding all ~9 monotonic state-bearers become mandatory) is correctly gated behind an explicit operator threat-model decision, not taken autonomously. Ship retain-all; do NOT let this design's momentum carry into hard-prune without that operator sign-off.",
            "one_change_that_would_improve_it": "Make the manifest fully NON-load-bearing for correctness and tamper, realizing the design's own 'availability pointer, not a trust root' claim that the append seam currently violates: derive the seam seed (the prev_hash/seq for the first record of a fresh active segment, and _read_last_entry when the active segment is empty) from the prior SEALED segment's ACTUAL last record via a cheap seek-from-end read, cross-checked against — never trusting — the manifest's boundary_hash/seq_hi, so an unsigned/corrupted manifest field can never inject a bad prev_hash into an acked append. Pair it with a standing invariant that entry_count, count(), and every tamper decision re-derive from records and NEVER read the manifest's convenience count/seq fields while manifest_sig is null. This shrinks the manifest's trust surface for retain-all to exactly zero and pre-closes the trap for the deferred signed-manifest tier.\""

Torn-tail (7): _last_valid_boundary (store.py:83-121) runs at SEAL time under the bounded lock BEFORE gzip, and because seal and append share spine.lock the truncate cannot race a concurrent append — race-free by construction. fsync (8): append fsync (store.py:183) untouched; manifest uses _atomic_write_text (checkpoint.py:33-60) verbatim.             "consumer_transparency": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "32/34 consumers unchanged because every read primitive is made segment-spanning while preserving public signatures: iter_records concatenates manifest segments in seq order = today's exact list for since_seq<0; get(seq) does an O(1) manifest range-lookup then offset-seek or single-segment gz scan; tail(n) now walks backward across the seam, FIXING the latent B3 bug where today's store.py:244-268 seeks only self.path and would silently shrink after a rotation. entries()/count()/verify()/verify_record/checkpoint/SpineTailer are byte-identical because they consume iter_records+entries(). Only two mechanical edits (killswitch.py:57 and gesture/session.py:121 raw st_size -> change_token; cli.py --reset generalized), both genuine correctness fixes forced by store.path becoming a dynamic active-segment path. The 9 old-seq resolvers stay correct because every acked seq still resolves (retain-all). No consumer makes a seq==line or 0-start assumption."

            "durability_crash_safety": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "The STEADY-STATE cutover is genuinely crash-safe and I could not construct a steady-state interleaving that loses or double-counts an acked record given the stated protocol. Ordering is correct: seg-(k+1) is created+fsync'd+dir-fsync'd BEFORE the manifest names it, so no crash yields a dangling manifest reference; the manifest swaps via atomic os.replace so readers see old-or-new, never torn; entries() derives the set from the manifest and NEVER globs, so the Phase-3->4 window where both seg-k.jsonl and seg-k.jsonl.gz exist cannot double-count; the startup sweep unlinks only manifest-unreferenced files after a seam chain-valid check. D1 holds IF Phase 2 gzip truly runs lock-free (the file is immutable post-seal, so a kill-switch PANIC append to seg-(k+1) is never blocked) — but this REQUIRES the opportunistic post-append trigger to NOT run Phase 2 inline under the held append lock, and requires care against BSD-flock same-process re-entrancy if rotation opens a second spine.lock fd while an append holds the first (RLock is re-entrant, flock is per-fd and would deadlock). D2 rests on snapshotting ALL segment fds right after the manifest read (POSIX keeps an unlinked inode alive for the holder), backed by a 60s grace for the manifest-read->open gap; a full verify() that runs longer than the grace is still safe via the held fds, at the cost of holding one fd per segment (fd-pressure as segment count grows). D3 is soundly closed: the active append target is never os.replace'd (only the manifest and .gz are), on top of the inode-stable spine.lock. The head/manifest decoupling (crash-scenario-3 elimination) is real and verified. Docked one point for the ONE concrete acked-record-loss path the design under-specifies — the Slice-0 migration vs a concurrent old-code writer (see fatal_flaws) — plus the two D1 fidelity conditions (inline-trigger, flock-nesting) that must be nailed in Slice 3."
            },
            "tamper_evidence_integrity": {
              "score": 5,
              "rationale": "Retain-all keeps entry_count ABSOLUTE, so classify_head's `n < signed_n` truncation gate (checkpoint.py:93) stays a live guard — a replayed short/low-count head is still caught because the reassembled chain is longer. The head bytes are byte-identical and stay valid over the reassembled chain (head_hash==tip entry_hash, last_seq==tip seq, entry_count==total), so old verifiers pass with no code change and the A1 asymmetric-failure trap (SpineTailer swallows a bad head at tail.py:118 vs verify_checkpoint crashing with no try/except at checkpoint.py:104-109) is sidestepped entirely. The manifest is deliberately UNSIGNED and that is sound: a malicious reorder/drop/duplicate of segments produces a verify_chain seq-gap or prev_hash break (chain.py:41,44) or n<signed_n TAMPERING — the signed head + independent chain re-derivation prove order, matching 'order must come from the chain, not filenames.' C1 (external anti-rollback floor), C2 (Merkle prefix commitment), and A1 (head-schema migration) are honestly and correctly deferred to the operator-gated hard-prune tier because they only bite once records are deleted; nothing here weakens them."
            },
            "implementability_blast_radius": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "The slice plan is disciplined: Slice 0 (manifest+lockfile, rename-not-copy), Slice 1 (segment-spanning reads), Slice 2 (A4 token+reset), Slice 3 (the crash-critical cutover, isolated for a 10-scenario crash-injection matrix + a seam-verify test), Slice 4 (optional cold-archive), and an explicit deferred prune tier surfaced as ONE operator decision. But the changes land on the hottest, most safety-critical path: get the RLock key wrong (still keying on the segment path instead of the lockfile) and the cross-process fork window reopens; get the empty-active seam seed wrong and every rotation forks a fresh genesis (store.py:174 footgun); miss one of the three A4 st_size sites and a same-size rotation slips a stale kill-switch verdict through in the un-halting direction. Blast radius touches append durability, the kill-switch panic path, and the index simultaneously. The phasing contains it, and no 97MB copy is needed (in-place rename), but this is not a low-risk change — it is the highest-value/highest-risk kind."
            },
            "fatal_flaws": [
              "Slice-0 migration split-brain (the one concrete acked-record-loss path, deployment-gated): os.rename(spine.jsonl -> segments/seg-00000.jsonl) is guarded 'under the lockfile', but the lockfile is a Slice-0 novelty — old-code writers still flock the SEGMENT fd (store.py:160), not spine.lock, so they do not serialize against the migration. A surviving old-code daemon (bridge server / gesture daemon / agent runner) that appends after the rename reopens 'spine.jsonl' by path in append mode (store.py:157 self.path.open('a+b')), finds it gone, and CREATES a fresh empty file starting at seq=0/_GENESIS_PREV (via the store.py:174 build_chain fallback). Its acked records now live in a forked genesis file invisible to every manifest-based reader -> silent acked-record loss + a second seq=0. The stated mitigation is insufficient because it assumes all writers already honor spine.lock. Fix: two-step deploy — ship the lock-repointing (flock spine.lock before resolving the active segment) to ALL writers and fully restart them, and only THEN, once no live process still flocks the segment path, run the rename+manifest; plus a split-brain guard that REFUSES to auto-create a bare spine.jsonl once manifest.json exists. This is the only place the design AS SPECIFIED can lose an acked record; the steady-state cutover has no such path."
            ],
            "weighted_verdict": "Shippable safely for the RETAIN-ALL scope (Slices 0-3), NOT for prune. The steady-state cutover is crash-safe and D1/D2/D3-sound as specified: correct fsync-before-reference ordering, atomic manifest swap, manifest-not-glob set derivation, orphan sweep, an inode-stable lockfile that also never inode-swaps the active append target, and — decisively — the retain-all choice eliminates the head/manifest crash-consistency coupling (crash-scenario-3 cannot arise) and keeps entry_count absolute so every tamper gate stays live. I could not construct a steady-state interleaving that loses or double-counts an acked record or blocks a kill-switch panic, GIVEN the stated protocol is implemented faithfully. The residual risk is concentrated and correctly isolated in Slice 3 (empty-active seam seed vs the store.py:174 footgun; lock keyed on the lockfile not the segment; Phase-2 gzip strictly lock-free and not run inline under the append lock; the three A4 sites unified) and must be gated by the 10-scenario crash matrix + a 3-segment seam-verify test asserting verify()==today. The ONE acked-record-loss path in the design as written is the Slice-0 migration against a concurrent old-code writer — bounded to a one-time event and fixable with a two-step deploy. Hard-prune's C1/C2/A1/state-folding are honestly deferred to an explicit operator threat-model decision, which is the right autonomous ceiling.",
            "one_change_that_would_improve_it": "Make Slice 0 a two-step, split-brain-safe migration instead of a single guarded rename: (1) deploy ONLY the lock-repointing (every writer flocks the inode-stable spine.lock before resolving the active segment) to ALL spine-writing processes and fully restart them, so no live process still flocks the segment path; (2) ONLY THEN, under spine.lock, run os.rename(spine.jsonl -> seg-00000.jsonl) + write the single-segment manifest; and add a permanent split-brain guard that refuses to auto-create a bare spine.jsonl once manifest.json exists (surface instead of forking a new genesis). This closes the sole acked-record-loss path the design as specified still contains, and it hardens the exact seam (lock key + genesis fallback) whose mis-implementation is also the worst steady-state failure."
          },
          {
            "design_name": "Retain-all segment rotation with gzip-on-seal (phased; hard-prune deferred)",
            "invariant_preservation": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "Retain-all genuinely preserves the load-bearing invariants because it moves no record, renumbers no seq, and never touches the signed head. entry_count stays absolute (checkpoint.py:82 counts entries() = every record), the reassembled chain stays genesis-anchored (chain.py:38-41 seeds _GENESIS_PREV over the concatenated segments), and classify_head's n<signed_n truncation guard (checkpoint.py:93) stays a LIVE alarm because the reassembled chain is always >= the signed count. Invariant 12 (one contiguous genesis chain) is the most stressed and is handled by carrying seq_hi/boundary_hash across the seam so verify_chain's prev_hash linkage (chain.py:40) and seq contiguity (chain.py:44) hold unbroken; A2 seal-time _last_valid_boundary truncate (store.py:83-121) closes the invariant-7 sealed-torn-tail gap. Residual: (1) invariant 14/D3 — the lock-key move is correct in principle but the census misses envelope.py:130, which keys its replay-gate RLock on store.path; leaving it unchanged while store.path becomes dynamic reintroduces a cross-rotation nonce-TOCTOU window. (2) an off-by-one at any seam turns the whole spine into a false verify() break — real but test-coverable risk."

            "consumer_transparency": {

              "rationale": "CORRECTNESS transparency is strong and real: every read primitive spans the manifest segment set to present one seq-ordered genesis chain, so all 34 consumers keep working. get(old_seq) resolves via manifest range -> owning segment for the 9 old-seq resolvers; the 23 full-scanners (verified: rebuild.py:55, budget.py:30, promotion.py:48, actor_scope.py:49, mesh/registry.py:46+71, killswitch.py:75, envelope.py:92, revise.py:16, recall.py:35, approvals.py:62, bastion.py:253, dashboard.py:28, daemon.py:87, session.py:56+217, cli.py:283, mcp x2) get complete iter_records() so no monotonic reconstruction or security watermark drops. TWO real defects in the transparency claim, though: (1) the 'EXACTLY TWO files need edits' census is WRONG — envelope.py:130 `spine_lock(store.path)` is a third REQUIRED edit and it is the nonce-replay atomicity path (verify_envelope receipt), because store.path becomes dynamic and the append lock moves to LOCKFILE_PATH; the design's own invariant-14 intent implies this edit but never enumerates it. (2) 'read latency is bounded' is misleading for the full-scan MAJORITY: 23 full-scanners stay O(total records) AND now pay gzip decompression per scan, including check_anchor's per-poll store.entries() (tail.py:87) on the live-tail hot path driven by the UI/mobile tick. Only get()/old-seq resolution actually gets faster. Correctness intact, but the census undercounts and the latency framing oversells."

            "durability_crash_safety": {
              "score": 4,
              "rationale": "Crash-safety is well-reasoned: the manifest is the sole linearization point swapped by the existing _atomic_write_text (checkpoint.py:33-60, temp->fsync->os.replace->dir-fsync), and because retain-all NEVER touches the head there is no head/manifest crash-consistency coupling — this genuinely eliminates the hardest crash scenario (segment-vs-head disagreement). D1 is respected: gzip is the only O(file) step and runs lock-free in Phase 2, so a kill-switch PANIC append to seg-(k+1) is never blocked. D2 reader-grace (snapshot all fds after the manifest read, defer unlink) handles the live-reader/unlink race; entries()/count() derive the set from the manifest never a glob (avoids double-count in the unlink window). The path-stable spine.lock flocked before resolving the active segment (D3) is the right fix for os.replace swapping the inode out from under an flock-on-target. Residual: the crash matrix hinges on the manifest generation++ ordering and the startup orphan sweep confirming every seam is chain-valid before unlinking — correct but unforgiving; and the missed envelope.py lock-key edit means one cross-process serialization path is left on the wrong (dynamic) key, a latent durability/atomicity gap rather than a data-loss one."
            },
            "tamper_evidence_integrity": {

Leaving the manifest UNSIGNED is defensible and I verified it: a malicious manifest reorder or interior-segment drop becomes a prev_hash/seq break caught by verify_chain (chain.py:40-44); a tail-segment drop trips classify_head n<signed_n (checkpoint.py:93). So the manifest is a pure availability/ordering pointer and the CHAIN proves the order, matching 'order must come from the chain, not filenames.' Critically, C1 (durable external anti-rollback floor) and C2 (Merkle prefix commitment) are correctly identified as NON-arising under retain-all — they only bite once records are DELETED — and are honestly deferred to an explicit operator threat-model decision rather than hand-waved. The A1 closed-head-schema trap (SpineTailer swallows a bad head, verify_checkpoint crashes) is sidestepped entirely because no v2 head is introduced. The migration is os.rename(spine.jsonl -> segments/seg-00000.jsonl) with NO 97MB copy (atomic on the same fs), and the manifest swap reuses the proven _atomic_write_text. Slice 1 (read API) is exercisable via hand-crafted 2-segment manifests before any real rotation exists — clean review isolation. Slice 3 (cutover) correctly gets the heavy crash-injection matrix + seam-verify test. (2) gzip get() is O(segment) stream-decompress and full-scanners pay decompression per scan; the mitigation (LRU decompressed-segment cache / per-segment .idx sidecar) is deferred to an 'efficiency option,' so Slice 3 as-shipped carries a real perf regression on the check_anchor poll path. (3) A3 stale-cache in long-lived daemons (gesture, agent runner) needs an audit of the ~8 next_seq readers (mcp/server.py:85,120, rebuild.py:171, dashboard.py:19, query.py:117, steward.py:25, runner.py:18, pipeline.py:72, notifier.py:26) — flagged but not enumerated in the plan."

The design moves append's lock key to a fixed LOCKFILE_PATH AND redefines store.path as the dynamic active-segment path. If envelope.py is left unchanged (as the census implies), then across a rotation two concurrent replays of one captured envelope resolve store.path to DIFFERENT active-segment paths -> DIFFERENT RLocks -> they no longer serialize -> the replay-freshness TOCTOU (the exact bug the atomic block at envelope.py:127-133 exists to close) reopens at every rotation boundary. Fix is one line (spine_lock(LOCKFILE_PATH)) and the design's invariant-14 intent already implies it, but as written the stated slice plan ships the regression. Must be added to Slice 0's blast radius and covered by the same cross-process replay test."

The slice decomposition is real (0-2 ship a byte-identical one-segment system; rotation turns on only at 3), so it is independently reviewable and each slice leaves a working system. What holds it back from a clean pass: the consumer census is over-optimistic in two concrete ways — it misses the envelope.py replay-gate lock-key edit (security-relevant, must be in Slice 0), and it oversells 'read latency bounded' for the 23 full-scanners and the per-poll check_anchor scan, which get SLOWER (decompression) even as per-file size is bounded. Neither is architectural; both are fixable within the stated plan. This closes the one place the stated plan ships a real regression (the nonce-TOCTOU at rotation boundaries) and forces the lock-key change to be treated as the highest-risk item it actually is, rather than 'no behavior change' scaffolding.\""
